New `state` (I-4): `{connected_now (the stream task's last word, null before it said anything), last_update_age_ms_now, reconnects_total, resnapshots_total, messages_total, unavailable_total, down_since_ns, last_error, tier_a_now, tier_a_complete, store_slot_now (the newest slot of a stream update applied: the lag rule's store position)}`. `store.pending_groups_now` counts the CommitTracker's pending groups. `commit`: `{groups_delta, by_w_src_delta {tx-stream, shred, unknown}, complete_delta, superseded_delta, timeout_unknown_delta, timeout_quiescent_delta, fork_dropped_delta, late_member_delta, torn_suspect_delta, w_after_resolve_total (write sets registered after their group resolved), lag_ms (pct(interval), ms; complete and superseded groups with a first shred), lag_ms_by_w_src}`. `forks`: `{abandoned_slots_delta, dead_slots_delta, versions_dropped_delta, refetch_delta, fork_priced_delta, contested_now, root_now}`. `lag_slo`: `{window: "minute", p50_ms, p90_ms, ok, breaches_in_row, good_in_row, action}` (the last complete minute). **WP-04 review:** `health.shut` can be `resync` (the re-read after the stream's first connection or a reconnection has not landed: `Exec::wants_send` refuses, `reject_resync`); `state` gains `last_account_age_ms_now` (the newest stream account update's age; `last_update_age_ms_now` counts slot statuses too), `resync_now` (null, or `{why, since_ns, resume_slot, accounts, chunks, chunks_done, behind, failed}`), `unverified_now` (accounts not read again since the last connection: their pools are refused `resync`), `reads_inflight_now` and `reads_pending_now` (the engine's RPC reads: chunks being read, chunks waiting for their first read or a retry); `store` gains `kept_below_total` (older-slot versions kept below the current one for a fork rollback; `stale_total` counts every version not applied as current, these included); `forks` gains `late_version_delta` (writes of a slot already abandoned, refused). `accuracy`: `{scored_delta, exact_delta, pre_state_unknown_delta, by_venue {venue: {scored, exact}} (totals), queue_now, overflow_total, action: "log"}`. `alts`: `{cached_now, waiting_txs_now, fetching_now, pending_retries_now, gave_up_now, retries_delta, gave_up_delta, learned_delta}` (WP-02's `missing_now` is gone: a table is never missing for the run). `latency` adds A.8's v2 commit stages (`first_shred→update_recv`, `update_recv→commit`, `commit→engine`, `engine→priced`, `priced→decided`); the WP-00 state series stay; the v1 decoded-trigger series are gone (no decoded trigger is priced).

**As WP-05 writes it.** `health`: `leaders_ok` (the target slot's leader, tip + 1, is in the journaled schedule or the LeaderBook; null before a tip), `blockhash_age_slots_now` and `blockhash_src_now` (the blockhash the send checks would use, from the journaled WP-03b ring head and the state stream's block metas, `exec::SendView`: its age in slots behind the tip and its source; null until the ring has said anything), `blockhash_commitment` (`[send_policy] blockhash_commitment`), `jito_token_age_s_now` (the cached block-engine token's age; null without a bundle path, or once it is no longer usable). `coverage.leader_known_pct`: the share of the attempts since the last line (would-sends included) whose target leader's identity and kind were known; null without an attempt. `exec` (`Exec::status_v2`, present whenever an executor runs; null without one): `kind` (`live`, `canary`, `shadow`, `dry_run`), `stopped`, `counts_total` / `counts_delta`, `verdicts_delta`, `over_rate_total {crowded, other}` (copies the rate queue dropped; null without a send path), `decisions_total` / `_delta`, `refused_total`, `would_send_total` / `_delta` (the would-sends of shadow and dry runs and of a closed gate, I-7), `attempts_total` / `_delta` (sends and would-sends), `sent_total` / `_delta`, `landed_total` / `_delta`, `ok_total`, `in_flight_now` (sent, not final), `inflight_keys_now` (the in-flight lock's entries), `queue_now` and `lambda_now` (the rate queue's waiting sends and λ; null without a send path) and `net_calls_send_path_total` (network calls made inside the send path's scope, from the decision to the fire: 0 by design, F28). `money` is present whenever an executor runs (zeros for shadow and dry runs, which send nothing; the supervisor reads `failed_fees_total_lamports`): `{src: "bot", failed_fees_total_lamports, failed_fees_delta_lamports, failed_fees_cap_now_lamports, fees_landed_total_lamports, tips_paid_total_lamports, round_trip_total_lamports (the winning landings' on-chain round trips: the last leg's output from the route program's events minus the size; their net is it − tips − fees), round_trip_unknown_total (winning landings whose round trip is not in `round_trip_total_lamports`: found by the expiry check before their wallet record, or without the route program's events; while it is not 0 the net is partial), new_token_accounts_total}` until WP-07's ledger (`bot_profit_total_lamports` is gone: the balance changes it summed included rent). Each landing is counted once: a landing the expiry check found counts the base fee (what a one-signature bundle at CU price 0 pays; a failed one also against the failed-fee cap) until its wallet record replaces it (WP-05 review #3). `chainfetch` gains `wallet_unlanded_by_slot_delta` (of `wallet_unlanded_delta`, the signatures given up because the chain passed their attempt's target slot by 16 slots, §4.15).

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.status/2.1`; `Ledger::money_json`). `money` is the ledger's (`src` `ledger`; with no ledger, the bot's counters as in 2.0): `stop_basis` (`sol_wsol`), `equity_stop_now` (SOL + WSOL: the last good snapshot and the entries landed after it), `equity_liquid_now`, `native_sol_now`, `wsol_now`, `rent_recoverable_now`, `equity_total_now` (+ recoverable rent + the tokens' value), `token_value_now`, `tokens_now` {mint: amount}, `drop_1h_now`, `drop_24h_now` (the stop basis, campaign-wide), `drop_window_src` (`campaign`: the start came from an earlier segment; `segment`), `start` (§4.1's components), `floor_lamports`, `floor_margin_now`, `budget_left_now`; `_total` sums over this segment's entries landed after the campaign's start: `trade_gross_total`, `tips_total`, `fees_win_total`, `fees_fail_total`, `fees_other_total`, `rent_nonrecoverable_total`, `rent_locked_total` / `_delta`, `rent_reclaimed_total` / `_delta`, `trade_net_total`, `net_spend_total`, `transfer_in_total`, `transfer_out_total`, `entries_total` / `_delta`; `failed_fees_total_lamports` = the ledger's `fee_fail` + the base fees of failed landings the executor found by a status check whose records have not come (what the bash supervisor reads) and `failed_fees_delta_lamports`; `unreconciled_now` (the last reconciliation's residual), `last_recon_ok`, `last_recon_t_ns`, `recon_misses_total`, `snapshots_total`, `last_snapshot_age_s_now`, `counts` (the ledger's: `snapshot`, `snapshot_failed`, `snapshot_slot_mismatch`, `recon_ok`, `recon_miss`, `dup_segment`, `dup_campaign`, `raw_missing`), `supervisor` `{limits {basis, max_drop_per_hour_lamports, max_drop_per_day_lamports, floor_lamports, token_floor_tolerance, token_floors}, fired, recon_misses_total, alerts}` and `bot` (2.0's `money`, diagnostics).

**WP-07 review.** `money.stop_basis` is `net_spend` (review #4): `stop_value_now` (the campaign's start SOL + WSOL less the net spend: what the floor and the drop caps compare), `net_spend_now` (the campaign's, the earlier segments' included), `stop_tokens_now` (the start's token amounts moved by routes only), `floor_margin_now` (stop value − floor), `budget_left_now` (the test budget less the net spend), `drop_1h_now` / `drop_24h_now` on the stop value; `equity_stop_now` stays SOL + WSOL as the money view has it (informational: the owner's transfers are in it). New: `stale` (why the money view is stale, null when fresh; review #3), `trade_pnl_total` (the routes' trading result on the budget's basis), `owner_transfers_total`, `last_snapshot_age_s_now` (from the last good snapshot), `supervisor.acts` (whether the stop rules act: a sending run).

**As WP-20 writes it.** `alerts` (null without alerts; atomics only, read on the engine thread): `{enabled, off_why, raised_total, sent_total, messages_total, dropped_total, failed_total, deduped_total, held_total, below_level_total, coalesced_total, attempts_failed_total, rate_waits_total, queued_now, pending_now, last_sent_ns, last_error}`. `raised_total`: alerts at or above `[alerts] min_level` handed to the alerts (`below_level_total`: under it, not sent); `deduped_total`: of them, repeats of a key sent within `dedup_s`, counted and not sent (`held_total`: transitions held for the end of their window, §4.51); `dropped_total`: lost before any try (the queue full); `sent_total`: alerts delivered, in `messages_total` Telegram messages (`coalesced_total`: alerts that shared a message with an earlier one); `failed_total`: alerts whose message failed after its tries (a refusal, the tries spent), and those still undelivered when the process ended; `attempts_failed_total`: HTTP tries that failed, retried ones included; `rate_waits_total`: times a ready message waited for the rate; `queued_now`: in the queue, `pending_now`: taken by the sender, not yet delivered or failed; `last_error`: the last failure's text, redacted (§4.51). The counters are the process's (`_total` since its start, §2.2).

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.status/2.1`). New `rpc_budget` (`rpc::Registry::budget_json`): each provider's budget and every role's use of it since the previous line: `{interval_s, providers: {<name>: {max_rps, calls_delta, rps, used_pct, queued_now, cooling_ms_now, roles: {<role>: {calls_delta, rps, share_pct, reserved_rps, reserved_delta, throttled_delta, wait_ms_delta, rate_budget_delta}}}}, roles: {<role>: {calls_delta, rps, share_pct}}}`. `rps` is requests sent per second of the interval, `share_pct` the share of the provider's `max_rps` (in `roles`: of the summed `max_rps` of the limited providers), `reserved_rps` the role's reserved share (null: none), `reserved_delta` the tokens it got through that share; a role with a share is listed even when idle; `interval_s` null on the first line. `alts` gains `bytes_now` (what the cache counts against `max_mb`: 33 bytes a slot, 64 a table), `evicted_delta`, `refetch_delta` (fetches of tables the cache had evicted), `fetch_calls_total` and `fetch_tables_total` (the batched fetcher's requests and the tables they carried; null offline). `chainfetch` gains `idx_check_delta.below` / `.above` (stream comparisons the slot index listed before / after the stream's index), `slotidx_pos_kind_delta` `{block_index, nonvote_rank, unverified}` (lines by position kind, §4.16b), `slotidx_rank_only_delta` (tracked positions given as ranks or unverified: not written as `idx`), `slotidx_votes_omitted_by_now` (the providers whose getBlock lists leave the votes out). `chainfetch.by_role` and resources' `rpc` name the role `alt` (the lookup tables) and `snapshot` (the state re-read).

**At the merge of the FT-000 fixes (integ/stage0)** (`arbx.status/2.2`). WP-07's 2.1 (`money` from the ledger) and the FT-000 fixes' 2.1 (`rpc_budget`) were two shapes under one number: a 2.2 line has both, and WP-20's `alerts` (which its lane added without a bump). A 2.1 line comes from one lane's build (never run on Cherry): the fields it lacks are absent.

**WP-06** (at the merge of WP-06, `arbx.status/2.2` unchanged: the counter keeps its meaning, the token accounts our landings left behind; WP-07's `money` is the ledger's, the executor's counters are `money.bot`). `money.bot.new_token_accounts_total` counts the held-set additions that landed (a bundle that kept the token account it created: the size budget's `held_ata` fallback, attempts.jsonl `tx.held_addition`), not every account a bundle created: a create+close bundle locks the rent and reclaims it in the same transaction (rent locked = rent reclaimed, nothing held), and the ledger books it so (`rent_locked_total` and `rent_reclaimed_total` grow by the same rent: §4.42). `[live] max_new_token_accounts` is gone (a config that still has it does not load); `[builder] held_max` (the permanent WSOL, USDC and USDT aside) bounds the held set, and it is 0 by default: no held-set addition is ever made unless the owner opts in (review WP-06 #1; §4.37), so this counter stays 0; WP-07's rent cap (`[supervisor] rent_cap_sol`) limits those additions only (§4.37). meta.json `limits` drops `max_new_token_accounts` and adds `rent_neutral` (true unless held-set additions are configured: `held_max` > 0 with `create_close`), `create_close`, `held_max`, `allow_uva_create` and `payer_guard`.

### 4.9 feedhealth.jsonl

Producer: `src/rec/health.rs`, every 10 s, one line per feed (including the state stream as feed `state` and each tx stream).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| seq, t_ns, interval_ns | | |
| feed, name, kind | integer, str, str | |
| connected_now | bool | |
| reconnects_total, errors_total | integer | |
| packets_delta, txs_delta, forwarded_delta | integer | |
| first_arrivals_delta | integer | signatures this feed delivered first |
| lead_ms | pct(interval) | this feed's arrival minus the first arrival, over signatures it delivered |
| last_msg_age_ms_now | ms | |
| slot_max_now, slot_lag_now | slot, integer | feed's newest slot; chain tip minus it |
| provider_lag_ms | pct(interval) / null | `t_recv − created_at` when the provider fills `created_at` |
| shreds | {data_delta, code_delta, dup_delta, bad_delta, sets_ok_delta, sets_failed_delta, version} / null | raw shred feeds (`AsmStats`) |
| udp_drops_delta | integer / null | kernel drops for the feed's socket |
| channel_drops_delta | integer | events the feed could not hand to the engine |

**As WP-02 writes it.** Written by the engine's housekeeping every 10 s (the line builder is `src/rec/health.rs`), the state stream first (`feed` null, `name` `state`, `kind` `state-stream`), then each enabled feed by id (`kind` as in the config: `udp`, `multicast`, `jito-entries`, `yellowstone-tx`, ...). `seq` counts lines (one per feed per tick). `first_arrivals_delta` and `lead_ms` (ms, pct(interval)) are counted when a signature leaves its 10 s window (txarr), so they lag the line by up to 10 s. `connected_now` is true from a feed's first message after a (re)connect until the stream ends; raw feeds are connected while their receive thread runs. `slot_max_now` is the newest slot of the feed's messages (shreds' headers for raw feeds; for the state stream, from WP-03 on, account updates and processed / confirmed / finalized slot statuses only: interslot statuses run ahead, review WP-03 #6), `slot_lag_now` the engine's newest processed slot minus it. `shreds` (raw feeds): the assembler's totals copied every 2 s (`bad_delta` counts bad packets, implausible slots and other shred versions). `udp_drops_delta` (raw feeds; null otherwise): the kernel's drops of the feed's socket from `/proc/net/udp` (read every 10 s by the monitor). `provider_lag_ms` is null (WP-03 parses `created_at`). **As WP-03 writes it:** `provider_lag_ms` (pct(interval), ms) is the receive time minus the message's `created_at` for the state stream, the transaction streams and the deshred feeds when their provider stamps messages (null for a feed whose provider never did); a provider clock ahead of ours counts as 0.

### 4.10 resources.jsonl

Producer: `src/rec/health.rs`, every 10 s.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| seq, t_ns, interval_ns | | |
| cpu_user_ms_delta, cpu_sys_ms_delta | integer | process, from `/proc/self/stat` |
| engine_cpu_ms_delta | integer | engine thread |
| rss_bytes_now, threads_now | integer | |
| load_avg_1m_now | number | |
| recorder_queues | {stream: {now, hwm_delta}} | |
| recorder_bytes_delta | {stream: integer} | compressed bytes written |
| disk | {run_dir_bytes_now, fs_free_bytes_now} | |
| rpc | {"<role>/<provider>": {calls_delta, errors_delta, rate_limited_delta, p50_ms}} | from the global per-(role, provider) stats registry (F29). Before revision 2, clients built with `Rpc::only` had fresh, invisible `Stats` (rpc.rs:69-72) |
| runtimes | {hot: {busy_ms_delta, tasks_now}, bg: {busy_ms_delta, tasks_now, blocking_now}} | WP-02 runtime split |

**As WP-02 writes it** (the monitor thread, every 10 s). `recorder_queues` is by queue (`core`, `accounts`, `journal`, `chain`, `bulk`, `slow`), `hwm_delta` the high-water mark since the last line, `bytes_now` and `bytes_hwm` the declared payload bytes queued (the byte cap's count); `recorder_bytes_delta` lists the streams that wrote compressed bytes since the last line. `udp_drops_delta`: the kernel's receive drops of every UDP socket of the process (`/proc/net/udp`, `udp6`). `engine_cpu_ms_delta` reads the engine thread's `/proc/self/task/<tid>/stat`. `rpc`: WP-03b's registry (below). `runtimes.*.busy_ms_delta` is tokio's worker busy time; `blocking_now` counts the large-reply parses running in rt_bg's blocking pool; `runtimes.hot.rpc_violations_total` the RPC work found on an `arbx-hot` thread (0 unless a client was not bound). A first line's deltas are null.

**As WP-03b writes it** (`rpc::Registry::resources_json`; every client of the process records into the one registry, `rpc::Registry::global`). `rpc` = `{"<role>/<provider>": {calls_delta, ok_delta, errors_delta, rate_limited_delta, timeouts_delta, body_errors_delta, http_errors_delta, transport_errors_delta, rpc_errors_delta, no_result_delta, rate_budget_delta, throttled_delta, throttle_wait_ms_delta, p50_ms, p90_ms, p99_ms}}` for the keys with requests or refusals since the previous line (`calls_delta` is one count per HTTP request sent, `ok_delta` + `errors_delta`: a race or a retry counts every provider asked; a call refused for want of a token sent nothing and counts only in `rate_budget_delta`; percentiles over the answered ones, ms; `{}` when nothing was asked). Roles: `boot` (the run's root client until the executor is built: pools, the account snapshot, the route program, the prewarm), `exec` (the `[exec] rpc` client), `engine` (lookup tables, DLMM bin arrays), `leaders`, `provenance` (the venue programs), `wallet`, `blockhash`, `backfill`, `slotidx`, `sample`, `warm` (the keep-alive `getSlot`), `snapshot` and `settle` (later WPs), `other`. Classes: `rate_limited` (HTTP 429, or a JSON-RPC rate-limit error), `timeout`, `body` (a reply cut or garbled while it was read, or not JSON: cmp2's Shyft "error decoding response body"), `http`, `transport`, `rpc` (a JSON-RPC error answer), `no_result`, `rate_budget` (no token of the provider's bucket before the call's deadline: no request); `throttled` counts requests that waited for their token (one granted at once is not throttled). **Budgets**: each provider has one token bucket for the whole process (`[[chainfetch.provider]] max_rps`, else `[chainfetch] default_max_rps`, 0 by default: no bucket; burst max(1, max_rps)). Roles have four priorities (meta.json `chainfetch.priorities`): critical (boot, snapshot, exec, settle) may take its last token, normal (engine, leaders, provenance, wallet) keeps a quarter of the burst in reserve, index (slotidx, sample, blockhash) and bulk (backfill, warm, other) half; callers that must wait queue in the bucket by priority, then in order of arrival, so a waiting caller is never overtaken by a later or a lower one (the slot index is served before the backfill on a provider they share); a 429 drains the bucket and cools the provider down (250 ms, doubling with each 429 in a row, at most 8 s; no tokens accrue while it cools), and a bounded call passes over a cooling provider while another is ready. A plain call of the `exec` role waits at most 1 s for a token (then fails over or fails), the others 10 s. A bounded call ends at once on a JSON-RPC error that states a chain fact (-32007, -32009 skipped slot, -32015 unsupported version); another error answer is taken as the provider's own: the call asks the providers that have not answered, and fails only when every one refused (an error without a code, a gateway's message, is retried like a transport failure). **The boot policy** (PLAN-ADDENDUM I-1): from the root client's creation until the executor is built, every call of every client made from it (`Exec::new`'s too) retries with backoff (250 ms doubling to 8 s, jitter) across the providers, each request 5 s at most, for `[chainfetch] boot_retry_s` (90); each failed request is an events.jsonl `rpc_boot_retry` (at most one a second), and only a failure that lasts the whole budget, or a refusal from every provider (an error answer each, `...; every provider refused the request`), ends the boot: `rpc_boot_failed`, and stop.json reason `error` whose detail reads `<method>: sustained RPC failure: no provider answered in <s> s (<n> attempts: <provider> <class> x<k>, ...; <why>); last: <error>`. A stop during the boot ends the retries at once. Background tasks started during the boot (the LeaderBook, the warm-up calls) have a phase of their own and call as configured. **Connections**: the providers' hosts are resolved once at boot (`Rpc::prewarm`, which also opens each connection; run.log `rpc <name>: DNS ...`) and every reconnect is served from that cache (re-resolved every 5 minutes in the background); HTTP/2 pings every 15 s and TCP keep-alives keep connections up, and a `getSlot` (role `warm`, only with a token to spare) goes to a provider idle for 20 s.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (S1-1, S1-3). Roles: `alt` (the engine's lookup tables, normal priority, batched: up to `[alts] batch_max` (100) tables a getMultipleAccounts, `calls_inflight` (2) at once) and `snapshot` (the state stream's re-read after its first connection and every reconnection: critical; in FT-000 it queued 51.4 s behind the lookup-table fetches); `engine` keeps the fork refetches and new DLMM bin arrays. At the merge with WP-07 (integ/stage0), the ledger's balance snapshots and its reconciliation's window searches (§4.43, §4.44) are role `ledger` (critical: the money view gates every live send; a few calls an hour): WP-07 had used the then unused `snapshot`. **Reserved shares** (`[chainfetch] reserve`, default `{slotidx = 0.5, backfill = 0.25}`: the share of each serving provider's `max_rps`; a chain-fetch role's providers are those `[[chainfetch.provider]]` gives it, any other role's every [rpc] provider; at most 0.95 of a provider in all, `rpc::MAX_RESERVE`): a role with a share holds a second bucket of entitlement at its rate (burst max(1, rate)); while it holds a token, the role's waiting callers are served ahead of the priority order (after critical callers), leaving a quarter of the burst for critical callers as normal ones do; every grant still takes the provider's token, so its `max_rps` is never exceeded, and an idle role's share goes to the others. The backfill sizes itself to at least its shares (`spare_rps`). A provider's own `[[chainfetch.provider]] reserve` replaces `[chainfetch] reserve` for it: the deploy configs give Shyft `{slotidx = 0.95}` (3.8/s of its 4: the slot index needs one getBlock per confirmed slot, 3.7/s in FT-000) and Haan `{backfill = 0.5}` (2/s). resources' `rpc` keys gain `reserved_delta` (tokens granted through the role's share). FT-000's 30 minutes, simulated on the deploy split with segment 1's recorded lookup-table fetch times (`chainfetch::tests::ft000_the_rpc_budget_split_on_the_recorded_workload`, `ARBX_FT000_RUN=<copy of the run>`): FT-000's build made 10,959 lookup-table requests (one table each; 76% of all requests), its slot index read 1,663 of 6,664 confirmed slots and ended 3,015 behind (FT-000 measured 2,060 of 6,709 and 3,005 behind), its backfill fetched 57 signatures (0.03/s) and the wallet history 748; with the fixes, the 10,958 tables went in 3,406 requests (3.2 a request; a table waited p50 251 ms, p90 461 ms for its answer), the re-read took 7.4 s (13.4 s in the simulation of FT-000's build, 51.4 s measured), the slot index read all 6,664 slots (3.70/s, at most 19 behind, 0 at the end), the backfill 3,792 signatures (2.11/s, against a measured need of 15.8/s: OD-1), the wallet history nothing (§4.15), and the blockhash poll ran 71 times (4,025 skipped: the slot index's blockhash was fresh). The synthetic 6-minute variant (`ft000_the_rpc_budget_split_before_and_after`, 7.5 tables a second) is a test.

**Runtimes** (`src/runtime.rs`, WP-02 item 1). `rt_hot`: 2 workers named `arbx-hot`: the state stream, the gRPC feeds, the send paths and their keep-alives, the Jito result streams, the executor's send tasks. `rt_bg`: `[runtime] bg_threads` (3) workers named `arbx-bg`, niced +5 (its blocking pool too): every RPC call. The client is bound to it (`Rpc::bind`): each call is spawned there whichever runtime awaits it, and replies over 256 KiB are parsed in its blocking pool. The boot, the route-program check, the engine's lookup-table and bin-array fetches, the executor's simulations and settlements and the venue-program hashing therefore run on rt_bg. RPC work found on an `arbx-hot` thread is counted (a debug assertion in debug builds; a run.log WARNING at the end).

### 4.11 keepalive.jsonl (v1, kept)

Producer: `send/mod.rs::Paths::start` (:777-798). Fields: `t_ns`, `path`, `ms` (round trip, null on error), `error`. In Stage 0 only the jito-bundle path is enabled, so it measures the block engine's RTT.

### 4.12 accounts.bin (new): every watched-account update

Producers:
- the feed task in `feeds/mod.rs::forward` (account items);
- RPC fetches: boot, DLMM array fetches (`engine.rs::fetch_accounts`), fork refetches.

All go through `src/rec/accounts.rs`, which assigns each record its `aseq` when it is emitted. `aseq` is carried in `Event::Account` and the store version, so the journal and replay can name the exact record. Every update is recorded, including ones the store will reject as stale. The store's verdict is not known to the feed task, so it is recorded in the journal's ACCOUNT record (F24).

**Byte format** (after zstd decompression), little-endian:

```
file header:  magic "ARBXACC\0" (8) | u16 format = 2 | u16 hlen | hlen bytes of JSON header (§1.3)
record:       u8 kind | u32 body_len | body
  kind 1 DICT   u32 acct_id | [32] pubkey | u8 role | u32 pool_index (0xFFFFFFFF = none)
  kind 2 FULL   u64 aseq | u32 acct_id | u64 slot | u64 write_version (0 if src is RPC) | u64 t_recv_ns | u8 src | u8 flags
                | [64] txn (only if flags.bit0) | u64 lamports | [32] owner | u64 data_xxh3
                | u32 data_len | data
  kind 3 XOR    same as FULL, but `data` is data XOR (the previous record's data for this acct_id in this file);
                lengths must match, else the writer emits FULL
  kind 4 MARK   u64 t_ns | u8 mark (1 boot_begin, 2 boot_end, 3 stream_reconnect, 4 watch_list_changed, 5 fork_refetch)
                | u32 n | n × u32 acct_id
  kind 6 GAP    u64 first_lost_aseq | u64 n_lost        (written with the next record after a queue drop)
```

| Field | Semantics |
|---|---|
| aseq | segment-wide sequence number, without holes unless a GAP record says so |
| role | 0 other, 1 pool, 2 vault, 3 extra/config, 4 dlmm_bin_array, 5 mint, 6 clock_sysvar, 7 dlmm_bitmap_ext, 8 programdata, 9 wallet, 10 wallet_token_account, 11 pump_fee_config, 12 pump_global_config (WP-16), 13 wallet_pump_uva (WP-06). WP-16: role 5 also covers every Token-2022 mint of a watched pool (its quote scans it for a transfer fee or hook), not only PumpSwap base mints |
| src | 0 grpc stream, 1 rpc boot, 2 rpc refetch, 3 rpc snapshot |
| flags | bit0 has a txn signature; bit1 `is_startup`; bit2 (2.1, WP-04) has a `bank_id`: a `u64 bank_id` follows the txn (revision 1's "stale" bit is gone: the feed task cannot know it) |
| data_xxh3 | xxh3-64 of the full (reconstructed) data; replay verifies it |

RPC-sourced records have no `write_version` (0). Within one slot the store orders them by `aseq`, after every stream record of that slot. References always use `aseq`, never `(acct_id, slot, write_version)`, which would be ambiguous (F25).

Keyframes: the first record of an account in each file is FULL; then every `accounts_keyframe_every` (64) records; and whenever the data length changes. Readers keep the last data per `acct_id` and apply XOR records.

**As WP-04 writes it** (`arbx.accounts/2.1`, still format 2: a 2.0 reader misreads only records with bit 2 set, which only a provider sending `bank_id` produces). FULL / XOR: `... | u8 flags | [64] txn (bit0) | [u64 bank_id] (bit2) | u64 lamports | ...`; `bank_id` is `SubscribeUpdateAccount.bank_id` (0 / absent: no bit). `src` 3 `rpc_snapshot` is the engine's re-read of every followed account after a state-stream reconnect (I-4); `src` 2 `rpc_refetch` also covers an account a fork left with no version (preceded by MARK 5 `fork_refetch` naming it). The engine reads RPC accounts 100 at a time, each batch with its own context slot. **WP-04 review:** the re-read (`src` 3) also follows the stream's first connection (the boot snapshot is read before the stream subscribes), starts one slot after the stream's resume slot (its first account update or processed slot), paces its chunks 120 ms apart (at most 4 reads at once), and packs each pool's own accounts (pool, consistency set, vaults, followed bin arrays) into one chunk; each chunk's records are followed by journal INPUT 11 `fetch` (§4.13). The boot snapshot (`src` 1) labels each 100-account call with its own context slot (it used one label, the last call's, for every account). The store keeps a version older than the current one in its ring when no version of its slot (and bank) is held (the late-leader fork: the canonical block replayed after the abandoned one, at a lower slot); a fork rollback then falls back to it. The store orders versions as above: `(slot, write_version)` for stream records; an RPC read after every stream record of its slot, RPC reads by `aseq`.

Used by: replay (store reconstruction), attribution R4 (route state at every version), predictions R8, X1 (route-wide age), X3 (repricing at the post-trigger version), `moves.log` is derivable from it.

**As WP-03 writes it** (`src/rec/accounts.rs`, `AcctRec::record`). Producers: the state stream's feed task (`feeds/mod.rs::forward`, src 0 with `write_version` and `is_startup`), `arbx run`'s boot snapshot (src 1, between MARK `boot_begin` and `boot_end`; programdata accounts are not in the snapshot: megabytes each, the stream reports their upgrades), and the engine's re-fetches of DLMM bin arrays it starts following (`engine.rs::fetch_accounts`, src 2). `src` 3 and MARK `fork_refetch` come with WP-04 / WP-07. The `aseq` is taken and the record queued under one lock, so the file is in `aseq` order whatever thread records. `role` and `pool_index` come from the run's role table (`rec::accounts::Roles`): the registry's pools (1), vaults (2), PumpSwap base mints (5), CPMM configs (3) and the PumpSwap fee config (11), and WP-03's extra watched accounts: the Clock sysvar (6), every DLMM pool's bitmap-extension PDA whether or not it exists (7, with its pool), the programdata accounts of the route program and every venue program (8), the wallet (9) and its token accounts (10: its WSOL account and its associated account for every registry mint); DLMM bin arrays (4) are added as the engine follows them, with a MARK `watch_list_changed` naming the new ones. A reconnect of the state stream writes MARK `stream_reconnect` (n = 0). Keyframes: FULL for an account's first record in a file, on a length change, and when `accounts_keyframe_every` - 1 XOR records followed the last FULL (the recorder's interval, ×4 under disk pressure). The queue's byte cap counts each record's data. Reader: `rec::accounts::parse` / `read_dir` (XOR applied, `data_xxh3` checked).

**WP-06.** Role 13 `wallet_pump_uva` is the wallet's PumpSwap user volume accumulator (PumpSwap PDA `["user_volume_accumulator", wallet]`), followed with the wallet (`[state] watch_wallet`) whenever the registry has a PumpSwap pool, so its creation is seen (a PumpSwap buy without it would create it: 1,346,200 lamports, never recovered; §4.32 `uva_missing`). The store keeps each record's `lamports` (`store::Acct::lamports`: the stream's, the boot snapshot's and the engine's re-reads'; 0 for a tool's read without them) and the engine's state digest folds them, so the bankroll a size cap reads (`Engine::bankroll`: the wallet's lamports and its WSOL account's amount, for WP-07's `bankroll_cap`) is a journaled input. Whether the wallet holds a token's account is the store's view of its role-10 record (a token account in state initialized or frozen: `store::token_account_exists`): an account the owner creates mid-run, or a held-set addition landing, stops the create and close from the next decision on, and a replay builds the same bundles. The DLMM bitmap extension (role 7) is no longer checked once at boot (pools.json `flag` stays false): the builder names it when the store holds it (§4.37).

### 4.13 journal.bin (new): the engine's input journal

Producer: `src/rec/journal.rs`, called from `Engine::step(ev, t_engine)` and `Engine::timers(t)`. These replace the body of `engine.rs::run` (:324-358) in WP-03 (moved from WP-09, F11).

**Determinism contract.**
- **Clock.** The engine reads the clock once per event. That value (`t_engine_ns`) is journaled and used for every decision made while handling the event.
- **Timers.** Timeouts (commit groups, quiescence, lookup-table retries, in-flight expiry, the lag SLO minute) are evaluated only in `timers(t)`, and every call is journaled as TIMER. One exception kept from arb-lab: expired confirmation waits are also checked at the end of each input, at that input's `t_engine_ns` (deterministic).
- **Outside inputs.** Inputs from other subsystems that change engine or send-check decisions reach the engine as events and are journaled as INPUT: blockhash-ring heads, leader-schedule windows, SendGate changes, quarantines, in-flight releases, health changes.
- **Latency.** Stage timestamps for latency reports are taken with real clock reads, but they are never decision inputs, and replay does not compare them.
- **Coverage.** Recordings made before WP-03 have no journal and cannot be replayed.

```
file header:  magic "ARBXJRN\0" | u16 format = 2 | u16 hlen | JSON header (schema arbx.journal/2.1)
record:       u8 kind | u32 body_len | u64 seq | u64 t_engine_ns | body
  1 TX_FIRST   u16 feed | u64 slot | u64 t_rx | u64 t_decoded | i8 executed (-1 none, 0 failed, 1 ok)
               | u8 enc (0 wire bytes, 1 protobuf Transaction) | u32 len | bytes
               | u8 has_loaded | [u16 nw | nw×32 | u16 nr | nr×32] | u8 has_poh | [u64 slot | u64 hashes | u32 ticks | u64 hpt]
  2 TX_COPY    [64] sig | u16 feed | u64 t_rx            (a later copy: timing only, as the engine uses it)
               | u8 has_poh | [u64 slot | u64 hashes | u32 ticks | u64 hpt]     (2.1; readers of the 74-byte form ignore it)
               | u64 slot                                                      (WP-09: the copy's slot; readers of 2.2 ignore it)
  3 ACCOUNT    u64 aseq (accounts.bin) | u8 store_verdict (0 accepted, 1 stale)
  4 SLOT       u64 slot | u64 parent (u64::MAX = unknown) | i32 status | u64 t
  5 BLOCK_META u64 slot | [32] blockhash | u64 height | u64 parent_slot | u64 t
  6 ALT        [32] table | u8 found | u16 n | n×32
  7 INPUT      u8 kind (1 blockhash_head, 2 leader_window, 3 gate, 4 quarantine, 5 inflight_release, 6 health,
               7 wallet_tx, 8 slotidx_missing, 9 settled, 10 engine_init, 11 fetch, 12 exec_init, 13 snapshot) | u32 len | JSON
  8 TICK       (no body: housekeeping ran here; status only)
  9 STATUS     (ignored by replay)
 10 TIMER      u64 t | u8 what (2.1: 1 flush, 2 housekeeping; absent = both)
               (timers_at(t, what) ran: replay calls it at exactly this point, time and scope)
 12 GAP        u64 first_lost_seq | u64 n_lost
```

**2.1** (review WP-03; one minor version over 2.0, same framing, no 2.0 recordings exist): the TX_COPY PoH tail, a repeated TX_FIRST for a later copy that carries loaded addresses (readers that count TX_FIRST as unique transactions must count signatures instead), INPUT kinds 9 and 10, and TIMER `what`.

**2.2** (WP-04; same framing; readers of 2.1 ignore the appended fields): SLOT appends `u64 bank_id` and BLOCK_META `u64 bank_id` (u64::MAX: not sent); ALT `found` 2 is a failed fetch (`u16 n` = 0, then `u16 len | UTF-8 error`; both 0 and 2 are retried) and `found` 3 a lookup table preloaded at boot from a previous segment's alts-final (`u16 n | ceil(n/8)-byte known-bitmap | 32 × each known entry`), written with the boot records (`t_engine_ns` 0); a later copy from an executed feed is a TX_FIRST too (its status settles signer outcomes and registers W(T)); TIMER `what` bits are 1 the commit timers (WP-03's state flush, which commit groups replaced), 2 housekeeping, 4 the accuracy monitor's idle work (an absent `what` means all); INPUT 6 `health` (the state stream's task: `{src: "state", connected, connects, reconnects, error}`) and INPUT 8 `slotidx_missing` (`{slot, missing: [sig]}`, WP-03b) are engine inputs; engine_init adds `signer_stats_sha256` (the file's sha256 as read, I-5) and `knobs` (WP-04's `[state]` / `[alts]` / `[accuracy]` settings as the engine runs them, applied by `replay_engine`). **WP-04 review** (same framing, no version change: a reader that does not know INPUT 11 skips it): INPUT 11 `fetch` is how one chunk of an engine RPC read ended (`reads.rs`: the re-read after a connection, a fork refetch, new DLMM bin arrays), written after the chunk's ACCOUNT records: `{job, chunk, what: resnapshot | fork_refetch | dlmm_arrays, attempt, n, ok: true, slot (the call's context slot), missing: [pk the node did not have]}` or `{..., ok: false, err}`; the engine verifies a re-read chunk's accounts when its slot is at or after the stream's resume slot, and reads a failed or behind chunk again (250 ms, 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, then every 10 s; given up `resync_give_up_s` after the re-read started, 600 s for the others). Chunk starts are engine times run by the timers (`what` 1), so a replay reads the answers from the journal and decides the same. ACCOUNT's verdict is now "refused": the store held a newer version (stale, possibly kept below the current one for a fork rollback) or the update's slot was already abandoned (`Engine::account_refused`; replay checks it against the same rule).

**WP-09** (same framing, no version change: readers of 2.2 ignore the appended field): TX_COPY appends `u64 slot`, the copy's slot, after its PoH tail. An unexecuted copy raises the engine's shred slot (the tip while the state stream is down, I-4; in the state digest), so a replay feeds each copy with its slot (review WP-09 #15; a record without it is fed at slot 0, as before).

**2.3** (the FT-000 fixes; same framing): ALT `found` 4 is an eviction of the lookup-table cache (S1-4), written by the engine after the input (or the boot's preload) whose handling evicted: `[0; 32] | 4 | u16 n | n × 32 (the evicted tables, at most 65,535 listed) | u64 evicted_total | u64 evict_hash` (the cache's running count and a running xxh3 of every evicted table, in order). It is not an input: the cache follows only from the journaled inputs and engine_init's knobs (`alts.max_tables`, `alts.max_bytes`; a record written before them has none), so a replay evicts the same tables at the same inputs, and `journal::replay` checks the pair at each record (`ReplayStats::alt_evictions`, `alt_evict_mismatches`; replay.json `journal.alt_evictions`, `alt_evict_mismatches`, and `not_whole` `alt_evict_mismatch` unless a what-if changed `[alts]`). A table needed again after its eviction is fetched again: its answer is an ordinary ALT record. Readers of 2.2 read `found` 4 as an answer without addresses (the toolkit's reader knows it: `evicted`). The lookup tables' answers come from the batched fetcher (§4.10): each table's answer is still its own ALT record. At the merge with WP-07 (integ/stage0) a 2.3 journal also holds WP-07's INPUT 13 `snapshot` and the decision-point record's `money` ("As WP-07 writes it" below: same framing, added in its lane without a version change); a journal of either lane alone replays in the merged build (a reader takes each INPUT kind and ALT `found` value as it comes).

`seq` has no holes unless a GAP record says so. Replay refuses to compare decisions inside a gap interval and reports it. Volume: §5.

**As WP-03 writes it** (`src/rec/journal.rs`; `Engine::step(ev, t_engine)`, `Engine::timers(t)`).
- **Who writes it:** only the engine thread, at its dequeue point (`step`), and `arbx run`'s boot before the engine starts: the boot snapshot's ACCOUNT records carry `t_engine_ns` 0; replay applies them straight to the store, as `arbx run` does (`journal::boot_store`, which then derives the followed DLMM bin arrays).
- **The run loop:** `run` journals the engine_init record first, then takes one clock read per input and calls `step`; every decision made while handling it reads that value (prices' unix time for DLMM / DAMM v2 fees, deadlines, expiries, pool CU-price windows, signature windows). `timers_at(t, what)` writes a TIMER record, then runs the state flush (`what` 1), expired confirmation waits (always), and housekeeping (`what` 2; at most every 5 ms, with its 2 s DLMM drift check and 10 s status line). arb-lab's rules (review WP-03 #7): on every 5 ms idle wake-up both; after an input, the flush only, and only when the queue is empty with state triggers pending (an empty queue is the only sign a transaction's updates have all arrived: nothing is flushed while it is not); every 10 s while the queue never empties, housekeeping (plus the flush if the queue is empty then). Expired confirmation waits are also checked at the end of every input, at its `t_engine_ns` (deterministic, as at HEAD). The timer anchors (when housekeeping, the drift check and the status line last ran) start at the first journaled input's or timer's time, never at an unjournaled clock read (review WP-03 #2a). Stage timestamps (`st`, latency series) stay real clock reads.
- **engine_init** (INPUT 10, `t_engine_ns` 0, after the boot snapshot's ACCOUNT records; review WP-03 #2b, #2c): what the engine starts with that no input carries: `{min_tip (the cheapest enabled send path's tip: every opportunity's net), strategy_sha256 (the [strategy] section as JSON, signer_stats_file left out), executor (bool), signer_stats_file, signer_stats: {signer: [ok, miss]} as loaded}`. The signer statistics file is cross-run, rewritten every 10 s and shared (PLAN-ADDENDUM I-5): only its content as loaded makes trigger-skip decisions reproducible. `journal::replay_engine(reg, cfg, records, accounts)` builds the replay's engine from it (the boot store, an offline engine, the record applied; an error without the record or when `cfg`'s `[strategy]` is not the run's); `Engine::new_offline` never reads or writes the statistics file. A run with an executor folds its send checks and routes into `decision_digest`; an offline replay reproduces them from WP-05 / WP-09 on.
- **The boot section** is the leading records with `t_engine_ns` 0 (the boot snapshot's ACCOUNT records and engine_init); a GAP among them (it carries the writer's clock) does not end it, and replay reports it.
- **TX_FIRST** holds the bytes as the feed delivered them: wire bytes (`enc` 0) from raw shred and entries feeds, the protobuf `Transaction` (`enc` 1) from deshred and transaction streams; a synthetic arrival without bytes is re-encoded from its parsed form (`tx::to_wire`, legacy / v0, its first signature and a zero blockhash). A later copy that carries loaded addresses for its lookups is also a TX_FIRST record (the engine learns lookup-table entries from it); replay treats a repeated signature as a copy. **TX_COPY** appends `u8 has_poh | [u64 slot | u64 hashes | u32 ticks | u64 hpt]` (the engine keeps a later copy's PoH position when the first had none); readers of the 74-byte form ignore the tail.
- **ACCOUNT** names the record by `aseq`; the verdict is the store's check before the update (`Store::is_stale`). **SLOT** is every status of the state stream (interslot ones too), `parent` u64::MAX when unknown. **BLOCK_META** `height` u64::MAX when unknown. **ALT** is the answer of a lookup-table fetch (`found` 0: none). **STATUS** is `u16 feed | UTF-8 message` (a feed's status line; replay ignores it). **TICK** marks a status line written by `timers`.
- **INPUT** kinds written now: 2 `leader_window` (`{start, leaders: [pk]}`, from the LeaderBook's refreshes); 3 `gate` in two forms: `src` "decision" (review WP-03 #3), written by the engine thread at the send check that saw the SendGate's mode, whenever that mode differs from the last one journaled: `{mode (as the check read it), reason, detail, since_ns, sticky, src, opp}`, right after the input whose handling made the decision and with its `t_engine_ns` (the check reads the live gate, so a pause closes it at once; a replay of send checks applies the record at opportunity `opp`; the mode is also folded into `decision_digest`), and `src` "watch" (the SendGate's `json()` sampled every 100 ms on the background runtime whenever it changes: status and coverage, not a decision input; replay passes it to the engine, which ignores it); 9 `settled` (an executor settlement in full: `{attempt, error, fired (send::Fired), landed: {path, sig, slot, tx}, jito, nonce_value}`; the executor's replay is WP-05 / WP-09's); 10 `engine_init` (above). Kinds 4 and 5 come with WP-05 and WP-07; 6 `health` and 11 `fetch` are WP-04's (above). **WP-03b** writes 1 `blockhash_head` (`{hash, slot, height, src (blocks_meta, slotidx, entries, poll), commitment, t_ns}`, whenever the ring's newest fresh confirmed blockhash changes), 7 `wallet_tx` (`{sig, slot, idx, ok, err, fee, wallet_kind, wallet_sol, wallet_tok, attempt, found_by, created, closed, pre_run}`, `created` and `closed` as counts, for every wallet.jsonl record) and 8 `slotidx_missing` (`{slot, status (ok, skipped), missing: [sig]}`, for every slot whose confirmed block lacks D1 signatures an account update stamped with it); the engine journals them; WP-04's engine drops the versions a `slotidx_missing` names (fork evidence), the other two wait for WP-05 and WP-07.
- **As WP-09 writes it** (review WP-09 #3): a decision without an executor (`Engine::gate_no_executor`: reason `no_executor` while the SendGate allows sends, else `gate_<mode>:<reason>`) journals what it read of the SendGate as the send checks do: an INPUT 3 `src` "decision" record `{mode (null: the engine has no gate), reason, detail, since_ns, sticky, src, opp, executor: false, why (the decision's reason)}`, right after the input or timer whose handling made the decision, whenever the reason differs from the last one journaled (the reason, not only the mode). `journal::replay` stages every decision-point record ahead of the record whose handling made it (the records that follow it, TICK records between them skipped) and the engine applies each at its opportunity (`Engine::gate_seen`): the replay's decisions read the gate the run's read, whatever thread changed it and when. Before WP-09's records the replay's gate is meta.json `send_gate`. The `watch` records stay status and coverage only: a replay does not apply them either (the first WP-09 draft did, and disagreed with the run for up to 100 ms after each change).
- **As WP-04 writes it:** `replay_engine` also applies the boot section's preloaded tables (ALT `found` 3) and the run's knobs, and the boot store keeps the run's ring size; `replay` sets the replay clock (`feeds::clock::Virtual`, PLAN WP-04 item 11) to each record's `t_engine_ns`, so stage timestamps are journaled times too. A journal written by `Engine::run` with WP-04's inputs replays to the run's state digest, decision digest and identical `decisions.jsonl` / `commits.jsonl` lines (stages aside; test `a_wp04_run_replays_to_the_run`). Recordings made before WP-04 do not replay with this engine: `replay_engine` refuses them (the `[strategy]` hash in their engine_init changed with the removed keys, and their config no longer loads; WP-04 review N8). A replay reproduces a run of the same build.
- **Replay** (`journal::replay(engine, records, accounts)`, on the engine `replay_engine` built): each record's event with its `t_engine_ns`, each TIMER at its time and scope; it counts ACCOUNT records whose verdict differs from the replayed store's, records it cannot find in accounts.bin, GAPs, skipped INPUTs (settlements, engine_init) and decision-point gate records (`gate_observed`). A journal written by `Engine::run` itself replays to the run's state and decision digests (test `a_journal_written_by_run_replays_to_the_run`). `Engine::digest()` (the decision state: store contents and versions, signature windows, opportunities, predictions, signer statistics, lookup tables, counters, pending groups) and `Engine::decision_digest()` (a running hash of every opportunity and every route handed to the executor) compare runs.
- **As WP-05 writes it:** INPUT 3's decision-point record (`src` "decision") also carries what else the send checks read from outside the journal: `leader` `{slot, identity, kind, kind_src}` (the target slot's leader, its kind from the LeaderBook; WP-08 review #18), `jito_token_ok` (a valid cached block-engine token, or none needed) and `recorder_ok`. It is written whenever (gate mode, leader kind, token, recorder) differs from the last record, so the leader's kind reaches replay. The engine now reads INPUT 1 `blockhash_head` and 2 `leader_window` into `exec::SendView` (the blockhash the send checks use and its age, the target slot's leader), and BLOCK_META too (the processed blockhash; the block height attempts expire by). INPUT 7 `wallet_tx` settles our attempts (`Exec::on_wallet_tx`); the record gains `prog_events` (the route program's events decoded from its logs, `proglog::events`: `[{kind: leg | out, leg, amount, code}]`) and `cu_consumed`. INPUT 5 `inflight_release` is written for every block-engine result of our bundles (the Jito client's sink): `{src: "jito", sig, bundle, attempt, outcome, slot, index, validator, seq, t_ns, release (a landing: `processed` or `finalized`), code (a would-fail's)}`; a landing releases the attempt's in-flight entry at once; any other result (a would-fail, a lost auction, a drop, a rejection) leaves it for its hold (WP-05 review #2: the attempt is final at its landing or its blockhash's expiry, PLAN WP-07). Would-fails in a row cool the token down, an attempt's first would-fail counting once (`cool_repeat` counts the others); an accepted or landed bundle clears the run. INPUT 9 `settled` is an executor task's result: `{what: "fired", attempt, fired (send::Fired)}`, `{what: "statuses", why (`check`, `expiry`), at: [[attempt, label]], result (getSignatureStatuses' answers, or `{error}`), t_ns}` or `{what: "dry_sim", attempt, line}`; replay still skips it (WP-09).
- **As WP-09 writes it** (the executor replay; review WP-09 "For the WP-05 merge" items 1-7; same framing, no version change: a reader that does not know INPUT 12 skips it). **exec_init** (INPUT 12, `t_engine_ns` 0, in the boot section right after engine_init; written by `Engine::journal_exec_init` when the run has an executor): what the executor starts with that the frozen config does not hold, `exec::ExecInit`: `{mode (shadow, dry_run, live, canary), alts: [{table, addrs: [pk]}] (the `[exec] alts` lookup tables as the boot read them, in order; a table not found is left out), pump (PumpSwap's GlobalConfig as decoded: {disable_flags, protocol, reserved, buyback}, or null: PumpSwap legs do not build), held_mints (the tokens the wallet held an account for at boot, sorted), max_size_lamports, max_size_src (live, canary), bankroll_cap_lamports (null: none before WP-06 / WP-07; a size cap is the smaller of the two), would_send_per_sec (the bundle path's rate: a would-send's rate model), tips: {path, accounts, floor, share, max} (the bundle path's tip policy; its rotation starts at the first account), crowding: {window_h, buckets, static_list: [pk]} (the activity map's window and buckets and WP-00's crowded-pools list as loaded at the start), cfg_sha256 (sha256 of the executor's config sections: `[exec]`, `[[send]]`, `[send_policy]`, `[live]` without its failed-fee cap, `[canary]`, `[sim]`, `[dry_run]`, the wallet, `cu_price_micro`)}`. INPUT 3's decision-point record is now also written when the gate's **reason** changes while its mode stays (a pause, then the lag SLO's shadow over it: every refusal's `gate_shadow:<reason>` changed), and carries the reason the check read. INPUT 9 `settled` is fed back to the executor (`exec::Settled::from_json`), and INPUT 7 `wallet_tx` and INPUT 5 `inflight_release` reach it as before; a replay stages the decision-point records ahead of the record whose handling made the decision for the executor too (`Exec::stage_seen`: the last record at or before the opportunity holds). No real clock reaches the executor's state: the simulation and dry-run simulation windows, the big-band refund, a wallet landing's `t_wallet_ns` and the expiry without a slot run on the engine's time; the process-wide PoH index (shred walks, not journaled) is read only in a run, for a gauge and outcome lines. `Engine::digest` folds the executor's state (`Exec::digest`: totals, counters, the in-flight map, the big-band bucket, cooling, the would-send and simulation windows, open and final attempts, held tokens, the tip rotation) and the crowding map's (`Activity::digest`) when the engine has an executor; an engine without one digests what it did before.
- **As WP-07 writes it** (same framing, no version change: a reader that does not know INPUT 13 skips it). INPUT 13 `snapshot` carries the ledger's RPC answers: a balance snapshot `{what: "snapshot", why, keys: [pk], answers: [{slot, values: [{lamports, owner, data: [base64, "base64"]} | null]}], slot_mismatch, attempts, provider, ms, error, t_req_ns}` (the accounts' raw answers at their context slots) or a reconciliation's window search `{what: "sigs", to, result: [{signature, slot, err}], error}`. INPUT 7 `wallet_tx` carries `ledger` (`ledger::Raw`: `{kind, ok, payer, wallet_sol, wsol, tokens {mint: amt}, fee, tip, rent_locked, rent_reclaimed, rent_nonrecoverable, created, closed, token_accounts}`, computed by the chain-fetch service from the full transaction). INPUT 3's decision-point record carries `money`, the ledger's view the executor decided with (`ledger::MoneyView`: `{equity_known, liquid, wsol, native, rent_recoverable, stale}`: the bankroll cap, the rent cap, `equity_unknown` and `equity_stale` read it), and is written whenever that view differs from the last record. A replay has no ledger: its offline executor applies each record's `money` (`Exec::stage_seen`), so the sizes the bankroll capped, `bankroll_empty` and the rent cap replay as the run decided them; the ledger's own lines (ledger.jsonl, snapshots.jsonl, recon.jsonl) are the run's and are not re-derived by the replay. The stop's drain (§4.3) journals the inputs it handles and its housekeeping as TIMER (`TIMER_HK`).

- **WP-06** (same framing; a reader that does not know the new fields ignores them). **engine_init** adds `builder`, the fee and CU model the engine prices with (`engine::BuilderModel`): `{tips: {bands: {edges}, shares, contested_shares, floor, cap} ([tips], [bands]), cu: {table (data/cu-table.json as the run read it, §4.48; null: none), min_samples ([builder] cu_min_samples)}}`; null in a recording made before WP-06, whose replay prices with the rule it was made with (5,000 + `cu_price_micro` × CU / 10^6 + `min_tip`); a `builder` present but unreadable (another schema, a corrupt record) makes the record unreadable, and the replay refuses (review WP-06 #10), never the old rule in its place. A replay reads neither file: the model is the record's. **exec_init** adds `version` (2: WP-06's bundle layout and tip policy) and `uva` (whether the boot read found the wallet's PumpSwap user volume accumulator; null: not read), and `tips` becomes `{path, accounts (in rotation order), model (as engine_init's builder.tips)}`. `held_mints` stays the boot read (the executor's view where the state stream does not follow the wallet) and `bankroll_cap_lamports` stays null (gone at the merge of WP-06, below). A recording whose exec_init is not version 2 is not executor-replayed (replay.json `executor.exec_init.why_not`, `executor_not_replayed`, §4.50): this build does not rebuild its bundles. Where the state stream follows the wallet, whether it holds a token's account and whether its accumulator exists are the store's, from journaled ACCOUNT records (§4.12; the bankroll is WP-07's money view, below), and the lookup tables the size budget adds are the engine's cache, indexed by the registry pools their entries name (ALT records, journaled; §4.37): a replay builds the same bundles (test `s10_the_held_view_follows_the_wallet_on_the_stream_and_replays`). `Exec::digest` folds `uva`.
- **At the merge of WP-06** (onto integ/stage0, 2026-10-06; `arbx.journal/2.4`, same framing). 2.3's records (WP-07's INPUT 13 and decision-point `money`, the FT-000 fixes' ALT `found` 4) with WP-06's engine_init `builder` and exec_init version 2: a 2.3 journal (integ/stage0, the FT-000 lane) has neither (its engine_init replays with the old fee rule, its exec_init, version 1, is not executor-replayed), and a 2.4 journal is the one shape of both lanes' records. exec_init drops `bankroll_cap_lamports` (null wherever WP-06 wrote it; `exec::ExecInit::from_json` refuses a record with a value: this build caps on no static bankroll): the bankroll has one source, WP-07's money view, which every decision-point record carries (`money`) and a replay's offline executor applies (`Exec::stage_seen`), on `[live] bankroll_share` / `bankroll_reserve_sol` (in `cfg_sha256`, which now also covers WP-06's `[builder]`, `[tips]`, `[bands]` and WP-07's `[supervisor] rent_cap_sol`). The same money view decides WP-07's rent cap on held-set additions (`Exec::held_growth_ok`), so a replay applies it as the run did.
- **WP-09 part 2** (a live or canary executor replayed; `arbx.journal/2.4`, same framing). A decision the executor **sent** (live, canary) journals, right after it (after the fire, at the decision's `t_engine_ns`), an INPUT 3 decision-point record of its own: `{src: "decision", opp, executor: true, sent: {attempt, sig}}` (the attempt and its transaction's signature). A replay stages it with the other decision-point records (`Exec::stage_seen`), and its offline executor replays that send as sent with the run's signature (`take_sent`): the fire's result (INPUT 9), the block engine's verdicts (INPUT 5), the statuses (INPUT 9) and the wallet's record (INPUT 7) then find their attempt as in the run. A record staged at another opportunity, or one no send of the replay took, counts as `decision_records_mismatch`; a send replayed without one counts as `sent_records_missing` (§4.50). **exec_init** adds `money_guard` (the run had a ledger: live and canary send nothing before the start equity, WP-07; false in a record without it) and `cap_failed_fees_lamports` (the failed-fee cap the executor stopped at: `[live] max_failed_fees_lamports` lowered by `--budget-left`, which the frozen config does not show, or the canary's `max_spend_lamports`; a record without it: the config's); the offline executor applies both. A shadow or dry-run run writes no `sent` record.
- **As the FT-001 fix writes it** (same framing, no version change: a reader that does not know INPUT 14 skips it). INPUT 14 `digest_point`: the engine's own record of a digest point, journaled where it takes the digests, at the engine time of the last record it handled (`Engine::digest_point`, §4.50 "Digest points"): `run_end` by `finish` (the end of the run loop), `stop_end` at the end of the stop sequence (after the drain's records). Not an input: a replay digests there and does what the run did there (`finish`; the executor's `settle_at_stop`).

### 4.14 exec.jsonl (v2): every executed transaction touching a watched account

Producers, all through `src/rec/exec.rs`. Derived fields come from `src/classify.rs` and `src/proglog.rs` (WP-08).
- **The gRPC transaction stream** (`feeds/grpc.rs::feed_once`, yellowstone-tx). In Stage 0 its single filter is the PumpSwap program, as in arb-lab. It delivers every PumpSwap transaction, failed ones included, with its block index.
- **The backfiller** (`src/chainfetch/backfill.rs`, getTransaction). It covers watched transactions on the other venues, and failed watched transactions that the slot index shows landed.
- **The full-block coverage sampler** (`src/chainfetch/mod.rs`, `Service::on_sample`; it picks a block the slot index read).

One line is written per signature. If several sources deliver it, the first is written. When the slot index (§4.16b) later gives the block index of a record that has none, a line `{"sig": <sig>, "idx_update": <idx>, "src": "slotidx"}` is added, and readers merge it into the first line.

**Coverage denominator** (status `coverage`, report R0, Stage-0 gate):
- **D1:** every signature that wrote a watched account (the `txn` of `accounts.bin` records);
- **D2:** shred- or stream-seen signatures with a watched account among their keys that the slot index shows in a confirmed block. This includes failed ones, which write nothing.

Coverage = |records with full meta and `idx` within 10 minutes| / |D1 ∪ D2|.

The full-block sampler estimates the share of watched-touching transactions in a block that belong to neither D1 nor D2: `unseen_share_sample`, with its sample count. The gate requires coverage ≥ 99% and unseen share < 1% (PLAN §6).

Measured scale (PLAN §0): D1 ran at 31-60 signatures/s on the w5h registry, and 41-48% touched a PumpSwap pool. Without a second filter or provider (PLAN OD-1), the rest needs about 16-35 getTransaction/s, plus the failed ones.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| sig | sig | (v1) |
| slot | slot | (v1) |
| idx | idx / null | (v1) block index. Null for a backfilled record until the slot index supplies it (`idx_update`) |
| src | enum | `grpc-tx`, `rpc-backfill`, `rpc-wallet`, `rpc-block-sample` |
| found_by | [enum] | how the signature entered the denominator: `account_update`, `shred`, `tx_stream`, `ours`, `jito_processed`, `sigs_for_address`, `block_sample` |
| t | t_ns | (v1) receive time |
| ok, err | bool, str / null | (v1) `err` as `ix<i>:custom:0x<code>` / `ix<i>:ie<variant>` / `tx<variant>` (record.rs `tx_error`) |
| err_ix | integer / null | failing top-level instruction |
| why | str / null | (v1) last log line containing an error |
| fee | lam | (v1) total fee charged |
| cu | integer / null | (v1) compute units consumed |
| cost_units | integer / null | when the provider sends it |
| cu_limit, cu_price_micro | integer / null | from compute-budget instructions (null = default) |
| priority_fee | lam | `ceil(cu_limit_or_default × cu_price / 1e6)` |
| loaded_data_limit | integer / null | SetLoadedAccountsDataSizeLimit |
| version | enum | `legacy`, `0`, `1` |
| signer, signers | pk, [pk] | fee payer and all signers |
| recent_blockhash | str | base58 |
| nonce | pk / null | durable nonce account if instruction 0 is AdvanceNonce |
| keys | [pk] | static keys, then loaded writable, then loaded readonly (the full account list) |
| n_static | integer | how many of `keys` are static |
| alts | [{table, w: [u8], r: [u8]}] | lookup tables used |
| ixs | [{p, a: [u8], d}] | top-level instructions: program key index, account indices, data base64 |
| inner | [{ix, list: [{p, a, d, h}]}] | inner instructions per top-level index; `h` = stack height |
| logs | [str] / null | full log messages; null if the provider omitted them (`log_messages_none`) |
| return_data | {program, data} / null | |
| pre, post | [lam] | balances in `keys` order |
| pre_tok, post_tok | [{i, mint, owner, prog, amount: amt, dec}] | token balances |
| signer_sol | lam | (v1 `sol`) signer's lamport change |
| signer_tok | {mint: amt} | (v1 `tok`) signer-owned token changes, nonzero only |
| watched | [{acct, role, pool}] | watched accounts this tx wrote |
| venues | [str] | venue programs invoked (top-level or inner) |
| pools | [pk] | watched pools it touched |
| transfers | [{kind, from, to, lamports, mint, amount, sender, depth, ix}] | WP-08 classification; `kind` in A.4; `sender` = tip service; `depth` 0 top-level |
| tip_total | lam | sender_tip + cpi_tip paid (0 when `ok` is false; WP-08) |
| tip_asked | lam | (WP-08) sender_tip + cpi_tip asked for, whatever the outcome |
| tip_senders | [str] | |
| jito_tipped | bool | pays any Jito tip account |
| bundle | {known, src (`jito-result`, `explorer`, `heuristic`), id, pos, size} / null | |
| arb_like | {is, why (`venues`, `pools`, `known`, `program`)} | same test as `record_rival` |
| ours | {attempt} / null | our transaction |
| prog_events | [{kind, leg, amount, code}] / null | our program's decoded logs (only for our txs) |

**As WP-03 writes it** (`src/rec/exec.rs::exec_json`, built and serialized on the chain writer). Producer: the transaction streams (`[[state.tx_stream]]`, Stage 0: PumpSwap), `src` `grpc-tx`, `found_by` `["tx_stream"]`, `idx` from `TransactionInfo.index`. The backfilled and wallet records are WP-03b's (same builder). `cu_limit`, `cu_price_micro` and `loaded_data_limit` come from ComputeBudget instructions (legacy, v0) or the v1 transaction's `config` (then `cu_price_micro` is null and `priority_fee` is its `priority_fee`); `priority_fee` = ceil(limit × price / 10⁶) with the default limit min(1.4M, 200,000 × non-ComputeBudget instructions). `inner` and `logs` are null when the provider says it omitted them (`*_none`), `return_data` null when absent. `signer_sol` is null when the balances are missing. `watched` lists the watched accounts (the run's role table, §4.12) that are writable in the transaction; for a failed transaction only the fee payer can be among them (role names as accounts.bin's, `pool` the pool index). `venues` names venue programs invoked top-level or inner (the rival list's 13 programs). `transfers`, `tip_total`, `tip_senders`, `jito_tipped`, `bundle`, `arb_like` (WP-08) and `ours`, `prog_events` (WP-05 / WP-08) are null. The schema is `arbx.exec/2.0`; arb-lab's `sol` / `tok` are `signer_sol` / `signer_tok`. Review WP-03 fixes: one line per **(signature, slot)** from a transaction stream (`rec::exec::Recent`, kept across reconnects, the latest 200,000 pairs): a re-delivery (reconnect) is dropped, a transaction executed on two forks keeps both lines and the reader keeps the canonical fork's; `bank_id` (the transaction's bank when the provider sends `SubscribeUpdateTransaction.bank_id`, else null); a queued record counts against the chain queue's byte cap at its protobuf size.

**As WP-03b writes it** (`src/chainfetch/`: the RPC transaction converted to the stream's protobuf shapes, `chainfetch::txjson`, then `exec_json`, built on the chain writer). Three more producers: the backfill (`src` `rpc-backfill`): a `getTransaction` (`encoding` base64, `maxSupportedTransactionVersion` `[chainfetch] max_tx_version`, 1: mainnet blocks carry v1 transactions, 181 of the 1,193 of slot 453,032,285, and with 0 a provider refuses the whole block, -32015; `commitment` confirmed) of every D1 signature, and of every D2 signature the slot index found in its block, that has no record `after_ms` (1,500) after it was first seen (the first fetch waits, at most 20 s from first seen, until the signature's slot is confirmed: a `confirmed` getTransaction finds nothing before; status `deferred_delta`), at the rate its providers have spare after every other role (status `cap_rps_now`), newest first, and given up 10 minutes after it was first seen (`expired_delta`, `gave_up_delta`); a transaction stream record the recorder did not take (its queue full) does not cover its signature, which is backfilled like the others; our wallet's fetch (`rpc-wallet`, §4.15) for the wallet transactions that wrote the wallet or a D1 account; the coverage sampler (`rpc-block-sample`; `chainfetch::Service::on_sample`), for every watched transaction of the sampled block without a record (its `idx` the position in the block). `found_by` lists every source that had named the signature when the record was written (`account_update`, `shred`, `tx_stream`, `ours`, `jito_processed`, `sigs_for_address`, `block_sample`). `idx` is `getTransaction`'s `transactionIndex` when the provider sends it (Agave 4 does: the wallet transactions of slot 453,032,285 sit at their `transactionIndex` in the block), else null until the slot index supplies it (`{"sig", "idx_update", "src": "slotidx"}`). `t` is when the reply arrived; `bank_id` is null; `block_time` (`getTransaction`'s `blockTime`; null in a sampled block) is added. A signature gets its line from the first source that records it; a stream record arriving after another source's line is counted (status `chainfetch.dup_records_delta`). A reply without meta, or whose signature is not the one asked, is not recorded (txfetch `no_meta`, `signature_mismatch`).

**As WP-08 writes it** (`src/classify.rs`; schema `arbx.exec/2.1`). The derived fields are filled on the chain writer by the hook `rec::exec::derive` (`classify::annotate`) from the record's own fields (`keys`, `ixs`, `inner`, `pre`, `pre_tok` / `post_tok`, `venues`, `pools`), so any §4.14 record classifies alike whatever produced it; the chain-fetch service (WP-03b) makes the same one call (`rec::exec::derive(&mut v)` after `exec_json`, in `chainfetch::Service::emit_tx`, since the merge of the two), so wallet.jsonl and the backfilled, wallet and sampled exec.jsonl records carry them too. A record whose keys or instructions do not decode keeps them null.
- `transfers`, in execution order (each top-level instruction, then its inner instructions): every System Transfer, TransferWithSeed, WithdrawNonceAccount, CreateAccount and CreateAccountWithSeed (`lamports`), every SPL Token / Token-2022 Transfer and TransferChecked (`mint`, `amount` as a decimal string) and every CloseAccount (`lamports`, `mint`). `kind`, the first rule that matches: a System transfer to a tip account is `sender_tip` at the top level and `cpi_tip` from inside a program (any sender, the signer or a program's vault); a System transfer inside an Associated Token Account instruction, and every CreateAccount, is `rent_create`; a System transfer into the signer's WSOL account is `wrap` when a SyncNative of that account follows, else `self`; a transfer to a platform wallet (`data/platform-fee-wallets.json`, compiled in; none is sourced yet, so this kind does not occur) or to a token account one owns is `platform_fee` (`sender` = the platform); a token transfer made by a PumpSwap buy or sell (the instruction directly above it) to its protocol fee token account, coin-creator vault token account or buyback token account (accounts 10, 17 and the last, as build.rs fills them) is `venue_fee`; a transfer between the signer and accounts it owns (token accounts by their owner in the token balances, or as an InitializeAccount / ATA instruction of the transaction set them) is `self`; a CloseAccount of the signer's WSOL account is `unwrap`, of any other token account `rent_close`; the rest (swap legs, pool payments, refunds) is `other`. A close's `lamports` is the account's balance when it closes: its pre-balance plus what the instructions before it moved in or out (System transfers and creations, WSOL token transfers); null when the record has no pre-balances. `depth` is 0 at the top level, else the stack height minus 1 (1 when the provider gives no height); `ix` the top-level instruction; `from` / `to` are token accounts for token transfers. Accounts created and closed in the same transaction are in no token balance: their mint and owner come from the transaction's InitializeAccount{,2,3} or ATA Create instructions. A failed transaction (`ok` false) moved nothing: its transfers are the ones its instructions asked for, and the inner ones the provider recorded before the error (they name the lane it used: WP-19); with `inner` null only the top-level ones are known.
- The tip map: `send::tip_owner_names`, the same names rivals.jsonl's `tips` carry (§4.21): the code's sender lists first (`known_tip_accounts`, `OTHER_TIP_ACCOUNTS`), then the catalogue `data/sender-tip-accounts.json` (compiled in, `send::TIP_CATALOGUE`; a service's `sender`, else its key, and every service that adds an account of its own must have a `sender`) for every account those do not name: 46 more on 2026-10-04 (Glaive, Soyas, AllenHark, Solami, the bloXroute and SVS Lightspeed legacy accounts), each with its provider source (`tip_account_sources`: `source_url`, `fetched`, `attribution`). On cmp2's rivals this names Glaive's 4,197 rows, Soyas' 458, bloXroute's 72 and Solami's 16; about 33,800 rows over about 190 accounts stay unnamed (the largest AgmLJBMD... with 4,671 rows, then the eight FAST... accounts with 4,158; `testdata/classify/cmp2-rival-tips.json`).
- `tip_total`: the `sender_tip` and `cpi_tip` lamports **paid**: those the instructions ask for when `ok`, 0 for a failed transaction (it pays only its fee; review WP-08 #5); `tip_asked`: the lamports asked for, whatever the outcome (`tip_total` = `tip_asked` when `ok`). `tip_senders`: the services those tips go to, first tipped first; `jito_tipped`: one of them is one of Jito's eight tip accounts. Both describe the lane the transaction asked for, so they are set for failed transactions too.
- `bundle`: `{known: true, src: "jito-result", id, pos: 0, size: 1, slot, index}` for our transaction once the block engine's `processed` result named it (`classify::note_bundle_processed(sig, bundle, slot, index)`, called by WP-05 in send/jito.rs `on_result`; a line written before the result arrives stays heuristic); `{known: false, src: "heuristic", id: null, pos: null, size: null}` for any other successful Jito-tipped transaction (a bundle candidate); null otherwise, and always for a failed transaction (a Jito bundle lands only when all of it succeeds). Block adjacency and the explorer lookup (`explorer`) are the toolkit's (WP-10): a line is written once, before its neighbours in the block are all known.
- `arb_like`: `{is, why}` with `why` `venues` (two venue programs besides the aggregator), `pools` (two watched pools), `known` (a signer seen doing either before) or `program` (a watched pool touched by a top-level program seen at the top of such a transaction before); the learned signers and programs are process-wide and bounded (200,000 each), as the engine's rival detection.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.exec/2.2`).
- **Sampling** (S1-5): a transaction-stream record is written in full when the transaction touches a watched account: it reads or writes a pool, vault, DLMM bin array or bitmap extension, our wallet or its token accounts (the chain fetch's D2 roles), or writes any watched account (a failed transaction writes only its fee payer) (`rec::exec::touches_watched`). Of the others, 1 in `[record] exec_unwatched_sample` (20) is written, chosen by the signature (`rec::sample::keep_1_in`, as rivals.jsonl's), with `sample: {one_in, kept, seen}` (the population's running counts; manifest.json `sampling.exec_unwatched` the totals). Every record has `sample`, null when kept in full (the chain-fetch service's records too). A record left out does not cover its signature (none of them is in the coverage denominator), and its idx is still checked against the slot index. In FT-000's `exec.055`, 10,222 of 13,290 records (71.5% of the bytes) touched no watched account. Readers: R9 leaves the sampled records out (its population is the transactions touching watched accounts), the CU table takes them as they are.
- **`idx` is always a block index** (S1-2): the stream's `TransactionInfo.index` (checked equal to the public RPC's getBlock position, 1,081 of 1,081, slots 453,433,382..387), getTransaction's `transactionIndex` (Shyft sends it: 2 of 2 equal), or a full block's position when the block's list holds vote transactions. `idx_update` lines come only from slot-index lines whose positions are block indexes (`pos_kind` `block_index`, §4.16b); before, a rank among the non-votes was written as `idx` (FT-000). A sampled block (`rpc-block-sample`) without a single vote transaction left the votes out: its records keep `idx` null and carry `block_rank` (the position in that list) and `pos_kind: "nonvote_rank"`.

### 4.15 wallet.jsonl (new): our wallet's every landed transaction

Producer: `src/chainfetch/wallet.rs` through `src/rec/exec.rs` (WP-03b; F1). Revision 1 spent the single transaction filter on `@wallet`; it now stays on PumpSwap. Our signatures come from four sources, recorded in `found_by`:
- `account_update`: the `txn` of every wallet-account or wallet-token-account update. A landed transaction we pay for writes the wallet even when it fails, because the fee is charged;
- `ours`: attempts, at signing;
- `jito_processed`: Jito `processed` results;
- `sigs_for_address`: an hourly `getSignaturesForAddress`.

Each signature is fetched once with a bounded `getTransaction`. A signature found only by `sigs_for_address` raises `events` `wallet_update_missing`. Its block index comes from the slot index. The schema is the same as §4.14, plus:

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| attempt | integer / null | matched by signature |
| wallet_kind | enum | `route`, `wrap`, `unwrap`, `ata_create`, `close`, `nonce`, `transfer_in`, `transfer_out`, `other` |
| wallet_sol | lam | wallet lamport change |
| wallet_tok | {mint: amt} | changes of wallet-owned token accounts |
| created | [{acct, lamports, owner, space}] | accounts created and funded by the wallet in this tx |
| closed | [{acct, lamports, to}] | accounts closed in this tx |

**As WP-03b writes it** (`src/chainfetch/wallet.rs`; class core: a lost record closes the SendGate). Sources: (a) the state stream's updates of the wallet and its token accounts (the `feeds::forward` tap), (b) our attempts (the `Exec::send` fire task's tap, after each firing: the copies that left), (c) `chainfetch::note_jito_processed` (WP-05 wires it: a Jito result names its bundle, not the signature), (d) `getSignaturesForAddress(wallet, {limit: 1000, commitment: confirmed})`, paged back over the last hour, first 30 s after the start (once the state stream runs) and then every `sigs_every_s`. (a) and (d) show the signature landed, and it is fetched at once (an (a) also cuts a waiting retry short). A signature known only from (b) or (c) is watched: one `getSignatureStatuses([sig, ...], {searchTransactionHistory: false})` (role `wallet`, up to 256 signatures, txfetch reason `ours_status`, key the count) every 2 s while any is watched; it is fetched once a status shows it landed (failed or not), and counted `wallet_unlanded` after 90 s without one (most bundle attempts never land; fetching each blind cost about 22 getTransaction calls). Each landed signature is fetched once (a bounded call: 2 s, one more provider; at most 4 in flight), retried after 1 s, 5 s, then every 30 s while nothing is found, and given up 10 minutes after it was found (txfetch.jsonl). The wallet coverage (`wallet_with_tx_pct`) counts landed signatures only.
