The record is §4.14's (`src` `rpc-wallet`, `found_by` the sources then) plus `attempt` and `ours` ({attempt} / null: from (b)), `wallet_kind` (in this order: `route` a top-level route-program instruction; `nonce` a System nonce instruction other than AdvanceNonce; `wrap` SyncNative on the wallet's WSOL account; `unwrap` its CloseAccount; `close` the CloseAccount of another token account the wallet owns; `ata_create` an associated-token-account create for the wallet; `transfer_in` another payer, and the wallet's lamports rose; `transfer_out` a System transfer from the wallet; `other`), `wallet_sol` (null when the wallet is not among the keys), `wallet_tok` ({mint: amount} of the token accounts the wallet owns, non-zero only), `created` ([{acct, lamports (after the transaction), owner, space}] for accounts with no lamports before and some after, in a transaction the wallet pays; owner and space from the System create that made them, null when none did) and `closed` ([{acct, lamports (before), to (the CloseAccount's destination, null when none)}]) and `pre_run` (true for a transaction from before the state stream's first slot: (d) reads the last hour, so the previous segment may have recorded it too; WP-07 de-duplicates); checked against the legacy cases' known answers (testdata/legacy/signatures.json). `wallet_update_missing` (events.jsonl `{sig, slot, found_by}`) is raised for a signature found by (d), at or after the state stream's first update's slot, that no account update named within a minute. A record without idx gets `{"sig", "idx_update", "src": "slotidx"}` lines (here, and in exec.jsonl when it went there). Since the merge with WP-08 the record also carries §4.14's derived fields (`transfers`, `tip_total`, `tip_asked`, `tip_senders`, `jito_tipped`, `bundle`, `arb_like`), filled on the writer by the hook the transaction stream's records use.

**As WP-05 writes it.** Source (b) is the executor's fire task (`chainfetch::note_fired`, after each firing: the copy that left, the route's pools and the attempt's target slot). An attempt's signatures are watched until the chain is `WATCH_SLOTS_AFTER_TARGET` (16) slots past its target slot (four leader windows: a bundle lands in its leader's slots or not at all), within the 90 s; those given up so are counted apart (status `chainfetch.wallet_unlanded_by_slot_delta`). A later landing still comes by (a), and the executor checks once more when the blockhash expires (§4.40). Source (c) is wired: a `processed` result of our bundle names its signature (`chainfetch::note_jito_processed`) and the bundle to the transfer classification (`classify::note_bundle_processed`). The `wallet_tx` input (§4.13) carries `prog_events` and `cu_consumed`.

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.wallet/1.1`). `found_by` gains `executor_status`: a signature of ours that landed but whose record may not be fetched (the executor's expiry check found the landing first: its record settles the provisional money, §4.40; or the ledger's reconciliation found it in its window): fetched at once, and once more if its fetch had been given up. The `wallet_tx` input carries the ledger's raw fields (`ledger`, §4.13).

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.wallet/1.1`; S1-12). Source (d) takes only the rows of its hour (a row with a `blockTime` older than the listing's `since` is not fetched: FT-000's first page brought the wallet's 748 transactions back to slot 452,817,831, five days, fetched with 749 Shyft calls at every segment start). A segment starts after the previous one's cursor (`[chainfetch] wallet_cursor`, "auto": the newest `wallet-cursor.json` among the run directory's siblings, for this wallet; a path; "" none): the listing's `until` is the cursor's signature, so only newer signatures are listed and fetched. The cursor (`Wallet::cursor`) is the newest recorded signature (by slot) with no landed signature of an older or equal slot still waiting for its fetch; a signature still pending at the stop is newer than it, so the next segment lists it. `idx_update` lines (here and in exec.jsonl) come only from block-index slot-index lines (§4.16b).

**At the merge of the FT-000 fixes (integ/stage0)** (`arbx.wallet/1.2`). WP-07's 1.1 (`found_by` `executor_status`) and the FT-000 fixes' 1.1 (the hour's rows, the cursor, `idx` a block index only) were two shapes under one number: the merged stream is 1.2, with both. A 1.1 file comes from one lane's build (never run on Cherry).

**At the integration (integ/stage0): the cursor and the ledger.** In a campaign, the cursor a segment starts from never passes the window WP-07's ledger reconciles between segments (§4.44: from the previous segment's last good snapshot, or the first snapshot of its stop reconciliation left undecided): a later cursor is moved back to the newest earlier ledger entry at or before that snapshot, or not used when there is none (the last hour is listed, as without a cursor); run.log says which (`ledger::bound_wallet_cursor`, `History::window_cursor`). A record the previous segment wrote here but never entered in its ledger (at its end, or killed) is then listed again, and the window's residual sees it: a cursor past the window would have hidden one under the tolerance (a failed landing's 5,000-lamport fee) from the net spend. A manual run, or a campaign before its first good snapshot, uses the cursor as it is.

### 4.16 blocks.jsonl (new)

Producer: the `feeds/grpc.rs` blocks-meta subscription (`Event::BlockMeta`), only if `arbx grpc-probe` showed that the provider offers it (`[state] block_meta = "auto"`). One line per block. Without it, `slotidx.jsonl` carries the same slot-level fields at confirmed commitment.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| slot, parent_slot | slot | |
| blockhash, parent_blockhash | str | base58 |
| block_height | integer | blockhash validity = height + 150 |
| block_time | integer / null | unix seconds |
| executed_tx_count, entries_count | integer | |
| leader | pk / null | from the LeaderBook |
| t_recv | t_ns | |
| src | enum | `grpc` |

**As WP-03 writes it.** The state stream subscribes to `blocks_meta` when `[state] block_meta` is `on`, or `auto` and the probe file (`[state] probe_file`, default `<out_dir>/probe.json`) shows the state provider accepted it; the probe file is copied into the segment. Written by the state stream's feed task (`feeds/mod.rs::forward`); `leader` from the LeaderBook when it knows the slot. The engine keeps the newest block meta (`Engine::last_block_meta`) for WP-03b's blockhash ring, and journals each one. Review WP-03: `auto` needs the probe to have accepted it AND received at least one message (an inconclusive or silent result is off); `bank_id` (`SubscribeUpdateBlockMeta.bank_id`, null when 0 / not sent) is added; a block meta whose blockhash does not decode is dropped and counted in the state stream's feedhealth `errors_total`.

### 4.16b slotidx.jsonl (new): the block index of every confirmed slot

Producer: `src/chainfetch/slotidx.rs` (WP-03b; F1, F10). It makes one `getBlock(slot, {transactionDetails: "signatures", rewards: false, commitment: "confirmed", maxSupportedTransactionVersion})` per confirmed slot: about 3.7/s, as measured in w5h. The position of a signature in the returned list is its block index, which equals Yellowstone `TransactionInfo.index`. That equality is checked on every slot against the PumpSwap stream's `idx` (`idx_check`).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| slot, parent_slot | slot | |
| status | enum | `ok`, `skipped` (the RPC reports the slot skipped or not available), `error` |
| blockhash, previous_blockhash | str | base58; also fed into the blockhash ring (src `slotidx`) |
| block_height, block_time | integer / null | |
| tx_count | integer | all transactions in the block, votes included |
| tracked | [[sig, idx]] | positions of every signature we track: D1 and D2 candidates (§4.14), and ours |
| missing | [sig] | signatures stamped with this slot by an account update, but absent from the block: fork evidence (WP-04) |
| idx_check | {n, mismatches} | PumpSwap stream `idx` against this list |
| t_fetch_ns, latency_ms, provider | | |

The full signature list is not stored (about 3,000 signatures per block). Block-ordered route context per attempt is derived offline from this stream, `accounts.bin` and `exec.jsonl` (§4.41).

**As WP-03b writes it** (`src/chainfetch/slotidx.rs`). The slots are the state stream's confirmed statuses (the `feeds::forward` tap): each is fetched once confirmed (`getBlock`, `transactionDetails` signatures, `encoding` json, `maxSupportedTransactionVersion` 1; oldest first, at most 4 in flight). A gap after the last confirmed slot is filled: the slots after the new slot's parent were skipped on this fork and get their line without an RPC call (`by: "parent"`); the others are fetched (a confirmation that was missed, or a slot the RPC reports skipped, -32007 / -32009: `status: "skipped"`, `by: "rpc"`, `rpc_code`); a gap of more than 2,000 slots is dropped and counted. A block not available yet (-32004, -32014, or a null result) is asked again after 0.4 s doubling, at most 12 times; other failures after 1 s doubling, at most 6 times; then `status: "error"` with `err` and `detail`; a call refused for want of a token (nothing sent, no txfetch line) is asked again 250 ms later without using a try (`slotidx_delta.refused`). Its calls go before the backfill's in every bucket they share (priority index, §4.10), so the backfill cannot starve it. A slot more than 3,000 behind the newest confirmed one is dropped (counted: status `chainfetch.slotidx_delta.dropped` and `coverage.slotidx_pct`). `tracked` lists, sorted by idx, the D1 signatures (account updates stamped with the slot), the D2 candidates (unexecuted transactions the engine resolved to a watched pool, vault, bin array, bitmap extension, or the wallet and its token accounts) and ours found in the block; `missing` the D1 ones it lacks (a skipped slot: all of them); `idx_check` is `{n, mismatches, absent}` over the transaction stream's records stamped with the slot (`absent`: not in the block). D2 candidates found join the coverage denominator (and the backfill); those absent are forgotten (`d2_absent_delta`), and so are those of a slot whose block could not be read (`d2_unknown_delta`: nothing is known of them); D1 signatures in `missing` are fetched no more and leave the denominator. An ok block's blockhash and height go to the ring (src `slotidx`, confirmed). The getBlock of slot 453,032,285 (testdata/rpc) lists 1,193 signatures, our two wallet transactions of the slot at their `transactionIndex` 29 and 154.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.slotidx/1.1`; S1-2). FT-000's comparisons disagreed in 73% of 394,225 cases, whole slots at a time, following the leader's client. The cause, checked on the public RPC: Shyft's getBlock with `transactionDetails: "signatures"` leaves the vote transactions out. Its list is the block's non-vote transactions in block order (slots 453,426,744 / 745 / 747: 1,126 / 960 / 1,265 transactions, Shyft listed 461 / 331 / 623, exactly the non-votes; all 59 tracked positions equal the non-vote rank), so a position is a rank among the non-votes, equal to the block index only up to the first vote: slots whose leader puts every vote after the non-votes matched (453,426,743: 583 non-votes, then 667 votes). The stream's `idx` and getTransaction's `transactionIndex` are block indexes (§4.14). `tx_count` is the provider's list length (the non-votes for such a provider). New fields: `idx_check.below` / `.above` (compared stream transactions the list puts before / after their index); `pos_kind`: `nonvote_rank` when the list leaves transactions out (a compared stream transaction sits at an index past the list's end, or at least two comparisons and half of them are listed below their index and none above; or the same provider showed it before: kept per provider), `block_index` when it does not and at least 90% of the comparisons match (a stray mismatch is a stream copy from another fork), `unverified` otherwise (no comparison, or mismatches either way); `votes_omitted` (true, or null). A rank keeps the block order among non-vote transactions, which is what attribution compares within a slot, but it is never written as an `idx` (§4.14). Status: `chainfetch.slotidx_pos_kind_delta`, `slotidx_rank_only_delta`, `slotidx_votes_omitted_by_now`.

### 4.17 slots.jsonl (new)

Producer: `feeds/grpc.rs` slots subscription with all statuses (`interslot_updates = true`).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| slot | slot | |
| status | enum | `processed`, `confirmed`, `finalized`, `first_shred`, `completed`, `created_bank`, `dead` |
| parent | slot / null | |
| dead_error | str / null | |
| t | t_ns | |

**As WP-03 writes it.** `[state] slot_statuses = "all"` subscribes with interslot updates; `"commitment"` keeps arb-lab's three statuses; `"auto"` (the default, review WP-03 #5) is `all` where the probe file shows the state provider accepted `slots_interslot` with at least one message, else `commitment` (the interslot filter shares the state stream's one subscription with the accounts: a provider that refused it would refuse the whole stream). meta.json `state.slot_statuses` is the effective value. Written by the state stream's feed task; `status` names as above (`unknown` for a value outside 0-6); `bank_id` (`SubscribeUpdateSlot.bank_id`, null when not sent; review WP-03 #12) is added. The engine journals every status; only processed, confirmed and finalized move its slot gauges and the stream's feedhealth `slot_max_now` (a first-shred slot is ahead of every processed one) until WP-04's fork tree. `bank_id` is not yet in accounts.bin or the journal (WP-04 adds it with its fork handling). **As WP-04 writes it:** every status (interslot ones too) feeds the engine's fork tree (`src/forks.rs`): a confirmed or finalized slot abandons the slots after the last root that are not its ancestors (as far as parents are known), a `dead` slot abandons itself (only its own bank's versions when the status names a bank) and every slot built on an abandoned one is abandoned; an unconfirmed slot with an unconfirmed sibling is contested (`fork_pending`). The journal's SLOT records carry `bank_id` (2.2) and accounts.bin records theirs (2.1).

### 4.18 txfetch.jsonl (new)

Producers: `src/chainfetch/*` (WP-03b). The 400 ms `getLatestBlockhash` poll is not logged per call; it appears only in the per-role stats and status `chainfetch.blockhash.polls_delta` / `poll_skipped_delta` (it is skipped while another source gave a confirmed blockhash in the last second, at most 8 slots behind the tip: with the slot index running it costs nothing).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns | t_ns | completion time |
| role | enum | `wallet`, `backfill`, `slotidx`, `sample`, `settle`, `snapshot`, `leaders`, `alt`, `dlmm_arrays` |
| method | enum | `getTransaction`, `getBlock`, `getSignaturesForAddress`, `getSignatureStatuses`, `getMultipleAccounts`, … |
| key | str | signature, slot or account |
| reason | enum | `wallet_update`, `ours`, `sigs_for_address`, `move_without_tx`, `shred_seen_landed`, `attempt_route`, `slot_index`, `coverage_sample`, `expiry_check`, `catch_up` |
| queued_ns | t_ns | |
| provider | str | |
| ok | bool | |
| err | str / null | includes `unsupported_version`, `rate_limited`, `not_found`, `timeout` |
| latency_ms | ms | |
| bytes | integer | response size |

**As WP-03b writes it** (`chainfetch::Service::txfetch`). Roles and reasons: `wallet` (`getTransaction`: `wallet_update`, `ours`, `sigs_for_address`, `jito_processed`, the first source; `getSignaturesForAddress`, key the wallet, `sigs_for_address`), `backfill` (`getTransaction`: `move_without_tx` (D1), `shred_seen_landed` (D2), `attempt_route` (priority 0)), `slotidx` (`getBlock`, key the slot, `slot_index`), `sample` (`getBlock`, `coverage_sample`). Extra fields: `attempts` (requests made: providers and retries), `rpc_code` (a JSON-RPC error's code), `detail` (the scrubbed error message, at most 200 characters), `note` (`skipped`: the getBlock of a skipped slot is an answer, `ok` true; `gave_up`: the last attempt for a signature given up 10 minutes after it was found). A call refused for want of a token sent nothing and has no line (the registry counts it: resources.jsonl `rate_budget_delta`). The `wallet` role also has one line per `getSignatureStatuses` (reason `ours_status`, key the number of signatures asked). `err`: the class (`rate_limited`, `timeout`, `body`, `http`, `transport`, `rpc`, `no_result`, `rate_budget`, `no_provider`, `stopped`), `unsupported_version` (-32015), or, for an answer that is no record, `not_found` (null: not at confirmed yet), `no_meta`, `signature_mismatch`, `unparsable`. `queued_ns` is when the signature was queued (the backfill) or the request issued.

### 4.19 txarr.log (v1 format, kept)

Producer: `engine.rs::write_txarr` (:250-258), when a signature leaves the 10 s window. Text, one line per forwarded signature:
`<sig8> <slot> <first_ns> <d_0> <d_1> … <d_n>`, where `d_f` is feed `f`'s arrival in microseconds after `first_ns`, or `-` if the feed never delivered it. Feed order = `meta.feeds` ids. arbx adds the header line `# arbx.txarr/1.1 feeds=<name,name,…>`.

### 4.20 arrivals.jsonl (v1, +fields)

Producer: `engine.rs::write_arrivals` (:603-609), per decoded trigger, after its 10 s window.
Fields: (v1) `slot`, `pool`, `venue`, `source`, `first_feed`, `arrivals` {feed name: t_ns}; (new) `sig`, `t_first`.

### 4.21 rivals.jsonl (v1, +fields)

Producer: `engine.rs::record_rival` (:272-312), at shred time, for every arbitrage-like transaction (two or more venue programs, two or more of our pools, or a signer already seen doing that).
Fields (v1): `sig`, `slot`, `t`, `feed`, `why`, `signer`, `n_sig`, `v`, `cu_limit`, `cu_price`, `v1_fee`, `tips` [[account, lamports, sender name]], `nonce`, `progs`, `venues`, `agg`, `pools`, `alts`, `n_ix`, `n_keys`, `head` [data hex (48 bytes), data length, account count].
New: `loaded_data_limit`, `jito_tipped`, sender names from the full tip map (§4.14 `transfers`).
**As WP-08 writes it** (schema `arbx.rivals/1.2`; review WP-08 #1, #2):
- **Tip names from the full map.** `tips[][2]` comes from `send::tip_owner_names` (the engine's `tip_names`), which now takes the catalogue `data/sender-tip-accounts.json` after the code's lists: the same names as exec.jsonl's `transfers` (§4.14). On cmp2's rows that names Glaive's 4,197 tips, Soyas' 458, bloXroute's 72 and Solami's 16, which arb-lab's map left null.
- **Sampled `venues` rows** (plan addendum I-9). cmp2's rivals.jsonl had 849,585 rows in 28 minutes (1.29 GB/h raw, 238 MB/h at zstd-3); 744,411 (88%) were `why = venues`. `[record] rivals_venues_sample` (default 10; 1 keeps all) keeps 1 in N of the `venues` rows, chosen by the transaction's signature (`rec::sample::keep_1_in`: splitmix64 of the signature's first 8 bytes, little-endian, modulo N), so a replay, a re-run and another bot's recording of the same transactions keep the same rows. `pools` and `known` rows are all kept. The choice is made right after `why`, before the row is built; a left-out row's signer is still learned (its next transaction can be a `known` row). Each kept `venues` row carries `sample` `{one_in, kept, seen}`: the rate and the running counts of the segment's `venues` rows written and seen, this one included; `sample` is null on every other row and when N is 1. Readers weight a row by `sample.one_in` (1 when null); manifest.json `sampling.rivals_venues` holds the segment's totals (§4.4). At N = 10 cmp2's rows become 179,899 (74,725 `venues`, 10.04%): 266 MB/h raw, 54 MB/h at zstd-3 (§5). exec.jsonl's `arb_like` (§4.14) is not sampled: the toolkit's v2 rival report reads it.

### 4.22 moves.log (v2: columns appended)

Producer: `engine.rs::on_account` (:882-887), the first update of each (transaction, pool) pair.
`<sig> <slot> <pool> <t_ns> <acct> <write_version> <role>`. The first four columns are arb-lab's; a v1 reader ignores the rest. **As WP-02 writes it:** `write_version` is `-` (WP-03 carries it), `role` is `pool` or `vault`. **As WP-03 writes it:** `write_version` is the update's (`-` for an RPC read, which never names a transaction).

### 4.23 poh.log (v2: columns appended)

Producers: `feeds/mod.rs::forward` (entries feeds, :171-179) and `feeds/shred.rs::poh_advance` (shred feeds, :247-266). Always on (no longer tied to exec).
`<sig8> <slot> <ms> <feed> <entry_idx>`: `ms` = position on the leader's PoH clock since the slot began (hashes / hashes-per-tick × 6.25 ms); `entry_idx` = entry index within the slot when known, else `-`. **As WP-02 writes it:** always on; entries feeds write `-` for `entry_idx` (they see one message's entries, not the slot's index); shred feeds write the slot's entry index, one record per finished data set (several lines). The same signature can appear once per feed; readers keep the line from the feed with full slot coverage (`slots-feed` `poh_complete`), else the earliest. **As WP-08 writes it:** entries feeds write the entry's index in its slot when the feed followed the slot from its first entry (the same condition as the position itself: entries arrive in order, so the count so far is the index), `-` otherwise; shred feeds as before. The positions of forwarded transactions (any feed; a shred feed's only once its walk reaches them, after the hand-over) are also kept in the process's PoH index (`feeds::poh_index`, by `sig8`, earliest kept, 3 minutes), which settlement and the `poh_trigger_pct` gauge read: the engine's own map gets only the entries feeds' positions (they ride on the arrival).

### 4.24 slots-feed.log (v2; arb-lab `slots.log`)

Producers: `feeds/mod.rs::forward` (entries feeds) and `feeds/shred.rs` (shred feeds), when a feed finishes a slot.
`<feed> <slot> <ticks> <entries> <txs> <t_first> <t_last> <valid> <data_shreds> <code_shreds> <sets_ok> <sets_failed> <poh_complete>`. The first eight columns are v1 (entries feeds; `valid` = 0 when the feed joined the slot midway). Shred feeds fill all columns; entries feeds write `-` in the last five except `poh_complete`. **As WP-02 writes it:** entries feeds only (shred feeds with WP-08); `poh_complete` = 1 when the slot was followed from its start (`valid`) with all 64 ticks. **As WP-08 writes it:** shred feeds fill every column, one line per slot, written when the PoH walk reaches the slot's last shred (LAST_SHRED_IN_SLOT) or, short of that, when the assembler drops the slot (20 s after its first shred): `ticks`, `entries` and `txs` as walked in order from the slot's first entry (votes included in `txs`), `t_first` / `t_last` the first and last shred's kernel time, `valid` = the walk started at the slot's first data set, `data_shreds` / `code_shreds` received (a duplicate data shred is not counted; code shreds are counted as they come), `sets_ok` / `sets_failed` data sets decoded / unparseable, `poh_complete` = the walk reached the last shred with 64 ticks. A slot that received only code shreds writes no line.

### 4.25 entries-<feed>.bin (v1 frame format, per feed)

Producer: `rec::archive` from `feeds/mod.rs::forward` (:159). One file series per archived feed (`[record] archive_feeds`). Frame: `u64 slot | u64 t_ns | u32 len | len bytes` (the raw entries message), after a header `magic "ARBXENT\0" | u16 1 | u16 hlen | JSON`. Bulk class: first to stop under disk pressure.

### 4.26 alts.jsonl (v2: events) and alts-final.jsonl

Producer: `engine.rs::learn_alts`, `fetch_alt`, `on_alt`, `process` (lookup-table resolution).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns | t_ns | |
| table | pk | |
| event | enum | `learned_feed` (entries learned from a feed that sends loaded addresses), `fetch_start`, `fetch_ok`, `fetch_missing`, `fetch_error`, `retry_scheduled`, `give_up`, `waiters_released`, `waiters_dropped` |
| n_addrs | integer / null | entries known after the event |
| attempt | integer | fetch attempt number |
| next_retry_ms | integer / null | |
| waiting_txs | integer | transactions waiting on this table |
| err | str / null | |

`alts-final.jsonl` (written at stop): `{table, addrs: [pk or null]}` per known table, the v1 `alts.jsonl` content.

**As WP-04 writes it** (`src/alts.rs`, PLAN WP-04 item 7, PLAN-ADDENDUM I-6; `arbx.alts/2.0`). `t_ns` is the engine time of the input or timer that caused the event; events: `fetch_start` (`attempt` 1, 2, ...), `fetch_ok`, `fetch_missing` (no such account yet), `fetch_error` (`err`: the RPC error, scrubbed), `retry_scheduled` (`next_retry_ms`: `[alts] retry_ms` 50, 250, 1,000, 5,000 after the 1st..4th failure, then `retry_every_s` 30 s), `give_up` (`give_up_s` 600 s after the first failure; the table's waiting transactions are dropped, and later ones needing it too), `waiters_released`, `waiters_dropped` (`err` `wait_cap` or `give_up`), `learned_feed` (entries learned from a copy carrying loaded addresses; a table being retried is known again) and `preloaded` (from the previous segment's alts-final at boot: `[alts] preload`, "auto" = the newest sibling segment's). A failed fetch is never cached as missing for the run; at most `max_inflight` (16) fetches run at once (the rest queue, `fetching_now` in status); transactions wait for their tables (at most `wait_cap`, 10,000, the oldest dropped). Counters: `alt_wait`, `alt_retry`, `alt_fetch_failed`, `alt_gave_up`, `alt_wait_dropped`, `alt_learned`, `alt_preloaded`. **WP-04 review:** `alts-final.jsonl` lines add `last_used_ns` (the engine time a transaction last used the table: its addresses resolved one; a preloaded table keeps the previous segment's value; null: never), and the preload takes only the tables used within `[alts] preload_max_age_h` (24) of the segment's start, at most `preload_max_tables` (5,000), the most recently used first (a line without `last_used_ns`, from an older file, counts as used when its file was written): the preloaded set, and the journal's boot section that holds it, no longer grow across a campaign.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.alts/2.1`, `arbx.alts-final/1.2`; S1-1, S1-4). Fetches are batched (`[alts] batch_max`, `calls_inflight`, role `alt`, §4.10): `max_inflight` (now 400) counts tables. FT-000's engine asked for one table a request (11,063 requests, 76% of the 14,480 the run made) while the chain fetch starved. The cache is bounded: tables are held at their own length (not 256 slots each), and past `[alts] max_tables` (20,000) tables or `max_mb` (192) MiB (33 bytes a slot, 64 a table) the least recently used are evicted down to 90% of the cap (the LRU clock: the latest of a transaction's use and the table's fetch or learning; 0 for a preloaded table no transaction of the segment has used yet, so such tables go first, by address: the journal's preload record does not carry the previous segment's times, and a replay must order the cache as the run did; never a table being fetched, queued, retried or waited for, nor one the input being handled touched; `preload_max_tables` must be at most `max_tables`). New events: `evicted` (`n_addrs`, `idle_ms`: how long since its LRU clock, null for a preloaded table no transaction used); `fetch_start` carries `refetch: true` for the first fetch of a table evicted before (every line has `idle_ms` and `refetch`). Counters `alt_evicted`, `alt_refetch`. Each eviction is journaled (journal 2.3, §4.13). alts-final.jsonl lists each table at its own length (an older file's 256-slot lists still load; their trailing gaps are dropped at the preload). FT-000's cache reached 19,143 tables after 30 minutes (162.9 MB at 256 slots, 103.6 MB at their own length by the caps' accounting: 162 slots a table on average; 20,000 tables of that mix are 108 MB), growing 350 a minute (about 300 a minute in segments 2 and 3, which preloaded 5,000 tables each and held 26,673 and 25,478 at their ends); its working set: 2,628 tables used in the last minute, 7,359 in five, 11,072 in ten.

### 4.27 leaders.jsonl (new)

Producer: `src/leaders.rs`, when a 400-slot window of the schedule is fetched, plus kind updates (WP-08; WP-03 writes none: a list refresh that changes a validator's kind is an `events.jsonl` `leaders_refresh` line, and the slots learned after it carry the new kind).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| slot | slot | |
| leader | pk | validator identity |
| kind | enum | `jito`, `bam`, `other`, `unknown` |
| kind_src | str | which source: the Jito validators API host (its `running_bam` / `running_jito` give BAM and Jito), `bundle_result` (validator identity seen in our Accepted/Processed results: `jito`, since a bundle result says the leader ran the Jito block engine, not BAM; WP-03 deviation 6), a public BAM list if one is identified, the static file and its date (F39), or `gossip_client` (WP-08: the client it announces). For `unknown` (WP-08), why: `no_source` (no source names the leader), `no_schedule` (attempts only: the slot is outside the schedule held) |
| kind_age_s | integer | age of that list |
| region | str / null | the region of the leader's gossip IP from the offline geo table: its metro (WP-08), else its city |
| city | str / null | (WP-08) `<country>-<city>` of the gossip IP |
| tpu_quic | str / null | (WP-08) the TPU-QUIC endpoint the leader announces (`ip:port`) |
| tpu_region | str / null | (WP-08) that endpoint's region: for BAM and Harmonic validators a shared ingress node, often in another city or country than `region` |
| tpu_shared_by | integer / null | (WP-08) how many cluster nodes announce that endpoint |
| rtt_ms | ms / null | latest QUIC-handshake RTT from our host to the TPU-QUIC endpoint the leader announces (`tpu_quic`; kept per endpoint, WP-08). This is **not** the bundle's path, which runs block engine → leader (F40); the block-engine RTT is `attempts.leader.be_rtt_ms` |
| rtt_age_s | integer / null | |
| rtt_src | enum / null | `quic_handshake`, `region_bucket` (fallback when handshakes are refused: the median of the fresh endpoints in `tpu_region`, or in `region` without an endpoint) |
| client | str / null | the gossip `clientId` (WP-08), else from the version string |
| t_ns | t_ns | when written |
| event | enum | (WP-08) `learned`, `kind_update` |

**As WP-03 writes it** (`src/leaders.rs`). One line per slot when it is first learned: windows of 400 slots from the current slot (`getSlotLeaders`, on the background runtime), a new window every 100 slots, so each refresh writes about 100 lines. `kind` as known at that moment, from the newest Jito validators API list (`kind_src` its host: `running_bam` → `bam`, `running_jito` → `jito`, absent → `other`), else the static fallback (`kind_src` `static:validators-meta.json@<date>`, `kind_age_s` from that date); a validator that our bundle results named (`Accepted` / `Processed` `validator_identity`) and no list puts in `jito` or `bam` is `jito` with `kind_src` `bundle_result`; `unknown` when no source says anything. `region`, `rtt_*` and `client` are null (WP-08). List refreshes are `events.jsonl` `leaders_refresh` / `leaders_list_stale` (failed fetches keep the older list or the fallback).

**As WP-08 writes it** (`src/leaders.rs`, schema `arbx.leaders/1.1`). Every line gains `event`: `learned` (the slot first learned, as above) or `kind_update`: after a source changed (a list refresh, the node table, a validator our bundle results named), the refresher's pass writes again every scheduled slot from the current one on whose `kind` or `kind_src` is not what its last line said (the WP-03 hand-off). `city`: `<country>-<city>` (the city without its district, spaces as `_`) of the leader's gossip IP in `[leaders] geo_table` (`data/ip-geo.csv`: DB-IP City Lite ranges, CC BY 4.0, of the cluster's addresses on 2026-10-04), null when the IP or the table does not say; `region`: the metro that city belongs to (`leaders::METROS`, review WP-08 #9: Tokyo's Shibuya, Shinagawa, Chiyoda, Koto, Minato and Inzai are `JP-Tokyo`; New York, Newark, Piscataway, Secaucus, ... `US-New_York`; Frankfurt, Offenbach, Hattersheim, Limburg, ... `DE-Frankfurt`; Ashburn, Sterling, Reston, ... `US-Ashburn`; 27 metros), else the city: the shipped table's 195 cities are 132 regions, and the 671 voting validators of 2026-10-04 fall in 69 regions (96 cities), 24 of them with a single validator (32 cities). `tpu_quic`, `tpu_region` and `tpu_shared_by`: the endpoint the leader announces in gossip, its region, and how many nodes announce it (review WP-08 #3, measured again on 2026-10-04's public getClusterNodes / getVoteAccounts: 472 of 671 voting validators, 68.7% of stake, announced a TPU-QUIC address on another IP than their gossip address; the 382 on port 5010 (BAM) share 16 endpoints, one of them serves 103; the 671 announce 233 endpoints; 210 validators, 24.5% of stake, are in another city than their endpoint, 121 (14.4%) in another region, 51 (6.9%) in another country). `kind_src` of an `unknown` kind is `no_source` (review WP-08 #6). `client`: the node's gossip `clientId` (`getClusterNodes`: Agave, JitoLabs, AgaveBam, Firedancer, Frankendancer, HarmonicAgave, FireBAM, Rakurai, ...), else the family its version implies (0.x Frankendancer, 26+ Firedancer, else Agave). A fourth kind source, `kind_src` `gossip_client`, names a validator no list names and no bundle result showed: `JitoLabs` is `jito`, `AgaveBam` and `FireBAM` are `bam`, plain `Agave` is `other` (other clients say nothing; a version-derived client is no evidence; a list that names the validator wins). `rtt_ms`: host → the announced TPU-QUIC endpoint: the handshake time of a QUIC connection to `tpu_quic` (ALPN `solana-tpu`, no stream opened, no data sent, closed at once; `rtt_src` `quic_handshake`), kept **per endpoint** (review WP-08 #3): the prober measures the endpoints of the leaders of the next 64 slots, each endpoint once however many validators announce it, at most `[leaders] rtt_per_sec` (2) handshakes a second, each again after 5 minutes (after 1 minute when its handshake failed or timed out at 1.5 s), and every validator behind an endpoint carries its RTT; a leader whose endpoint has no fresh measurement takes the median of the fresh endpoints in its endpoint's region (`tpu_region`; its gossip `region` when it announces no endpoint) (`region_bucket`), never of its gossip region's when they differ; an RTT older than 10 minutes is not used (null). `rtt_age_s`: the measurement's age (the newest sample's for a region). The lines are written when a slot is learned (about 160 s ahead), so their RTT is the one known then; the attempt's snapshot (§4.37) has the RTT at sending.

### 4.28 validators.jsonl (new)

Producer: `src/leaders.rs`, hourly snapshot (WP-08: every 10 minutes). One line per validator: `{t_ns, identity, vote, stake_lamports, gossip_ip, tpu_quic, version, client, jito, bam, region, rtt_ms, src}`.

**As WP-08 writes it** (schema `arbx.validators/1.0`, bulk class: the disk ladder's step 2 stops it with rivals). Every 10 minutes once the vote accounts are known (review WP-08 #7: the first snapshot comes before the prober measured anything, and a 20-minute segment must hold one with RTTs): one line per identity with a vote account (`getVoteAccounts`, current and delinquent), with `delinquent`, `client_src` (`clientId` or `version`), `kind` and `kind_src` (§4.27), `city`, `tpu_region`, `tpu_shared_by`, `rtt_age_s` and `rtt_src` added (§4.27's meanings); `jito` = the kind is `jito` or `bam`, `bam` = it is `bam` (both null when the kind is `unknown`); node fields null for a validator absent from `getClusterNodes`. `src` names the sources of the snapshot: `getClusterNodes+getVoteAccounts+<kind list>+geo:<table>`. The node table is fetched every 10 minutes and the vote accounts every `refresh_min`, on the background runtime (`events.jsonl` `leaders_refresh` with `src` `getClusterNodes` / `getVoteAccounts`, `leaders_list_stale` on a failure, with `fails` and `retry_s`: a failed fetch, a validator list's too, is tried again after 30 s, then 60 s, ... up to its period, review WP-08 #8; every error has its URLs cut to the host and key parameters masked, review WP-08 #4); each snapshot is a `leaders_refresh` event with `src` `validators.jsonl` and the RTT counts `rtt` {`probes_ok`, `probes_failed`, `endpoints_fresh`, `regions_fresh`, `validators`, `validators_rtt_own`, `validators_rtt_bucket`}: handshakes so far, endpoints and endpoint regions with a fresh RTT, and the validators with a vote account whose RTT is their endpoint's own, a region bucket's, or neither (the rest).

### 4.29 pools.json (new)

Producer: `main.rs::run` after `pools::boot`. The registry the segment traded: `[{idx, venue, pool, t0, t1, vault0, vault1, t0_prog, t1_prog, extra: [pk], flag, source (seed/ours/both), tier, pair_key}]`. `idx` is the pool index used in `accounts.bin` (`pool_index`). **WP-16 review (F8):** the candidates boot did not keep are recorded, with why, in meta.json `pools.dropped` and the events.jsonl `pools_dropped` record (A.6).

**As WP-09 writes it** (`replay::write_pools_json`, called by `arbx run` right after the boot; written atomically; read back by `replay::read_pools_json`). pools.json is the list above, exactly (plain JSON, no header; the toolkit's blockctx maps accounts.bin `pool_index` through it and reads route accounts from it): `venue` is the seed lists' name (`PumpSwap`, `RayCPMM`, `RayAMMv4`, `DLMM`, `DAMMv2`), `source` adds `none`, `tier` is "A" for a pool on a traded pair (else null), `pair_key` is "<t0>/<t1>" of its pair (or null); every pool of the registry is listed in index order (pools on no traded pair too: the indices must be the run's). meta.json `pools.file` names it, with its `file_sha256`.

**registry-run.json** (WP-09; `arbx.registry-run/1.0`): what a replay needs besides the pools, beside pools.json and written with it: `{schema, written_ns, producer (build_short), pools_file: "pools.json", pools_sha256 (of the pools.json written with it: a replay refuses a pair that does not match), n_pools, n_pairs, pairs: [{key: [pk, pk], pools: [idx]}], watch: [{pk, role, role_id, pool_index}]}`. `pairs` is the registry's pair map in its own iteration order with each pair's pool order (the order an evaluation prices a pool's routes in); `watch` is the state stream's base watch list in order with each account's role (§4.12 roles): the re-read after a connection reads it in chunks of this order, and a replay must ask for the same chunks to accept the run's journaled answers (INPUT 11). Recordings without both files (made before WP-09) are refused by `arbx replay` (`--pools` names a pools.json, its registry-run.json beside it). (The first WP-09 draft wrote one object with all of it as pools.json, which left the toolkit's attribution without pools: review WP-09 #1.)

### 4.30 triggers.jsonl (v2: decoded swaps are hints)

Producer: `engine.rs::on_trigger` (:611-672), for every decoded swap on a watched pool.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| sig, feed, slot, pool, venue, source | | (v1) |
| zfo, amount, exact_out, executed | bool, u64, bool, bool / null | (v1) |
| st | {rx, decoded, engine, resolved, decided} | (v1) t_ns stages |
| opp | null | (v1 field; always null in v2: hints are never priced) |
| hint_only | bool | true |
| signer | pk | |
| signer_stats | [ok, failed] | at the time |
| skip | enum / null | `will_fail` (slippage bound unmeetable), `signer_fails`, `unquotable`, `exact_out_unsolved` |
| w_expected | [pk] | watched writable accounts this tx will write (registered with the CommitTracker) |
| shadow_pred | bool | a shadow prediction was made for the accuracy monitor |

**As WP-04 writes it** (`arbx.triggers/2.0`): every field above; `skip` also `failed` (an executed feed delivered the swap as failed: its signer's miss is counted at once, I-5); `st` is `{rx, decoded, engine, resolved (= engine), decided}` (the hint's bookkeeping, series `hint_rx_to_done`); `w_expected` is the write set `process` registered (the transaction's writable keys that are parts of watched pools). `signer_stats` are the signer's statistics before this swap. **WP-16 review (F10):** a hint on a state arbx-sizing refuses is skipped with the crate's reason, not `unquotable`: `skip` is the A.1 reason (`sz_layout`, `sz_transfer_fee`, `sz_pump_cashback`, ...) and the counter `trigger_<reason>`; `unquotable` / `trigger_unquotable` remain for a missing account or a size the venue cannot fill. An exact-out PumpSwap `buy` is checked against its limit with the exact debit (`buy_exact_out`); the other venues' exact-out swaps keep the solved input.

### 4.31 commits.jsonl (new)

Producer: `src/commit.rs` when a group resolves.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| txn | sig | |
| slot | slot | |
| w_src | enum | `tx-stream` (PumpSwap filter; authoritative), `shred` (hint from a live shred/deshred feed), `unknown` (no W(T); resolved by quiescence) |
| w_expected | [pk] / null | null when `w_src = unknown` |
| w_seen | [{acct, wv, aseq, t}] | updates stamped `txn` |
| t_first_shred | t_ns / null | earliest arrival of `txn` from an **unexecuted** feed (shred, deshred, entries); yellowstone-tx arrivals are excluded (F33) |
| t_first_update, t_resolved | t_ns | |
| spread_ms | ms | last − first `t_recv` of the updates stamped `txn` (the input that sets `quiescence_ms`) |
| outcome | enum | `complete`, `superseded`, `timeout_unknown`, `timeout_quiescent`, `fork_dropped` |
| lag_ms | ms / null | `t_resolved − t_first_shred` (the state-lag SLO input) |
| late_members | [{acct, wv, aseq, t}] | updates stamped `txn` that arrived after resolution; a second line with `"late": true` is written when this happens (F2) |
| torn_suspect_decisions | [decision] | decisions priced on these pools between resolution and the late member |
| pools | [pk] | Tier-A pools written |
| evaluated | integer | route evaluations it triggered (join to decisions by `trigger.txn`) |

**As WP-04 writes it** (`src/commit.rs`, `engine.rs::on_resolution`; `arbx.commits/1.0`, core class). Groups are kept for the accounts pools are priced from (consistency sets, other parts, followed DLMM bin arrays); a group opens at the first such update stamped T. Fields as above, plus `group` (the group's sequence number), `w_late` (W(T) was registered after the group's first update: usual for `tx-stream`, which arrives p50 +0.23 ms / p99 +5.9 ms after T's first account update, w5h2-p1), `superseded` (members satisfied by a newer write of another transaction), `t_commit` (the resolution on the receive-time clock: the completing update's receive time, the stream evidence's, or the timer's), `resolved_by` (`member`, `register`, `timer`, `stream`, `fork`) and `reopened_from` (a late member's new group). `outcome`: `complete` / `superseded` (known W(T)), `timeout_unknown` (known W(T) still missing members `commit_timeout_ms` after the first update), `timeout_quiescent` (unknown W(T): no update stamped T for `quiescence_ms`), `fork_dropped`. Deadlines are checked by the timers (only on an empty engine queue) and by stream evidence (a later state-stream message received past the deadline: the stream is FIFO). `lag_ms` = `t_resolved` (engine time) − `t_first_shred`, for complete and superseded groups. `t_first_shred` is the earliest arrival from an unexecuted feed (F33). A late member writes a second line `{group, txn, late: true, t, late_members, torn_suspect_decisions, reopened_group}`; the late update opens a new group (W unknown).

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (S1-8). W(T) leaves PumpSwap pools' mints out (accounts.bin role 5; `Engine::write_set`): a swap marks its pool's mints writable without writing them, and an account a transaction leaves unchanged sends no update, so a group that expected one waited the full `commit_timeout_ms` and ended `timeout_unknown`. Of segment 1's 1,405 `timeout_unknown` groups, 628 (45%) missed only mints: they now complete at their last update. None of its 29,550 groups saw a mint written together with a pool account or vault: the 3,374 groups that saw a mint written saw nothing else (3,245 of them the PUMP mint `pumpCmXq…`), and 3,373 of them were W-unknown groups resolved by quiescence (all of the segment's `timeout_quiescent` groups). The other 777 stay as they were (documented, not fixed): 560 missed only accounts of pools the transaction marked writable but whose accounts sent no update in the group at all (a route that leaves a pool unchanged), 217 an account of a pool it did write (an unchanged vault or pool account). Telling those apart before the updates arrive needs what the transaction changed: a shred hint does not carry it, and the transaction stream's balance changes cover only token accounts. Each such group still waits up to `commit_timeout_ms` (5 ms). engine_init's knobs carry `wt_skip_pump_mints` (true from these fixes on; absent, so false, in an earlier journal): a replay of an earlier run waits for the mints as the run did (`--set knobs.wt_skip_pump_mints=true` replays it with the fix).

**As the FT-009 fix writes it** (WP-21; `Engine::write_set`, `Engine::clmm_unwritten`). FT-009 (2 h shadow of the 242 CLMM pools): 47,484 of the 94,940 commit groups touching a Raydium CLMM or Whirlpool pool (50.0%) ended `timeout_unknown` (other groups 4.4%), waiting p50 5,001 µs; their missed members were the Whirlpool Oracle PDA 38,588 times, tick arrays 12,315 (Whirlpool 11,675), the Raydium AmmConfig 3,307 (it alone held 1,210 groups of the older venues) and the bitmap extension 111. What the stream sends, measured on its accounts.bin: an account a transaction gives writable to the program that owns it is sent with every such transaction, changed or not, and no other account is. A Whirlpool pool's followed tick arrays sent 3.08 updates per update of its pool account (p50 of the 68 pools with at least 50; p10 2.54, p90 3.28: a swap names 3 arrays and crosses a tick far less often; Raydium 1.66 / 3.1 / 5.1, its swaps naming a varying number), an initialized Oracle came with 97% of its pool's updates (3,446 of 3,549; per pool p50 100%, the lowest 86%: LP instructions write the pool without it), the bitmap extension 21,379 times against its pools' 37,633, and the AmmConfig, read-only to every swap, 18-43 times a 30-min segment over its 18 accounts. Never sent: an Oracle PDA with no Oracle (the 94 that do not exist, and 2 that hold lamports but no data, on two busy pools, `83v8iPyZ…` and `FpCMFDFG…`; `getMultipleAccounts`: system-owned, 0 bytes), tick arrays not created (Orca's swaps take them by design), and an AmmConfig routers mark writable without giving it writable to Raydium. So W(T) leaves out, for a Raydium CLMM or Whirlpool pool: its AmmConfig; and any part the store holds empty (`clmm_absent`: an Oracle PDA with no Oracle, a tick array or bitmap extension not created), unless a top-level instruction of a CLMM program other than a swap names it (Orca's `initialize_tick_array`, Raydium's `open_position*` create the arrays they name: the group waits for the creation; the swaps are `swap`, `swap_v2`, `two_hop_swap`, `two_hop_swap_v2`, `swap_router_base_in`). Everything else stays: the pool, the vaults, every tick array that exists (crossed or not; a crossed one is never priced before its group completes), an existing extension, an initialized Oracle. A creation through another program's CPI (an LP manager) is not seen in advance: it arrives as a late member (torn_suspect, as before). The AmmConfig's own updates join no group (taken as updates of no transaction): only Raydium's admin changes it (`update_amm_config`, which writes no pool), and as T's member its unchanged update would be a late member (torn_suspect) or open a group of unknown W holding every pool that shares the config for `quiescence_ms`. The engine benchmark's CLMM commit-group shape (`engine_benchmark_clmm_commit_groups`: the CLMM shape's 80 pools, a transaction every 2 ms seen 300 µs early on a shred feed, members 4 µs apart, the engine driven as `run` drives it) gives the commit wait p50 / p90 / p99 4 / 4 / 4 µs without CLMM pools; 4 / 5,000 / 5,000 µs with them as FT-009 ran (61.7% of their groups `timeout_unknown`); 4 / 20 / 24 µs with the fix (CLMM groups 20 / 24 / 24 µs, none `timeout_unknown`; no late member, no torn_suspect). engine_init's knobs carry `wt_skip_clmm_unwritten` (true from this fix on; absent, so false, in an earlier journal): a replay of an earlier run groups as the run did (`--set knobs.wt_skip_clmm_unwritten=true` replays it with the fix).

### 4.32 decisions.jsonl (new): every evaluation and every rejection

Producers: the engine's evaluation (`best_cycle` and its caller) and the exec send checks (`Exec::wants_send`, `copies`, `send`). One line per evaluation of a pool after a commit (or quiescence), holding every route evaluated.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| decision | integer | id |
| t_ns | t_ns | |
| kind | enum | `commit`, `quiescent`, `resend_check`, `probe` |
| trigger | {txn, slot, t_first_shred, pool} | |
| routes | [{buy, sell, mint, buy_venue, sell_venue, size, gross, net, cu, crossed: [u32, u32], reason}] | every route evaluated; `reason` = `best`, `below_min_profit`, or a rejection code |
| best | index into `routes` / null | |
| sv | sv | state version of the best route |
| route_age_ms | ms | time since the last write to any account of the best route (X1 input) |
| parts_skew_slots | {buy, sell} | the skew within each pool's consistency set (PLAN WP-04 item 4); never across pools |
| w_src | enum | of the triggering group |
| torn_suspect | bool | set afterwards if a late member of the triggering group arrived (written as a follow-up line `{decision, torn_suspect: true}`) |
| fork_priced | bool | set afterwards if a version this decision read was on an abandoned fork (follow-up line) |
| band | integer | the shared band table (PLAN WP-06 item 8, `[bands] edges`): 0 <20k, 1 20k-100k, 2 100k-1M, 3 1M-10M, 4 ≥10M, over the expected **edge** = gross − base fee, before the tip (F22). The same value is used by the send queue and the tip policy |
| crowding | {signers_6h, bucket (0 <20, 1 20-150, 2 ≥150), src (`map`, `static_list`)} | WP-00 / WP-05 (F6) |
| verdict | enum | `send`, `reject` |
| reason | enum | rejection reason (Appendix A.1) or `sent` |
| ev, rank | number, integer | the send queue's EV and band rank |
| attempt | integer / null | when sent |
| experiments | {name: arm} | WP-11: every arm the enabled experiments assigned this decision, by hash of its id (the randomization; scope aside): `x2_delay_ms`, `x4_share`, `size_max_sol`, `margin`. `{}` with experiments off |
| stages | {t_first_shred, t_update_recv, t_commit, t_engine, t_priced, t_decided} | t_ns; `t_update_recv` = receive time of the first update stamped with the trigger txn |

**As WP-04 writes it** (`engine.rs::evaluate_pool`; `arbx.decisions/1.0`, core class). One line per evaluation of a Tier-A pool whose own state a resolved group moved (its consistency set, its account, its bin arrays; a write to a shared part such as the PumpSwap fee config evaluates nothing), plus `fork` re-evaluations after a rollback and deferred ones. `kind`: `commit` (complete / superseded), `quiescent`, `timeout` (timeout_unknown), `fork`. `trigger` adds `group`. Each route is checked in order: state availability (`state_unavailable`), the store's lag (`store_lagging`), both pools' readiness (`account_missing`, `resync` (WP-04 review: a part not read again since the stream's last connection), `pending_commit`, `fork_pending`, `parts_skewed`), route-level quiet after a timeout (`route_not_quiet`: the evaluation is deferred until the route is quiet, at most 3 times), the chain clock for DLMM / DAMM v2 legs (`clock_stale`), then priced on committed state (`unquotable_*`, `broken_quote`); a priced route's reason is `best`, `not_best` (it clears `min_profit_lamports` but another route nets more) or `below_min_profit`. `best` is the highest-net priced route; `sv`, `route_age_ms`, `parts_skew_slots` and `band` (provisional edges until WP-06's `[bands]`) describe it. `verdict` / `reason`: `reject` with the first route's reason when none was priced, `below_min_profit`, `warmup` / `resync` / `state_unavailable` (an opportunity the store's health gate keeps from the executor), `no_executor` or `gate_<mode>:<reason>` (no executor in a shadow run), the executor's refusal (`gate_shadow:<reason>`, `gate_stopped`, `warmup`, `state_unavailable`, `fee_cap`, `no_free_nonce`, ...), or `send` / `sent`. `opp` names the opportunity (opportunities.jsonl `id`). `crowding`, `ev`, `rank`, `attempt` are null and `experiments` empty until WP-05 / WP-11. `t_ns` is the engine time of the decision. Follow-up lines: `{decision, torn_suspect: true, txn}` and `{decision, fork_priced: true, slots}` (WP-04 review: also for a decision that read a version slot-index `missing` evidence dropped, `slots` then empty). A route's size never exceeds the ladder's top rung (WP-04 review #5: the refinement around the best rung stays inside the ladder; it used to reach twice the top rung, 8.4 SOL in Stage 0, a trade the executor never sends). **WP-16:** every leg is parsed and quoted by arbx-sizing (`src/quote.rs`), the exact maths proven against the real venue binaries; a state it refuses is the route's reason (`sz_*`, A.1). The size is the best exact round trip among the ladder's rungs and, where a rung makes money, the crate locator's optima (the Möbius closed form, the marginal walk over DLMM bins), still never above the top rung; an optimum past a capacity steps back to the last size the venues fill. `gross` is the guard's quote delta: the sell leg's output minus what the buy leg debits (a PumpSwap `buy_exact_quote_in` can debit one unit less than the size). `crossed` counts the DLMM bins with liquidity the size takes from (limit-order layers included).

**As WP-05 writes it** (`arbx.decisions/1.1`). For an opportunity the store's health let through, `verdict` / `reason` come from the executor (`Exec::decide`): the send checks first, in `exec::send_checks`' order (`gate_shadow:<reason>` / `gate_stopped`, `recorder_unhealthy`, `warmup` / `state_unavailable` / `resync`, the sender's own `sender_stopped` / `fee_cap` / `canary_count` / `canary_wait`, `blockhash_stale`, `leader_unknown` / `leader_skip_kind`, `jito_auth_stale`), then the route checks (`expected_below_min_profit` (nothing expected), `implausible_gap`, `token_cooling`, `rent_cap`, `inflight_same_state` / `inflight_same_leader`, `band_throttle`, `crowded_skip` (bucket 2 from `crowded_skip_min_expected_lamports`: not sent by policy), `expected_below_min_profit` (below the tip + base fee + `[live] margin_lamports`), a would-send's `would_rate` / `would_rate_crowded` (the bundle path's `max_per_sec` over the last second, a model of the queue; WP-05 review #10: `rate_queue_timeout` and `crowded_pool` are only the real queue's drops, copies.jsonl `drop`), `oversized` / `build_error:<detail>`), then `send` / `sent`; `no_executor` when the shadow executor did not start. New fields: `sent` (true: sent; false: a would-send, recorded in attempts.jsonl with `sent = false`; null: no attempt), `would_send` (every check but the SendGate passed and the bundle was built: in a shadow run its `reason` is `gate_shadow:mode_shadow`, in a dry run `gate_shadow:dry_run`, `would_send` when the gate was open), `reason_ungated` (with the gate closed: the first other check that refused the decision; null for a would-send). A shadow or dry run holds no block-engine client, so its send checks do not model the token (`jito_auth_stale` never appears in its would-sends; WP-05 review #18): I-7 runs every check up to the signature, and the token is the send's. `crowding` is the route's `{signers_6h, bucket, src (`map`, `static_list`, `map_partial`)}` (null when the executor did not reach the route checks): `signers_6h` is the larger of its two pools' counts of distinct arbitrage signers that landed there within the window, as of the engine's last housekeeping (not the union of the two: that needs a set intersection on the decision path; WP-05 review #4); the map lives in the process, so in a campaign's segments (shorter than the window) `src` stays `static_list`: WP-00's list is Stage 0's crowding, and only bucket 2 ranks. `ev` the expected net in lamports (expected − tip − base fee, an integer), `rank` the send queue's rank (0 small, 1 small crowded, 2 ≥1M, 3 ≥1M crowded), `attempt` the attempt (sends and would-sends). `band` is src/bands.rs's table: the executor's band (at the size it would trade, `[live] max_size_sol` or the canary's: the band the queue and the tip use, the attempt's `band`) once it reached the route checks, else the engine's; `band_at_opp` is always the engine's, at its priced size (WP-05 review #9).

**WP-06** (`arbx.decisions/1.2`). A route's `net` is its gross minus the engine's fee estimate, now the base fee plus the tip the bundle would pay at that gross (`tip::TipModel::fee`: the band's `[tips]` share of the edge, within the floor and the cap), where it was 5,000 + `cu_price_micro` × CU / 10^6 + `min_tip` (a bundle pays CU price 0: PLAN WP-06 item 5), so a decision's net and the executor's `ev` are the same number. A route's `cu` is the CU table's when its overhead and both legs have `[builder] cu_min_samples` samples, else the fallback formula ((29,000 + the legs' estimates) × 1.10, rounded up to 1,000: the value before WP-06), without the ATA create and close (attempts.jsonl `tx.cu_limit` adds them). Routes gain `dlmm_cap_bound` (bool; null when not priced; absent in a replay of a recording made before WP-06): the size search stopped below a larger size because a DLMM leg would have walked past its window of bin arrays (the active array and the arrays held contiguous with it, at most 5: the window the builder names, `quote::dlmm_window`, PLAN WP-06 item 6); when no size fits, WP-16's `unquotable_dlmm_array` stays the reason. The executor's route checks gain `uva_missing` after `token_cooling` (a PumpSwap buy while the wallet's accumulator is not known to exist and `[builder] allow_uva_create` is false; unknown is refused) and `mid_not_held` after it (`[builder] create_close = false` and the wallet holds no account for the route's token), and its build's reasons are `oversized` (over 1,232 bytes or 64 locks after the size budget's fallbacks, §4.37), `dlmm_ext_needed` (a DLMM leg's arrays leave the internal bitmap, indexes −512..=511, and the store holds no bitmap extension for the pool) and `build_error:<detail>`. `rent_cap` is no longer produced: a token the wallet holds no account for is traded with create+close (`[live] max_new_token_accounts` is gone). `band` is `[bands] edges`' (default 20k, 100k, 1M, 10M: the same table).

### 4.33 opportunities.jsonl (v1, kept)

Producer: `engine.rs::finish_opp` (:976-1008), for opportunities ≥ min_profit after `outcome_watch_ms`.
Fields (v1): `id`, `trigger`, `trigger_feed`, `slot`, `trigger_pool`, `buy_pool`, `sell_pool`, `mint`, `buy_source`, `sell_source`, `size_lamports`, `gross_lamports`, `net_lamports`, `cu`, `bins_crossed`, `st`, `arrivals`, `trigger_landed_slot`, `taker` {sig, slot, first_seen_ns, first_feed, window_ms}, `poh` {trigger {slot, ms_in_slot, ticks}, taker_ms_after_trigger}, `touches`.
v2 adds `decision` and `sv` (**as WP-04 writes it**: `arbx.opportunities/1.2`; `sv` is the state version's 16 hex characters; opportunities come from the state path only, `trigger_feed` null). **The `taker` is a heuristic** (the first tx touching both pools); it misses single-pool and zero-capture closers (W §4). Attribution uses R4 (§6.4), never `taker`.

### 4.34 predictions.jsonl (v2: all venues)

Producer: `engine.rs::check_prediction` (:909-942), extended by WP-04 item 13 into the accuracy monitor. It runs when the decoded trigger's commit group completes, at idle time, from a bounded queue.

Fields (v1): `sig`, `venue`, `pool`; DLMM: `pred_active`, `actual_active`, `match`; DAMM v2: `sqrt_price_pred`, `sqrt_price` (u128s), `match`; CP family: `r0_pred`, `r0`, `r1_pred`, `r1` (u128s), `err0_bps`, `err1_bps`, `err0`, `err1`. In v1 the prediction was made from the store when the hint arrived. Any earlier write to the pool in the same slot made a correct prediction look "inexact": DAMM v2 matched 662 of 758 (87%, res-code §3).

New in v2 (F5):

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns, slot | | |
| hint | {exact, err} | the v1 comparison (prediction made at hint time); kept for comparison with arb-lab |
| pre_state | {known (bool), versions: [{acct, aseq, slot, wv}]} | the version of each account T wrote, just before T's write (from the 8-version ring) |
| input_src | enum | `executed` (T's executed amounts, from the PumpSwap stream or a backfilled exec record) or `decoded` |
| exact | bool / null | the prediction recomputed on `pre_state` with that input, against T's committed state; null when `pre_state.known` is false (counted as `pre_state_unknown`, never scored) |
| dlmm_bins | {checked, exact} | per changed bin |
| window_exact_pct | number / null | the pool's exact share over its last 200 scored swaps |
| quarantine_action | enum | `log` in Stage 0 (no quarantine acts; PLAN OD-8) |

R8 (§6.4) extends the scoring offline to every successful swap on a watched pool, aggregator-routed ones included. It uses `exec.jsonl` inner instructions and `arbx quote` at the pre-state version.

**As WP-04 writes it** (`src/accuracy.rs`, `engine.rs::score_line`; `arbx.predictions/2.0`). A hint is queued when its transaction's commit group resolves (not `fork_dropped`) and scored at idle time (TIMER `what` 4, 64 per call; `[accuracy] queue` 10,000, overflow counted `pred_queue_overflow`). Pre-state: for each account of the pool (consistency set, other parts, the Clock (every venue since WP-16: below), the followed bin arrays), the version just before T's own write when T wrote it (T's group members say which), else the version before T's first write; a pre-state the ring (`[accuracy] ring_versions` 8) no longer holds, or a T-version it evicted, is `pre_state.known` false (`pred_pre_state_unknown`, `exact` null). `input_src` is `decoded` (the decoded amount; an exact-out swap's input is solved on the pre-state): the executed input from the transaction's meta is not carried to the engine yet. `exact`: CP family both effective reserves equal; DLMM the active bin and every changed bin's amounts (`dlmm_bins {checked, exact}`); DAMM v2 the sqrt price. When not exact, `pre_state_pred` and `committed` give both states. `hint {exact, err}` is the at-hint prediction against the same committed state (err: the larger reserve error in bps, the active-bin distance, or the sqrt-price error in bps), with the v1 fields beside it. `window_exact_pct`: the pool's exact share over its last 200 scores. Counters `pred_scored`, `pred_exact`, `pred_pre_state_unknown`, `pred_unquotable`, `pred_not_written` (the group wrote nothing of the pool), `pred_not_committed` (no group within 5 s), `pred_trigger_failed` (an executed feed said the swap failed), `pred_fork_dropped`. **WP-16:** the prediction takes the swap's amounts from arbx-sizing (the third party's own swap: a DAMM v2 rate limiter's fee is priced, any followed DLMM bin array may be walked) and the pool's post-state from each venue's bookkeeping (`src/accuracy.rs`: CPMM's owed protocol, fund and creator fees with the single fee ceil; PumpSwap's protocol and creator fees leaving the quote vault; DLMM's market-making layer moving the bin's amounts, limit-order fills not; DAMM v2's full port), checked exact on 1,252 real swaps on the venue binaries (`src/quote/tests.rs`, testdata/venue-parity). The Clock is now read for every venue (CPMM and AMM v4 open times; PumpSwap's quote does not read it, but the monitor asks the same pre-state of every pool), so its pre-state version is needed for every venue (WP-16 review F5): the Clock has one version per slot and the ring keeps `[accuracy] ring_versions` (8), so a hint scored more than about 8 slots after its transaction (a queue backlog, a late resolution) loses the Clock's pre-state and is `pred_pre_state_unknown` for every venue, CP pools included. A swap the crate does not model (`sz_*`, A.1) is not scored: `exact` null, and the line carries `refused` (the reason); **WP-16 review (F10):** it is counted `pred_<reason>` (`pred_sz_transfer_fee`, ...), and `pred_unquotable` stays for a swap that does not quote for another reason (an account missing, a size the venue cannot fill). **WP-16 review (F6):** every line carries `exact_out` (the decoded swap is exact-out) and `exact_out_model`: `exact` for PumpSwap's `buy` (the base out exactly, for the debit `buy_exact_out` computes: exact on 420 of 420 recorded buys), `solved` for the other venues' exact-out swaps (the output of the smallest exact-in size that reaches the amount, not the venue's own exact-out rounding), null for exact-in. A `solved` prediction is scored and recorded but kept out of the per-venue rate (status `accuracy.by_venue`; counter `pred_exact_out_solved`); the toolkit's R8 counts it apart (`exact_out_solved`), and counts the null-`exact` lines as `pre_state_unknown`, `refused` (by reason) or `unquotable`.

### 4.35 caps.jsonl (v1, kept)

Producer: `engine.rs::record_caps` (:844-874).
Fields: `id`, `kind` (`state`, `decoded`), `slot`, `buy`, `sell`, `buy_venue`, `sell_venue`, `mint`, `best_size`, `best_net`, `at_trade_size` [size, net], `caps` [[budget_sol, size, net or null]]. v2 adds `decision` (**as WP-04 writes it**: `arbx.caps/1.2`; `kind` `state` or `fork`, written after every pool of the group was priced). **WP-16:** `best_size` is arbx-sizing's locator over (0, the largest cap] with the decision's size beside it, evaluated exactly (it was a doubling ladder and a ternary search); a cap's size past a DLMM leg's walk budget is null, like one past the bin arrays we follow.

### 4.36 sims.jsonl (v1, diagnostic only)

Producer: `exec.rs::simulate` (:372-416), at most `[sim] max_per_sec`. RPC simulation at processed commitment, usually one slot behind the trigger (res-engine §2): it measures RPC lag, not the gap. No gate uses it any more.
Fields (v1): route fields (`opp`, `trigger`, `trigger_feed`, `slot`, `buy_pool`, `buy_venue`, `buy_source`, `sell_pool`, `sell_venue`, `sell_source`, `mint`, `size_lamports`, `predicted_mid`, `predicted_back`, `predicted_spent`, `predicted_profit` (WP-16 merge: `predicted_back` − `predicted_spent`, the buy leg's debit), `gross_at_opp`, `net_at_opp`), `t_ns`, `build_us`, `canary_bytes`, `build_error` or (`bytes`, `locks`, `cu_limit`, `creates_ata`, `min_profit`, `sim_ms`, `sim_slot`, `err`, `code`, `meaning`, `encoding_ok`, `units`, `delivered`, `logs_tail`) or `sim_error`.

**As WP-05 writes it.** A dry run with `[dry_run] simulate` adds one line per simulated would-send (at most `max_sim_per_sec` a second): `{attempt, src: "dry_run", t_ns}` with `sim_ms`, `sim_slot`, `err`, `code`, `meaning`, `encoding_ok`, `units`, `delivered`, `logs_tail`, or `sim_error`: the signed bundle transaction simulated read-only (`simulateTransaction`, sigVerify off since the throwaway key is not the payer, the recorded blockhash kept so its age shows), never sent. `[sim]` lines (`Exec::simulate`) are as before, without `canary_bytes`.

**WP-06.** A `[sim]` line adds `closes_ata`; the transaction simulated has the bundle's layout (the create and the close when the wallet holds no account for the token), without a tip. **After the merge of WP-06** (`arbx.sims/1.2`): WP-06's lane added `closes_ata` under 1.1, which the earlier builds (the FT-000b recordings) write without it; 1.2 is the shape with it.

### 4.37 attempts.jsonl (v2)

Producer: `exec.rs::send`, at signing. One line per attempt.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| attempt, decision, opp | integer | |
| t_ns | t_ns | (v1) signed time |
| kind | enum | `live`, `canary` |
| trigger | {txn, slot, kind (`commit`, `quiescent`, `resend`), t_first_shred} | (v1 `trigger`, `trigger_feed`, `slot`) |
| route | {buy_pool, buy_venue, buy_source, sell_pool, sell_venue, sell_source, mint, mint_prog, hub} | (v1 flat fields) |
| size_lamports | lam | (v1) |
| expected | {mid, back, spent, gross, fee, tip, net} | (v1 `predicted_mid`, `predicted_back`, `expected_profit` = gross). WP-16 merge: `spent` is the buy leg's debit at `size_lamports` (arbx-sizing; PumpSwap's `buy_exact_quote_in` now and then debits a lamport below the size) and `gross` = `back` − `spent`, the guard's quote delta, as the decision's route `gross` (§4.32); it was `back` − `size_lamports` |
| band | integer | |
| sv, route_age_ms, parts_skew_slots | | from the decision |
| min_profit_onchain, margin | lam | |
| tip | {lamports, account, sender, share, band, arm, policy} | |
| tx | {sig, bytes, locks, alts: [pk], alt_fallback (`none`, `alt_index`, `held_ata`), cu_limit, cu_src (table cell key or `fallback`), cu_price_micro, loaded_data_limit, creates_ata, closes_ata, guard_max_drop, program: {id, kind (`nexus-v2`, `arbx-exec`), layout}} | |
| blockhash | {hash, slot, height, age_slots, commitment, src (`blocks_meta`, `slotidx`, `shred_entry`, `rpc_latest`)} | F14 |
| leader | {target_slot, identity, kind, kind_src, region, rtt_ms, rtt_src, rtt_age_s, client, be_rtt_ms, next: {slot, identity, kind}} | the LeaderBook snapshot (WP-03 / WP-05; region, RTT and client from WP-08). `rtt_ms` is host → leader TPU-QUIC; `be_rtt_ms` is the latest keep-alive RTT to the block engine. **As WP-08 provides it:** `LeaderBook::snapshot(slot)` (`leaders::LeaderSnap`: `slot`, `leader`, `kind`, `kind_src`, `kind_age_s`, `region`, `city`, `tpu_quic`, `tpu_region`, `tpu_shared_by`, `rtt_ms`, `rtt_age_s`, `rtt_src`, `client`, `be_rtt_ms`, `next {slot, identity, kind}`, §4.27); an `unknown` kind carries its reason in `kind_src` (`no_schedule`: the target slot is outside the schedule held, `leader` null; `no_source`), so the field acceptance "identity and kind, or `unknown` with a reason" is checkable; `rtt_ms` is host → the endpoint the leader announces (`tpu_quic`), its own (`quic_handshake`) or its endpoint region's median (`region_bucket`); `be_rtt_ms` is what the send path last published with `leaders::note_be_rtt` (WP-05's keep-alive loop), null when older than 5 minutes |
| crowding | {signers_6h, bucket, src} | as in decisions |
| inflight | {key, prev_attempt, prev_sv, why_allowed (`first`, `new_state`, `new_leader`, `new_slot_verified`)} | |
| queue | {rank, ev, wait_us, lambda} | send-slot queue |
| experiments_assigned | {name: arm} | WP-11 (review #10): every arm the decision was assigned (decisions.jsonl `experiments`), so an attempt is attributed by its assignment; absent with experiments off |
| experiments | {name: arm} | WP-11: the arms that applied to this attempt (in their experiment's scope): `x2_delay_ms` (the send task slept that long before the copy left; recorded on would-sends too), `x4_share` (the share that replaced the band's: also `tip.arm`), `size_max_sol` (the cap the engine's size search ran under), `margin` (the on-chain margin floor used). `{}` with experiments off; `tip.arm` null |
| stages | {t_first_shred, t_update_recv, t_commit, t_engine, t_priced, t_decided, t_built, t_signed, t_enqueued, t_dequeued, t_sent, t_ack} | (v1 `st` had rx, decoded, engine, resolved, decided, built, signed. From WP-00, state-trigger attempts fill `rx` with the update receive time and `engine` with the dequeue time instead of one value, and they add `fired`) |
| copies | [{path, sig, bytes, tip, min_profit}] | (v1) one copy in Stage 0 |
| route_in | {accounts: [{acct, wv}], dlmm_arrays: {pool: [i64]}, buy_end, sell_end} | references for offline rebuild (X6) |

v1-only fields (`fee_arm`, `path_arm`, `trigger_cu_price_micro`, `rival_cu_price_micro`, `cu_price_micro`, `priority_fee_lamports`, `worst_fee_lamports`, `creates_token_account`, `nonce`, `not_sent`, `bundle_resends`) are read by the legacy reader only.

**As WP-05 writes it** (`arbx.attempts/2.0`, `exec.rs::AttemptLine`; a send's line right after its fire, a would-send's at once). One line per attempt: a send (live, canary: `sent` true) or a would-send (shadow, dry run, a closed gate: `sent` false, `not_sent` the gate's reason or the mode; PLAN-ADDENDUM I-7). `t_ns` is the build time (`stages.t_signed` the signature's). `kind`: `live`, `canary`, `shadow`, `dry_run`. `trigger` adds `feed`; its `kind` is the evaluation's (`commit`, `quiescent`, `timeout`, `fork`). `tx`: `sig` (null for a would-send of a live, canary or shadow executor: not signed), `alts` (the tables the message loads from), `alt_fallback` `none`, `cu_src` `fallback` (the engine's per-venue estimate; WP-06 brings measured CU), `cu_price_micro` 0 (a bundle pays its tip only), `loaded_data_limit` and `guard_max_drop` null, `closes_ata` false, `payer`, `program {id, kind: "nexus-v2", layout: "v2"}`. `blockhash.src` is the ring head's source (`slotidx`, `blocks_meta`, `entries`, `poll`) and `commitment` `[send_policy] blockhash_commitment`'s; never a durable nonce. `leader` is `LeaderBook::snapshot(target_slot)` as WP-08 provides it (target = the tip + 1). `tip {lamports, account, sender (the bundle path), share, band, arm: null, policy: "share_of_edge"}` (the share of the edge is rounded down). `queue {rank, band_rank, crowd_rank, ev, wait_us: null (the wait is copies.jsonl `queue_wait_us`), lambda}`. `inflight.why_allowed`: `first`, `new_state`, `new_leader` (`new_slot_verified` from Stage 1). `stages` adds `t_build_start`; `t_dequeued`, `t_sent`, `t_ack` are null (copies.jsonl has the copy's times). `copies` `[{path, sig, bytes, tip, min_profit}]`: one bundle copy. `route_in.accounts` `[{acct, slot, wv}]` (the versions the decision priced), `dlmm_arrays {buy, sell}`. New: `checks` (the send and route checks it passed, in order), `sent`, `not_sent`, `net_at_opp`, `gross_at_opp`, and for a dry run `dry_run {signer, tx_b64 (the signed transaction, base64), bytes, checks: [{check, ok, detail}] (`parses`, `one_signature`, `signature_verifies` (with the throwaway key), `payer_is_wallet`, `size`, `accounts_resolve`, `locks`, `blockhash`, `cu_limit`, `no_cu_price`, `tip_last` (the last instruction, a System transfer of the tip from the payer to its account), `tip_to_jito`, `tip_check` (the send path's own copy check), `route_program`, `pools_named`, and from the route instruction decoded out of the signed bytes (WP-05 review #12): `route_data` (it decodes: version 2, two legs), `route_amount_in` and `route_min_profit` (`{signed, want}`: the attempt's `size_lamports` and `min_profit_onchain`), `min_profit_covers_tip` (the signed min_profit ≥ every System transfer from the payer + the base fee + `[send_policy] margin_floor`: a landed bundle cannot lose), `route_accounts` (the authority is the payer, the quote account the payer's WSOL account, each leg delivering into the mid token's account then the quote account, through its venue's program, naming its pool)), checks_ok, accounts: [{key, signer, writable, via (`static` or the table)}], simulated}`. The toolkit keeps would-sends apart (`model.Segment.would_sends`): they are never attempts, so never in a land rate.

**WP-06** (`arbx.attempts/2.1`; PLAN WP-06, PLAN-ADDENDUM go-live gate 2026-10-05). The bundle transaction's instructions, in order: SetComputeUnitLimit; SetLoadedAccountsDataSizeLimit (`[builder] loaded_data_limit`: `auto`, a byte count, or `off`); the ATA program's CreateIdempotent of the mid token's account, when the wallet holds none (the store's view where the state stream follows the wallet, else the boot read); the nexus-v2 route; the token program's CloseAccount of that account to the payer (the route leaves it empty, so the close returns its rent and nothing else); the tip transfer, last. `tx` as written: `alt_fallback` (`none`; `alt_index`: lookup tables from the engine's cache that name the route's pools were added, greedily the one that loads the most of the message's remaining static accounts, at least 2, at most `[builder] max_alts` (4), while the message is over the limits; `held_ata`: still over, the account is kept (no close: a held-set addition). Held-set additions are off by default (`[builder] held_max = 0`, review WP-06 #1: go-live gate item 2, nothing stays locked), so the default is `oversized`; an explicit opt-in (`held_max` > 0) allows one while the held set, the permanent WSOL, USDC and USDT aside and the additions in flight counted, is under `held_max`, and only when the expected profit covers the decision's bar plus the rent the account locks (`build::HELD_RENT_BOUND`, 2,282,880 lamports: a 200-byte account at 6,960 lamports a byte, above mainnet's 1,488,440 / 1,513,840 on 2026-10-04); still over 1,232 bytes or 64 locks: refused `oversized`, with why the held-ATA rung was not taken in the build error), `alt_index_tables` (the tables `alt_index` added; `alts` lists every table the message loads from), `cu_limit` (the route's CU from `cu_src` plus the ATA create's and close's when made: the table's p99 × 1.10, else 30,000 and 5,000; at most 1.4M), `cu_src` (`table:<buy leg key>+<sell leg key>` or `fallback`, §4.48), `loaded_data_limit` (null when `off`; `auto`: every account the transaction loads at its size from the store (64 KiB when unknown) + 64 bytes, every program it invokes at its deployed size + 36 bytes (program/venue-harness MANIFEST sizes; 4 MiB when unknown), 8,248 bytes per lookup table, + 25% + 256 KiB, rounded up to 32 KiB, at most 64 MiB: SIMD-0186's accounting), `creates_ata`, `closes_ata`, `held_addition` (created and not closed), `held_rent_lamports` (a held-set addition's rent bound, in its `min_profit_onchain`; null otherwise), `mid_account` (the account created; null when none), `guard_max_drop` (`[builder] payer_guard`: nexus-v2's optional 8-byte `max_payer_lamport_drop` trailer on the route instruction; off by default, null). `tip` adds `floor` and `cap` (`[tips]`); `share` is the share the tip was cut with (`[tips] shares` by band; `contested_shares` once the Stage 2A selector calls a gap contested) and `band` the expected edge's (`[bands] edges`): the tip is ⌊share × edge⌋ within [floor, cap] (Stage 0: 0.7 in every band, 1,000 to 100,000 lamports: the values before WP-06; the jito-bundle path keeps only its `tip_lamports`, which may not exceed the floor, and its tip accounts). `min_profit_onchain` still covers the tip, the base fee and the margin: the route's profit check reads the quote (WSOL) account only, and the create's rent returns to the payer in the same transaction, so a landed create+close bundle moves the payer's lamports by the base fee and the tip only (executed on the real programs: program/venue-harness `tests/arbx_bundle.rs`). A held-set addition's `min_profit_onchain` adds `held_rent_lamports`: the WSOL it must make covers the SOL the kept account locks. `checks` lists `uva` where it listed `rent`. The dry run's checks add, derived from the signed bytes and the decision, never from the build (review WP-06 #2): `cu_limit` becomes `{tx, want, route, creates, closes}`: the decision's route CU (`cu_src`'s) plus the create's and the close's allowance for each one the signed message makes, at most 1.4M; `ix_order` (`{signed, want}`: the instruction kinds above, in that order, nothing else); `loaded_data_limit` (`{signed, want, need}`: `auto` re-derived from the signed message's own accounts, every distinct account of its full list at its weight on the decision's sizes, a program it invokes as one, 8,248 per lookup table it names, with `auto`'s margin;
