a fixed limit is the configured one; `off`: none); `ata_create` (exactly one CreateIdempotent when the wallet holds no account for the token, none otherwise: paid by the payer, of the payer's associated account for the route's mint and token program, the route's mid account); `ata_close` (exactly one CloseAccount of that account, to the payer, by the payer, through the mint's token program, unless a justified held-set addition; none when nothing is created); `rent_returns` (every account created is closed to the payer after the route, unless it is the justified held-set addition; nothing else is closed); `held_addition` (`{kept, held_ok, with_close: {bytes, locks}, rent}`: an account created and not closed to the payer passes only when held-set additions are configured and allowed at the decision (`held_max` > 0, off by default, and the held set may grow), it is the route's mid account, and the same signed instructions with its close after the route, compiled on the tables the message names, are over 1,232 bytes or 64 locks); `route_min_profit` wants the decision's min_profit plus the rent bound of a justified addition, and `min_profit_covers_tip` adds the rent bound of every account kept (`kept_rent`); `route_payer_accounts` (`{foreign}`: no leg names the payer's USDC or USDT account unless it is the route's mid account: the route's min_profit watches the quote account only, review WP-06 #7); and `route_guard` (`{signed, want}`); `route_data` accepts the route with or without the trailer.

**At the merge of WP-06** (onto integ/stage0, 2026-10-06; attempts stays 2.1). An attempt's `size_lamports` is at most the executor's size cap at the decision: the canary's or `[live] max_size_sol`, under WP-07's bankroll on the ledger's money view (the engine re-quotes the route at it; the executor refuses `size_cap` above it). A held-set addition (`held_ata`, `held_addition` true; only with `[builder] held_max` > 0) is also bounded by WP-07's rent cap: the held set may grow only while the money view's recoverable rent plus `held_rent_lamports`' bound stays within `[supervisor] rent_cap_sol`, otherwise the bundle that needed it is refused `oversized`; the dry run's `held_addition` check reads the same rule (`held_ok`). A create+close attempt (`creates_ata` and `closes_ata` true) is never capped by rent. The index the size budget draws `alt_index` tables from follows the bounded lookup-table cache (the FT-000 fixes evict): an evicted table leaves it, so it stays as bounded as the cache (a table the cache does not hold was never a candidate: decisions do not change).

**FT-006** (`arbx.attempts/2.2`; the BAM landing fix, `[send_policy.bam]`). On a BAM-led target slot with the policy on, the bundle bids at least `min_tip_per_cu` × its CU limit (the build's `cu_limit`), never above `max_share` of the edge (gross − base fee), the `[tips]` cap or the decision's bar (expected ≥ tip + base fee + max(`margin_floor`, `[live] margin_lamports`)), and never below the band's tip; Jito-led slots bid the band's tip. `tip.policy` is `bam_floor` when the bid was raised (else `share_of_edge`; `tip.share` stays the band's share), and `tip.bam` is `{band_tip, floor, cu_limit, room, reached, raised, min_tip_per_cu, max_share, below_floor}` on a BAM-led slot with the policy on (null otherwise); with `below_floor = "skip"` a bid that cannot reach the floor is not sent (reason `bam_bid_below_floor`, A.1; counter `reject_bam_bid_below_floor`, A.7). `copies[]` add `endpoint` (the configured jito-bundle path's host, where the copy goes or a would-send would go; from the config, so a replay gives the same) and `leader_kind` (the target slot's leader kind).

### 4.38 copies.jsonl (v1, +fields)

Producer: `send/mod.rs::Paths::fire` (:813-867), one line per copy, sent or not.
Fields (v1, struct `Sent` :689-706): `attempt`, `path`, `sig`, `tip_account`, `tip_lamports`, `t_sent` (0 = never sent), `t_ack` (0 = never answered), `ms`, `ok`, `status` (HTTP; null for gRPC), `reply`, `bundle`.
New: `blockhash_slot`, `queue_wait_us`.

**As WP-05 writes it** (`arbx.copies/1.2`): `blockhash_slot`; `queue_wait_us` (µs waited for a slot of the bundle path's rate queue; null: no queue, or no slot); `drop` (why the copy never left: `rate_queue_timeout`, `crowded_pool` (the queue; F6), `bad_tx` (it did not pay the path), `jito_auth_stale` (no valid cached token); `send_task` (the fire task failed); null: it left, or the endpoint refused it); `lambda` (the queue's λ when it was queued). `t_sent` is 0 for a copy that never left. `t_ack` is the time the endpoint answered: an HTTP status, or a gRPC status with which the block engine refuses the bundle (invalid argument, resource exhausted, permission denied, unauthenticated, failed precondition, ...: `jito::refused_code`); 0 for a copy that left without an answer (a timeout, a transport error), which may still land (WP-05 review #7). Stage 0 fires one copy per attempt, the bundle.

**FT-006** (`arbx.copies/1.3`): `endpoint` (the path's host, never its URL; a jito-bundle copy: its block engine; null for a copy whose send task failed) and `leader_kind` (`jito`, `bam`, `other`, `unknown`: the kind of the target slot's leader the copy was aimed at; null when not given, e.g. `arbx senders --raw`).

### 4.39 bundles.jsonl (v2): every block-engine result

Producer: `send/jito.rs::on_result` (:337-358), for every result on the subscription, ours or not yet matched.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns | t_ns | (v1) receipt time |
| bundle | str | (v1) uuid |
| outcome | enum | (v1) `processed`, `finalized`, `accepted`, `lost-auction`, `would-fail`, `dropped`, `rejected` |
| slot, index, bid | integer / null | (v1) slot; bundle index in block (processed); winning bid (lost-auction) |
| detail, code | str / null | (v1) block-engine text; parsed code (`0x4e580002`, `blockhash-not-found`, `instruction-error`, `other`) |
| sig | sig / null | (v1) our transaction |
| seq | integer | nth result for this bundle (0 = first) |
| validator | pk / null | from `Accepted`/`Processed` `validator_identity` |
| attempt | integer / null | |
| stream_epoch | integer | result-stream connection number; a gap between epochs is in `events.jsonl` (`jito_stream_down`) |

**As WP-05 writes it** (`arbx.bundles/2.0`, `Jito::on_result_at`): every result as it arrives, ours or not, with `seq`, `validator`, `attempt` (null until our sendBundle reply names the bundle: a result that came first is kept under its bundle id and joined then) and `stream_epoch`; `slot`, `index`, `bid`, `detail`, `validator` are null when the result does not carry them. Every result of ours is also an INPUT 5 (§4.13).

### 4.40 outcomes.jsonl (v2)

Producer: `exec.rs::on_settled`, rewritten around `ledger.rs` (WP-07), when the attempt is final.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| attempt, opp | integer | (v1) |
| t_ns | t_ns | (v1) written |
| sig | sig | our transaction |
| final | enum | `landed_ok`, `landed_fail`, `not_landed_expired`, `not_landed_refused`, `unsettled` |
| final_t_ns, final_src | t_ns, enum | `wallet`, `jito`, `expiry` (after the one bounded `getSignatureStatuses` with `searchTransactionHistory` found nothing), `statuses` |
| amend | bool | true on a later line for the same attempt, written when a landing is found after the attempt was finalised (for example from the next segment's wallet stream, F43); readers take the last line per attempt and report amends |
| verdicts | [{t_ns, outcome, slot, validator, index, bid, code, detail}] | every block-engine result, in order |
| first_verdict_ms | ms / null | from `t_sent` |
| accepted | {t_ns, slot, validator} / null | first `accepted` |
| processed | {slot, index, validator} / null | |
| landed | {slot, idx, t_wallet_ns, leader, leader_kind} / null | from `wallet.jsonl` |
| slots_after_trigger | integer / null | (v1) |
| poh | {trigger: {slot, ms, feed}, ours: {slot, ms, feed}, ours_ms_after_trigger} | (v1 shape + feed) |
| ms_after_trigger_src | enum | `poh`, `block_idx`, `arrival` |
| seen | {first_ns, first_feed, by: {feed: t_ns}} | (v1 `seen_by`) our tx in our feeds |
| send_to_seen_ms | ms / null | (v1) |
| ok, err, code, meaning | | (v1) |
| fee_lamports, cu_consumed | lam, integer | (v1 fee) |
| prog_events | [{kind (`leg`, `out`, `profit_fail`, `guard_fail`, …), leg, amount, code}] | decoded program logs |
| delivered, predicted | [u64], [mid, back] | (v1) |
| round_trip_on_chain, round_trip_predicted | lam | (v1) both on one basis: the last leg's output minus the size (the route program's events carry what each leg delivered, not the buy leg's debit); `expected` is the guard's quote delta (§4.37 `expected.gross`) |
| ledger | {trade_gross, tip, fee, rent_nonrecoverable, rent_locked, rent_reclaimed, trade_net, equity_delta, liquid_delta} | from `ledger.jsonl` |
| closer | null | always null in the bot; the toolkit's `blockctx` and R4 compute it |
| statuses_checks | [{t_ns, result}] | bounded cross-checks |

v1-only fields (`gate`, `copies` [...], `landed` as a path name, `tip_paid_lamports`, `profit_lamports` (includes rent), `profit_vs_expected`, `jito` (one verdict), `settle_error`, `failed_fees_lamports` (running total), `cap_lamports`, `run_profit_lamports`) are read by the legacy reader.

**As WP-05 writes it** (`arbx.outcomes/2.0`, `Exec::finalize`; settlement from the chain). An attempt sent is final when: the wallet stream records its signature (`final_src` `wallet`: `landed_ok` / `landed_fail`, with the fee, CU and `prog_events` of INPUT 7); or the chain passes its blockhash's validity (the newest block height above its height + 150; 300 slots past its slot without heights; 120 s without either) and one bounded `getSignatureStatuses` with `searchTransactionHistory` (2 s, one retry; F43) finds it landed (`statuses`) or not (`not_landed_expired`, `expiry`; when that check fails, what a cross-check saw decides, else `not_landed_expired` with `error` "expiry check failed: ..."); or its copy never left or the block engine refused it (`not_landed_refused`, `final_src` `send`, `error` the copy's `drop` code or reply; its in-flight entry, its fee reservation and its ≥1M-band token are given back); or the run stops first (`unsettled`, `final_src` `stop`; stop.json lists them). A copy that left without an answer (copies `t_ack` 0: a timeout, a transport error) may still land: it stays open like an accepted one (cross-checked, settled by its landing or its blockhash's expiry), its entry and reservation kept, and its final line's `error` is `send: <the reply>` (WP-05 review #7). The cross-checks at +2 s, +10 s and +60 s (one batched `getSignatureStatuses` each, 500 ms, no history) are kept in `statuses_checks` `[{t_ns, at (`+2s`, `+10s`, `+60s`, `expiry`), result: {slot, ok, err, confirmation} | null | {error}}]`. A later record of a final attempt writes an amend line `{attempt, t_ns, sig, final, final_t_ns, final_src, amend: true, amends, landed, ok, err, fee_lamports, cu_consumed, prog_events, delivered, round_trip_on_chain, failed_fees_total_lamports}` within the segment: a landing found after a final that said not landed (a later wallet record, a late cross-check: `amends` `not_landed`), or the wallet record of a landing the expiry check found first (`amends` `statuses`: it brings the fee, the CU and the route program's events the status check does not have; WP-05 review #3); readers take the last line per attempt. The money (§4.8) then counts what the record says instead of what was counted, each landing once; a landing of an earlier segment's attempt is in this segment's wallet.jsonl by signature (`attempt` null), for WP-07's ledger to join. Beyond the table: `decision`, `kind`, `bundle`, `path`, `error`, `expected`, `tip_lamports`, `min_profit_onchain`, `blockhash {slot, height, valid_until_height}`, `target_slot`, `failed_fees_total_lamports` (the running total). `poh.trigger` and `poh.ours` are `{slot, ms, ticks}`; `ms_after_trigger_src` is `poh` or null. `delivered` comes from `prog_events` (the `out` records, in leg order) and `round_trip_on_chain` = delivered[1] − size. `ledger` is null until WP-07. Would-sends (shadow, dry run) have no outcome.

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.outcomes/2.1`). `ledger` is the ledger's line of the landing's transaction (`{trade_gross, tip, fee, rent_nonrecoverable, rent_locked, rent_reclaimed, trade_net, equity_delta, liquid_delta}`, §4.42) on the final line written from the wallet record and on an amend line; null on a final that has no record yet (a landing the expiry check found first: the executor then asks the chain-fetch service for its record, `found_by` `executor_status`, and its amend line brings the fee, the round trip and the ledger's line). The reconciliation (§4.44) checks the executor's money against these lines by signature, and that no attempt final `not_landed_refused` ever has one.

**WP-07 review** (#6). An amend line adds `first_final` (`not_landed_refused` when the attempt was final as a refusal first, else null) and `refused_then_landed` (true when such an attempt landed after all: the executor keeps its first final, and the engine hands it to the ledger at once).

### 4.41 report/blockctx.jsonl (toolkit output): block-ordered route context per attempt

Producer: the toolkit, `arbx-report blockctx <segment>` (WP-10; F10). Revision 1 had the bot fetch a full `getBlock` for every slot of every attempt's window. Because an attempt that never lands is final only when its blockhash expires, those windows covered nearly every slot at more than twice the planned fetch rate. The bot now records the inputs once per slot (`slotidx.jsonl`, `accounts.bin`, `exec.jsonl`), and the toolkit derives the context deterministically.

- **Window:** [trigger slot, max(landed slot, last verdict slot, trigger slot + 4)] + 1.
- **Shared slots:** slots shared by several attempts are processed once.
- **Gaps:** a slot without a slot-index line, or a route-writing transaction without an `exec` record, is a gap. `--fill-gaps` fetches the full block for such a slot through `chain.py` (rate-limited, cached in `chain-cache/`).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| attempt | integer | |
| route_accounts | [pk] | |
| window | {from_slot, to_slot} | as above |
| slots | [{slot, leader, kind, tx_count, complete, txs: [{idx, sig, signer, ok, wrote: [pk], ours, jito_tipped, tip, bundle_pos}]}] | transactions that wrote a route account (from `accounts.bin` `txn`), failed transactions that touched one (from `exec.jsonl`), and ours, in block order. `bundle_pos` only when known (our `processed` results, or explorer) |
| trigger | {slot, idx} / null | |
| ours | {slot, idx} / null | |
| closer | {sig, signer, slot, idx, kind, ms_after_trigger_poh} / null | from R4 (§6.4) |
| src | enum | `recorded` (slot index + accounts + exec), `rpc-block` (gap filled), `mixed` |
| complete | bool | every slot in the window has a slot-index line, and every route-writing transaction has an `exec` record with `idx` |

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (S1-2). Each slot's transactions are ordered by one kind of position (`blockctx.order_slot`): their exec block indexes when all have one (`pos_kind` `block_index`); else their slot-index positions (the line's `pos_kind`: `nonvote_rank` keeps the order among non-votes; a line written before slotidx 1.1 is `unverified`); else the kind most of them have, the others without a position, and the slot is `pos_kind` `mixed`, `complete: false`. An `idx` merged from an `idx_update` of a line that is not `block_index` (FT-000's recordings) is not used as a block index. A gap slot filled from a full block is `block_index` unless that list holds no vote transaction (`nonvote_rank`). Slot rows carry `pos_kind`. `positions()` (R9's ladder) takes a slot's exec block indexes only where its slot-index line is `block_index` (or absent). The ladder's distances (`idx+1`, `idx+2..5`) count non-vote transactions in a `nonvote_rank` slot.

### 4.42 ledger.jsonl (new): money by signature

Producer: `src/ledger.rs`, one line per transaction in `wallet.jsonl`.

**Equity definitions** (F7). Revision 1 counted all rent as an asset in `equity_total`, yet also required rent categories to sum to the equity change. Both cannot hold for a transaction that creates an account, and the non-recoverable PumpSwap accumulator rent was hidden as an asset. Revision 2 defines:
- **Liquid equity** = native SOL + WSOL token amount.
- **Recoverable rent** = lamports held in accounts the wallet owns and can close: token accounts (for the WSOL account, its lamports minus its token amount), nonce accounts, lookup tables, and other owned accounts flagged `recoverable`.
- **Total equity** = liquid equity + Σ other tokens at the recorded mid + recoverable rent at face value.
- **Non-recoverable rent** (the PumpSwap user volume accumulator, 1,346,200 lamports, owner pAMMBay6) is never an asset. It is expensed in the transaction that creates it.
- **Which equity is used where:** stops, budgets and the main reconciliation use total equity. The liquid basis is reconciled too.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| sig, slot, idx, t_ns | | |
| attempt | integer / null | |
| kind | enum | `route`, `wrap`, `unwrap`, `ata_create`, `close`, `nonce`, `alt`, `transfer_in`, `transfer_out`, `other` (= `wallet.jsonl` `wallet_kind`) |
| ok | bool | |
| wallet_sol | lam | wallet lamport change (post − pre) |
| wsol | lam | change of the WSOL **token amount** in the wallet's WSOL account(s) (raw = lamports) |
| tokens | {mint: {amount: amt, value_lamports: lam, mid_src}} | other wallet-owned token-amount changes, valued at the recorded mid |
| fee | lam | `meta.fee` |
| tip | lam | sender_tip + cpi_tip paid by the wallet |
| rent_locked | lam | lamports the wallet put into **recoverable** accounts created in this tx (informational) |
| rent_reclaimed | lam | rent lamports returned to the wallet by accounts closed in this tx. For a WSOL account close, only its rent part; the amount part is a `wsol` decrease (informational) |
| rent_nonrecoverable | lam | lamports the wallet put into accounts created in this tx that it cannot close (an expense; a summing category) |
| liquid_delta | lam | `wallet_sol + wsol` |
| equity_delta | lam | `liquid_delta + Σ tokens.value_lamports + rent_locked − rent_reclaimed` (the change of total equity; `rent_nonrecoverable` is not added back) |
| trade_gross | lam | ok route txs: `equity_delta + fee + tip + rent_nonrecoverable`; 0 otherwise |
| trade_net | lam | `trade_gross − fee − tip` |
| categories | [{cat, lamports}] | **summing** categories of Appendix A.3. Their sum equals `equity_delta` exactly |
| stranded | [{mint, amount}] | non-quote tokens left by a route (their value is already in `tokens`) |
| check | {delivered_round_trip, diff} / null | wins: `trade_gross` against the `NXV2OUT` round trip from logs |

**Summing categories:** `trade_gross` (+), `tip` (−), `fee_win` (− fee of an ok route tx), `fee_fail` (− fee of a failed tx), `fee_other` (− fee of a non-route tx), `rent_nonrecoverable` (−), `transfer_in` (+), `transfer_out` (−), `other`.

**Informational (never summed):** `rent_locked`, `rent_reclaimed`, `liquid_delta`, `stranded`; `inventory_reval` exists in recon only.

**Identities a reader may rely on:**
- Σ summing categories = `equity_delta` on every line.
- A failed tx has `equity_delta = liquid_delta = −fee` (tips and creations revert).
- A rent-neutral route has `rent_locked = rent_reclaimed`.
- `equity_delta − liquid_delta = Σ tokens.value_lamports + rent_locked − rent_reclaimed`.

**Golden examples** (WP-07 offline tests; fee F, tip T, rent R; values from res-pnl §2 where named):

| Case | wallet_sol | wsol | rent_locked | rent_reclaimed | rent_nonrecoverable | liquid_delta | equity_delta | Summing categories |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATA create only (R = 2,039,280) | −R − F | 0 | R | 0 | 0 | −R − F | −F | fee_other −F |
| Route win with create + close, WSOL gain G | −F − T | +G | R | R | 0 | G − F − T | G − F − T | trade_gross +G, tip −T, fee_win −F |
| Route win that creates the accumulator (U = 1,346,200) and leaves a Token-2022 ATA open (R = 1,513,840; w5h-p6#69) | −F − T − U − R | +G | R | 0 | U | G − F − T − U − R | G − F − T − U | trade_gross +G, tip −T, fee_win −F, rent_nonrecoverable −U |
| Route win leaving an ATA open (R = 1,488,440) | −F − T − R | +G | R | 0 | 0 | G − F − T − R | G − F − T | trade_gross +G, tip −T, fee_win −F |
| Failed (landed) route | −F | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | −F | −F | fee_fail −F |
| Wrap X (WSOL account exists) | −X − F | +X | 0 | 0 | 0 | −F | −F | fee_other −F |
| Unwrap: close the WSOL account holding A, rent R | +A + R − F | −A | 0 | R | 0 | R − F | −F | fee_other −F |

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.ledger/1.0`, src/ledger.rs; core). The chain-fetch service turns each wallet record's transaction into the raw fields (`ledger::raw`: §4.13's INPUT 7 `ledger`); the engine values the tokens and writes the line (`ledger::entry`), once per signature. Rules, from the transaction alone: `fee` is `meta.fee` when the wallet pays it, else 0; `tip` (an ok route transaction only) is every System transfer from the wallet, top-level or inner, to an account neither made in the transaction nor a token account of the wallet; an account with no lamports before and some after is rent when the wallet funded its System create: recoverable for a token account the wallet owns (also one another payer made for the wallet: an inflow), a nonce account whose authority is the wallet, a lookup table the wallet is the authority of; non-recoverable for the PumpSwap accumulator, any other program's account and a token account of another owner; without a create it is a transfer, not rent; an account made and closed in the same transaction (WP-06's create + close) counts its create's lamports as both `rent_locked` and `rent_reclaimed`; a closed account returns rent when it was the wallet's (its token account, WSOL's lamports beyond the amount; a nonce withdrawn whole; a lookup table closed). Categories: an ok route transaction `trade_gross`, `tip`, `fee_win`, `rent_nonrecoverable`; a failed one `fee_fail` (and `other` for anything else, which a failed transaction cannot change); any other transaction `fee_other` (a failed one too: `fee_fail` stays the bot's failed landings, the Stage-0 gate's count), `rent_nonrecoverable`, and the rest as `transfer_in` or `transfer_out`. Tokens are valued at the engine's quote for selling the amount into WSOL through the registry's best pool on committed state, less `[ledger] token_haircut` (`mid_src` the pool; unpriced: 0, null). Beyond the table: `stop_delta` (the change of the stop basis: `liquid_delta`), `pre_run`, `created` and `closed` (`[{acct, lamports, rent, class (recoverable, nonrecoverable), what (token, nonce, alt, other), owner, mint, to}]`), `dup: true` on a line whose signature an earlier segment's ledger has (written, never counted twice), `check` for a win `{delivered_round_trip, diff}` (the executor's on-chain round trip of the attempt). Checked on the legacy cases (testdata/legacy/expected.json, every field and category), res-pnl's sums over the 119 landings (trade_net −2,337,448 to the lamport), wallet-synthesis §2's wins, and the synthetic create-and-close and unwrap above. `spend.json` (`arbx.spend/1.0`, the campaign's; BUDGET.md's columns): per segment and in all, each signature once and only what landed after the campaign's start, `fees`, `tips`, `rent_locked`, `rent_recovered`, `rent_nonrecoverable`, `trade_gross` (trading P&L), `trade_net`, `transfer_in` / `_out`, `wins`, `landed_fail`, `net_spend` = fees + tips + non-recoverable rent − trade_gross − other; `budget {test_budget_lamports, net_spend, remaining}`, the start and now, and `budget_md_row` (the row to paste).

**WP-07 review** (#4). What the stops and the budget count of a line (`ledger::Entry::spend`, not a field): a route's `fee + tip + rent_nonrecoverable - trade_pnl`, where `trade_pnl` = `liquid_delta + rent_locked - rent_reclaimed + fee + tip + rent_nonrecoverable` (its trading result with a token left behind at 0); any other transaction's `fee + rent_nonrecoverable` (a `transfer_in` or `transfer_out` is the owner's: no spend). A line marked `dup` (a signature an earlier segment's ledger has) is counted by no one: neither the campaign's history nor the toolkit (review #11).

### 4.43 snapshots.jsonl (new)

Producer: `src/ledger.rs`, hourly (`snapshot_min`), at start and at stop, by bounded RPC on the background runtime. Every call carries `minContextSlot` = the context slot of the first answer. If the answers' context slots differ, the snapshot is retried up to `snapshot_retries` (3) times. After that it is written with `slot_mismatch: true` and is not used for reconciliation (F34).

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns, slot | | the common context slot of the RPC answers |
| slot_mismatch | bool | true if no common slot was obtained (the snapshot is not reconciled) |
| sol | lam | native balance |
| wsol | lam | WSOL token amount |
| tokens | [{mint, account, program, amount: amt, decimals, rent_lamports, mid_lamports_per_unit, value_lamports, stranded}] | every token account. `rent_lamports` = account lamports − token amount for WSOL, else the account lamports |
| nonce_accounts | [{account, lamports}] | |
| alts | [{table, lamports}] | |
| other_owned | [{account, owner, lamports, recoverable}] | e.g. the PumpSwap accumulator (`recoverable: false`) |
| rent_recoverable | lam | Σ `tokens.rent_lamports` + nonce + ALT + `other_owned` with `recoverable: true` |
| rent_nonrecoverable | lam | Σ `other_owned` with `recoverable: false` (shown, never in equity) |
| token_value | lam | Σ `tokens.value_lamports` (excluding WSOL) |
| equity_liquid | lam | `sol + wsol` |
| equity_total | lam | `equity_liquid + token_value + rent_recoverable` |
| mids_src | {mint: {pool, slot}} | |
| rpc | {provider, calls, ms, attempts} | |

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.snapshots/1.0`). One `getMultipleAccounts` (base64, `confirmed`) per 100 accounts on the background runtime (role `ledger` since the merge with the FT-000 fixes: §4.10; `snapshot` in WP-07's lane); every call after the first carries `minContextSlot`; answers at different slots are asked again up to `snapshot_retries` times, then written `slot_mismatch` (no balance, no reconciliation, not a miss); a failure or a mismatch is taken again `snapshot_retry_s` later (the next hourly one waits for it). The accounts read: the wallet, its WSOL account, its ATAs of the mints it held at boot and of `[ledger] extra_mints` (USDC, USDT), the configured nonce accounts and lookup tables, its PumpSwap accumulator (`ledger::accumulator_of`: frz's is CUiyUSbx…), and every token account its transactions touched since. Beyond the table: `why` (`start`, `hourly`, `retry`, `stop`), `missing` (accounts read that do not exist), `not_ours` (read but not the wallet's: another authority's nonce account or table, another owner's token account; left out), `wsol_accounts`, `equity_stop` (the stop basis: `equity_liquid`), `tokens[].mid_src`. `other_owned` holds only the payer's accumulator (not recoverable). Taken at start (nothing is sent before the first: `equity_unknown`), every `snapshot_min` after the last one, and at stop.

**WP-07 review** (#3). `[ledger] snapshot_min` is 10 by default: snapshots run inside the segment, each periodic one asked for up to 30 s (a quarter of the period) before it is due, so the view is refreshed before it is `max_view_age_s` (600) old. A failed snapshot is asked again `snapshot_retry_s` later; one is also asked for at once when the chain-fetch service gives up on a wallet record (`why` `refresh`). INPUT 13 carries `t_req_ns`, the engine time it was asked for (a snapshot asked for before a give-up does not clear it).

### 4.44 recon.jsonl (new)

Producer: `src/ledger.rs`, hourly and at stop, between two snapshots without `slot_mismatch`.

| Field | Type | Semantics |
|---|---|---|
| t_ns | t_ns | |
| from, to | {t_ns, slot} | the two snapshots |
| delta_equity | lam | `to.equity_total − from.equity_total` |
| sum_entries | lam | Σ `ledger.equity_delta` for txs with slot in (from.slot, to.slot] |
| inventory_reval | lam | `(to.token_value − from.token_value) − Σ ledger tokens.value_lamports` over the window: the part of the token-value change that comes from mid changes, not from transactions |
| residual | lam | `delta_equity − sum_entries − inventory_reval`. Because of how `inventory_reval` is defined, this equals `(Δequity_liquid + Δrent_recoverable) − Σ(liquid_delta + rent_locked − rent_reclaimed)`, which does not depend on valuation |
| liquid | {delta, sum_entries, residual} | `Δequity_liquid` against Σ `liquid_delta` |
| token_amounts | [{mint, delta_snapshot, sum_entries, ok}] | raw token amounts must match exactly; a mismatch is a miss |
| tolerance | lam | 10,000 |
| ok | bool | `abs(residual) ≤ tolerance` and `abs(liquid.residual) ≤ tolerance` and every `token_amounts.ok` |
| by_category | {cat: lam} | summing categories only; informational sums are listed separately |
| unexplained | [{sig, slot, src ("getSignaturesForAddress")}] | wallet txs missing from the ledger |
| action | enum / null | `shadow` on a miss |

Rent itself can never cause a false miss. Recoverable rent sits in total equity on both sides of the identity, and non-recoverable rent is an expense category.

**As WP-07 writes it** (`arbx.recon/1.0`). Decided `recon_grace_s` after the closing snapshot (the window's records may still come); an unexplained result searches the window with `getSignaturesForAddress` (the signatures the ledger lacks are `unexplained` and asked of the chain-fetch service) and is decided once more a grace later; the stop's recon is decided at once. A window whose closing snapshot did not read an account its entries created is `skipped: "snapshot_incomplete"` (not a miss: the next snapshot reads it and closes the window). Beyond the table: `seq`, `entries`, `informational {rent_locked, rent_reclaimed, liquid_delta}`, and `executor` (the executor's money against the chain, `ledger::exec_recon`): `{landings (matched), diff (Σ (round trip − tip − fee) of the executor's landings − Σ trade_net of their entries), tolerance, ok, diffs, provisional (landings without their record yet: listed, not a miss), refused_landed, not_counted (landed per the chain, not per the executor), unknown_route_landings (a route transaction of the wallet's no attempt of this segment, nor an open, expired or refused one of the campaign's last two segments, made: another process on the wallet?), earlier_landed (an earlier segment's attempt open or expired at its end that landed: listed, not a miss; one refused that landed is in refused_landed)}`. `ok` needs both: the snapshots' residuals within `tolerance` and the executor's `diff` within it with no refused, uncounted or unknown landing. A miss: `action` `shadow` (SendGate `reconcile_miss`, for the rest of the segment; the campaign's later segments record only), `recon_miss` and, for a refused bundle that landed, `refused_bundle_landed` events.

**WP-07 review** (#6, #7, #9). Every line carries `final` (true unless a stop reconciliation was left undecided) and `at_stop`. At the stop, a window that does not pass is searched and decided once more within `recon_grace_s` inside the drain; a snapshot residual still unexplained is written `final: false`, `action` `undecided` (a `recon_undecided` Warn notice, no shadow), and the campaign's history counts no miss for it. The next segment reconciles the window from that reconciliation's first snapshot (else the previous segment's last good snapshot) to its own first snapshot, with the earlier segments' entries of the span and its own (its pre-run records): `between_segments: true`, `from.segment`, `decides` (`{segment, t_ns}` of the undecided one); its executor part lists only the earlier segments' refused attempts that landed. Lines add `owner_transfers` (`[{sig, slot, category, lamports}]`, never a miss). A refused bundle that landed is also written at once as `{kind: "refused_bundle_landed", refused_landed, ok: false, final: true, action: "shadow"}`.

**FT-001 (p1's stop snapshot).** The stop snapshot is fetched within `ledger::STOP_SNAPSHOT_BUDGET` (15 s, inside the stop sequence's 20 s wait): a failed call is asked again after a backoff (250 ms, doubling to 2 s) while the budget lasts, each call bounded by what is left of it (at most 5 s) and asking every provider in turn (`Rpc::call_bounded`, role `ledger`: critical, the bucket's next token before the backfill, the slot index and the blockhash poll). FT-001 p1's stop snapshot failed in 1.7 ms with "rpc getMultipleAccounts: no attempt before the deadline", no provider asked: the run's RPC client carried the run's stop flag (`Rpc::set_stop`, made for a stop during the boot), which the stop request sets before the stop sequence, and every bounded call then failed at once; it now ends boot-policy calls only. A stop without its snapshot (it failed within its budget, or did not come within the 20 s) is written once: `{t_ns, seq, kind: "stop_snapshot_failed", from: {t_ns, slot} (the last good snapshot) | null, to: null, ok: null (neither a pass nor a miss), final: false, at_stop: true, why, action: "next_segment", detail}`, with a Warn notice `stop_snapshot_missing`. The next segment's first snapshot reconciles the window from that last good snapshot between segments (its line's `stop_snapshot_missing: {segment, why, t_ns}` says why the window spans the earlier segment's end; `ledger::History::prev_window`), as any segment's start: it passes when the records explain the window, and is a miss only when they do not.

### 4.45 experiments.jsonl (new, Stage 1)

Producer: `src/experiments.rs`. `{t_ns, experiment, event (`config`, `block_switch`, `pause`), arms, weights, seed, scope, block: {id, arm, from_ns, to_ns}}`. Per-decision arms are in `decisions.jsonl` and `attempts.jsonl`.

**As WP-11 writes it** (`arbx.experiments/1.0`, plain, slow; opened at its first line, so no file when every experiment is off; the stream is listed in meta.json `streams` and the manifest). At the run's start, one `config` line per enabled experiment, in the order x2, x4, size, margin: `{t_ns, experiment ("x2", "x4", "size", "margin"), event: "config", key (the records' key: "x2_delay_ms", "x4_share", "size_max_sol", "margin"), control (the first arm: review #3), arms (as configured: ms, a share of the edge, SOL, lamports), weights (one per arm; equal when the table gives none), seed (the one the segment assigns with: the config's mixed with the segment's name, review #5), config_seed, run (the segment's name), scope: {max_band, kinds, venues}, unit: "decision", arm_unit, assignment}`. The arm of decision `d` in experiment `E` is `pick(xxh3_64_with_seed(E ++ 0xff ++ d as u64 LE, seed), weights)`: the hash's top 53 bits as a uniform draw over the weights' sum. Two experiments under one seed assign independently (a factorial). `block_switch` lines (`block: {id, arm_index, arm, from_ns, to_ns}`) belong to X8, whose table is refused until WP-14 builds the discovery connection; `pause` is not written. Where each arm applies (PLAN WP-11 "As built"): X4 replaces `[tips] shares` for bands 0..=`scope.max_band` in the engine's fee estimate and the executor's tip (floor, cap and the BAM floor as before); the size arm caps the engine's size search at the arm and at the executor's cap below it (canary, live, bankroll) on the money view of the last journaled decision-point record (the decision's size is the attempt's; the executor still re-quotes at its own cap, e.g. at a segment's first decision); the margin arm replaces `[send_policy] margin_floor`; X2 sleeps on the send task before the copy leaves.

### 4.46 verify.jsonl (new, Stage 1)

Producer: `src/verify_client.rs`. `{t_ns, attempt or decision, mode (`offline`, `shadow`, `gate`), ok, err, equity_delta, cu, logs_tail, latency_us, svm_slot, program_hashes}`.

### 4.47 discovery.jsonl (new, Stage 1)

Producer: `src/feeds/discovery.rs`, every 10 s: `{seq, t_ns, connection, owners, updates_delta, accounts_now, lag_ms pct, rate_limited_delta, x8_block}`.

### 4.48 Cross-run files

- `runs/signer-stats.json` (v1, `engine.rs::save_signer_stats`): `{signer: [ok, failed]}`, saved every 10 s. **As WP-04 writes it** (PLAN-ADDENDUM I-5): the configured file is read once at start (its sha256 goes to run.log and the journal's engine_init) and never written by a run; every 10 s and at stop the segment writes `<run dir>/signer-stats.json` (the whole map, usable as a next run's input) and `<run dir>/signer-stats-delta.json` (this segment's own counts). `arbx signer-stats merge --into <file> <run dir>...` adds each delta to the file (atomically) and marks the run (`signer-stats.merged.json`; a second merge is refused, and so is a run without `stop.json`, still running: WP-04 review N7). The segment's files are serialized on the recorder's writer from the statistics as loaded and this segment's counts (WP-04 review #4: the engine thread no longer builds the 30,304-signer JSON every 10 s). A pending trigger's outcome comes from its transaction's own status when an executed feed delivers it, else from a state update stamped with it (ok) or the 5 s expiry (miss), which counts only when the state was available for the whole window (`signer_miss_unknown` otherwise). Offline engines never read or write it (WP-03).
- `data/cu-table.json` (new; toolkit `cu-table`): `{schema, built_ns, source_runs: [..], legs: {"<venue>|<bins_bucket>": {n, p50, p99}}, overhead: {program, n, p99}, extras: {ata_create, ata_close, tip}, cells: {"<buy_venue>|<sell_venue>|<bins_bucket>|<creates_ata>": {limit, src (`legs` or `fallback`)}}}`. The `legs` samples come from the venue programs' `consumed X of Y compute units` log lines in every watched transaction in `exec.jsonl`, rivals included (F36). **WP-06** (`src/cu_table.rs`, `arbx.cu-table/1.0`): the engine reads `[builder] cu_table` (default `data/cu-table.json`; `""`: none, every route the fallback) once at start and journals it in engine_init's `builder.cu` (§4.13). A route's legs are keyed `<venue>|<bins_bucket>` (DLMM by the bins with liquidity the leg crosses, `0`, `1-2`, `3-5`, `6+`; other venues `na`); `cells` is informational. The route's CU is (overhead p99 + Σ leg p99 + `extras.tip`) × 1.10 rounded up to 1,000 when the overhead and both legs have `cu_min_samples` (30) samples, else the fallback formula; an ATA create and close add their `extras` p99 × 1.10 (else 30,000 and 5,000). An optional `source` names a table's provenance when it was not built from recordings. The repository's `data/cu-table.json` is an interim table without samples (every route takes the fallback, the CU asked before WP-06) until the toolkit's `cu-table` builds one from Stage-0 recordings; `arbx check` says which. On the real programs (program/venue-harness `tests/arbx_bundle.rs`, `results/arbx-bundle-runs.json`) the fallback's allowances hold what the bundles consume: the ATA create 18,616 CU (Token-2022) and 14,917 (SPL Token) against 30,000, the close 1,464 and 120 against 5,000, our route program's overhead 17,405 to 18,204 against 29,000; a create+close HOTBOT bundle used 170,507 of the 247,000 it asked.
- Campaign equity (WP-07): no separate file. `arbx campaign-equity --campaign-dir` reads the segments' `ledger.jsonl` and `snapshots.jsonl` and prints `{t_ns, equity_total_now, drop_1h, drop_24h, segments_read}` as JSON. **As WP-07 writes it** (`campaign::equity_json`, the same `ledger::history` the segments and `arbx campaign` use): also `basis`, `start` (with its components), `equity_stop_now`, `tokens_now`, `floor_lamports`, `test_budget_lamports`, `net_spend`, `budget_left`, `totals` (§4.42's sums), `recon_misses`, `unreadable_lines` (lines that did not parse: a torn last line of a killed segment) and `campaign {mode, end_reason, max_drop_per_hour_sol}`; drops are on the stop basis (SOL + WSOL).

  **WP-07 review.** `arbx campaign-equity` adds `stop_basis` (`net_spend`), `stop_value_now`, `net_spend`, `budget_left` (the test budget less the net spend), `owner_transfers` (`[{segment, sig, category, lamports}]`), `recon_undecided` and `budget_state` (`{spent, remaining, entries}` of the runs directory's budget-state.json). spend.json is `arbx.spend/1.1`: `total.trade_pnl`, `owner_transfers`, `equity.stop_value_now`; `budget_md_row`'s Trading P&L is `trade_pnl`.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it.** `wallet-cursor.json` in each segment's directory (S1-12, §4.15): `{schema: "arbx.wallet-cursor/1.0", wallet, sig, slot, carried (true: the cursor it started from, carried on because it recorded nothing newer), t_ns}`, written by the chain-fetch service when its cursor changes (at most every 10 s) and at its end; the next segment's `[chainfetch] wallet_cursor = "auto"` reads the newest sibling's for its wallet.

### 4.49 probe.json (new)

Producer: `src/probe.rs` (`arbx grpc-probe --config <file>`; F14). It is run before WP-05, and a copy goes into each segment at boot if present. `{schema, t_ns, provider_host, subs: [{sub (`accounts_full`, `slots_interslot`, `blocks_meta`, `tx_filter_1`, `tx_filter_2`, `tx_filter_5`, `blocks_account_include`, `deshred_5`), accepted, error, messages_30s, first_msg_ms}]}`. No tokens or URLs with keys appear in it.

**As WP-03 writes it** (`arbx.probe/1.0`). Each subscription is opened alone, one after the other, for `--seconds` (30): `accepted` true when the stream stayed open for the whole window; false with `error` when the provider refused it (`<gRPC code>: <message>`), could not be reached, or closed the stream early (`stream closed by the server after <ms> ms`). `messages_30s` counts account, slot, transaction, block and block-meta updates (pings excluded; deshred: transactions). Extra fields per entry: `host`, `window_s`, `n_accounts` (`accounts_full`: the candidate pools, the clock, the wallet and the programdata accounts, padded with derived keys that name no account to max(3,000, 4 × pools)), `feed` (`deshred_5`, one entry per enabled `yellowstone-deshred` feed). `blocks_account_include` asks for transactions (not accounts or entries) at confirmed commitment. Written to `[state] probe_file` (default `<out_dir>/probe.json`) and a dated copy `probe-<YYYYMMDD>.json` beside it; `[state] block_meta = "auto"` reads the `blocks_meta` entry of a probe of the same `provider_host`.

**Review WP-03 #4** (PLAN-ADDENDUM I-2, I-3: Haan grants its streams to the first process only and answers the others 503; its outages are transient). Each entry also has `verdict` (`accepted`, `refused`, `inconclusive`), `code` (the error's gRPC code, e.g. `Unavailable`, `PermissionDenied`; null without a gRPC answer) and `http_status` (the HTTP status of a reply without gRPC status, e.g. 503). An error that says nothing about what the provider supports is **inconclusive**, `accepted` null: Unavailable (HTTP 429 / 502 / 503 / 504 map to it), ResourceExhausted, DeadlineExceeded, Aborted, Cancelled, or no gRPC answer at all (transport error, not opened in time). A definite refusal (PermissionDenied, InvalidArgument, Unimplemented, Unauthenticated, ...) or an early close is `refused`. `auto` (`block_meta`, and `slot_statuses` on `slots_interslot`) is on only for `accepted` with `messages_30s` > 0. `arbx grpc-probe` refuses to start while anything may hold the provider (a live segment's control socket under `<out_dir>` at depth 1-2, a held `<out_dir>/.supervisor.lock`, an `arbx … run` or `arb-lab … run` process on the host) unless `--force`, and holds `<out_dir>/.supervisor.lock` while it runs (deploy/run-w.sh does not start a campaign meanwhile; a lone `arbx run` started during a probe is not stopped). With any inconclusive entry it writes only `probe-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>-inconclusive.json`, leaves probe.json as it was, and exits 3. meta.json `probe` lists `accepted` (accepted with messages), `refused`, `inconclusive` (with `code` and `messages_30s`) and `age_s` (how old the probe was when the segment started).

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (`arbx.probe/1.1`; Cherry's probe of 2026-10-05 02:40 UTC). A ResourceExhausted refusal whose message names a plan limit, `<LIMIT> exceeded: <requested>/<allowed>` (`TX_INCLUDE exceeded: 2/1` for the 2- and 5-account transaction filters, `BLOCK_GROUPS exceeded: 1/0` for the blocks filter), is a definite refusal: `accepted` false, `verdict` `refused_plan_limit`, `limit: {name, requested, allowed}`, the gRPC `code`; `arbx grpc-probe` prints `PLAN LIMIT`. Any other ResourceExhausted (a rate limit), Unavailable and HTTP 503 stay inconclusive. Only an inconclusive result keeps probe.json from being written: a probe whose subscriptions were all accepted or refused (plan limits included) writes it (and its dated copy). `[state] block_meta` / `slot_statuses = "auto"` read any 1.x file.

### 4.50 Replay outputs (new, WP-09): replay.json, replay-diff.json, reprice.jsonl, `arbx quote`

Producer: `src/replay.rs` (`arbx replay`, `arbx quote`; PLAN WP-09 items 1-3). Plain files in the replay's `--out` directory: a new directory (refused if it exists: two replays never share one), by default `./replay-<run>-<unix s>-<pid>` in the current directory (never inside the run, whose recording stays as the run wrote it, nor beside it: a campaign directory holds segments only). It also holds the replay's own streams, written by its recorder as a run writes them (decisions, commits, triggers, ...), except journal.bin, which is switched off (it would not be replayable: it has no boot snapshot). Everything a replay can refuse (no journal, no pools.json / registry-run.json, no engine_init, a `[strategy]` that is not the run's, an unknown `--set`) is refused, exit 2, before the directory is made.

**How a replay runs.** The engine is rebuilt as `arbx run` built it: the registry and watch list from pools.json and registry-run.json (§4.29), the run's `config.toml` (or `--config`; its `[strategy]` must hash to the journal's engine_init, `journal::replay_engine`), the boot section of the journal with its accounts.bin records, the preloaded lookup tables, and the SendGate as meta.json `send_gate` recorded it. Every record after the boot section is handed to `journal::replay` in order, with the decision-point gate records the run wrote while handling it (§4.13 "As WP-09 writes it": staged ahead of it, applied at their opportunity; the 100 ms `watch` samples are status only). journal.bin and accounts.bin are read one rotation file at a time, and an account record is held only until the journal names it. **The executor** (WP-09 with WP-05): a run with an executor is replayed with an offline executor built from its exec_init record (§4.13; `Exec::offline`: `assemble` with no RPC endpoint, no send path and no key; a dry run's would-sends are signed by a new throwaway key; WP-09 part 2: a live or canary run's sends are replayed as sent with the run's signatures, from its `sent` records, and the record's failed-fee cap and equity guard apply), the crowding map rebuilt from the record, on the replay engine's health; the config's executor sections must hash to the record's `cfg_sha256` (else refused, as for `[strategy]`). It reads the run's decision-point records, gets the run's settlements and spawns nothing: nothing is sent, simulated or asked of an RPC. Its attempts.jsonl (and sims.jsonl from the run's settlements) are written into the replay's directory. The replay's recorder is held back when its queues pass a quarter full (a replay runs hundreds of times faster than a run and must not drop; a writer that does not drain within 60 s is a recorder loss). `--reprice-sizes` reprices in the same pass (`Engine::quote_route` changes nothing the replay records).

**Whole** (review WP-09 #2). `ok` needs a whole replay: replay.json `not_whole` lists each reason it is not, `[{why, n, detail}]`: `journal_gap` (GAP records), `accounts_missing` (account records the journal names that accounts.bin lacks: their inputs were skipped), `accounts_gap` (accounts.bin GAP records), `verdict_mismatch` (ACCOUNT records whose journaled store verdict the replayed store does not give: it diverged), `run_recorder_loss` (manifest.json: `dropped_total`, `suppressed_total`, `io_lost_total` or `encode_errors_total` of journal, accounts, decisions or commits, or `lost_at_close` of the core, accounts or journal queue), `replay_recorder_loss` (the replay's own recorder dropped records), `executor_not_replayed` (engine_init `executor` true and the executor not rebuilt: no exec_init record, a recording made before the executor replay, or one of an earlier exec_init version; such a recording is never `ok`; WP-09 part 2: a live or canary executor is replayed), and, for a replayed executor, `decision_records_mismatch` (a decision-point record the offline executor read that was not its own: staged at another opportunity, unreadable, or naming another target slot or leader identity than the replay's), `decision_records_missing` (executor decisions before any record: they read the gate stopped) and `decision_records_unused` (records no decision of a replay that ran to the end read: the run decided opportunities the replay did not); WP-09 part 2: `sent_records_missing` (sends the offline executor replayed without the run's `sent` record: a live or canary recording made before part 2, or a replay that diverged).

**Digests** (review WP-09 #6). `Engine::finish` writes an events.jsonl `engine_digests` record (subsystem `engine`): `{t_engine, decisions, opportunities, before_finish: {digest, decision_digest}, digest, decision_digest}` (16 hex digits each; `before_finish`: once the last journaled record was handled, what a whole replay reaches; then after `finish` emptied the opportunities and signature windows). A replay that ran to the end without `--set` checks its own against them (`digests: {checked, same, not_checked (why not), run, replay}`); a difference makes `ok` false. A run that ended before `finish` (a crash) has none: not checked.

**Digest points** (FT-001; field test FT-001 segment p1, 2026-10-06). `finish` runs at the end of the run loop, and WP-07's stop sequence then journals more records (the drain's inputs, its housekeeping as TIMER `what` 2, the stop snapshot's INPUT 13): the "last journaled record" above is no longer the end of the journal. The p1 replay compared the run's digests, taken before the stop sequence, with its own, taken after 0.209 s of it: 55,520 of 55,520 decisions and the decision digest identical, the state digest not (its slots, fork tree, state-stream messages and crowding map had moved). Each digest point is now journaled where it is taken, as INPUT 14 `digest_point` (§4.13), with the same content as its `engine_digests` record: `{point, t_engine (the engine time of the last record handled), decisions, opportunities, digest, decision_digest, ms (how long the digests took: the stop waits for them), parts: {name: hex}, before_finish (run_end only): {digest, decision_digest, ms, parts}, counts: {engine: {counter: n}, exec: {counter: n} | null}}`. `point` `run_end` is `finish`'s (before and after its bookkeeping), `stop_end` the end of the stop sequence (`Engine::stop_sequence`: after the drain, the stop snapshot, the final reconciliation and the executor's `settle_at_stop`). `parts` are the state digest's parts in order, each digested on its own (`Engine::digest_walk`: `store.accounts`, `store.dlmm_arrays`, `store.totals`, `sigs`, `opps`, `signer_stats`, `trig_pending`, `alts`, `commit`, `forks`, `reads`, `resync`, `unverified`, `counts`, `ids`, `slots`, `health`, `state_stream`, `deferred`, `acc_queue`, `decision_hash`, and with an executor `exec.totals`, `exec.counts`, `exec.inflight`, `exec.big`, `exec.cooling`, `exec.windows`, `exec.open`, `exec.finals`, `exec.by_sig`, `exec.seen`, `exec.held`, `exec.tips`, `exec.uva`, `crowding`); folded in that order they are the state digest, whose value is unchanged (a recording made before FT-001 checks against the same value). A replay digests at each INPUT 14 record and does there what the run did there (`run_end`: `finish`; `stop_end`: the offline executor's `settle_at_stop`), then goes on with the records after it. A recording made before INPUT 14 (FT-001 p1, build 11e25d1) has one `engine_digests` record without `point`: the replay digests and runs `finish` right after its last record at or before that record's `t_engine` (the stop sequence reads the clock after `finish`, so its records come later), `how` `inferred_t_engine`; without a `t_engine` the point is unknown and the digests are not checked ("the digest point is unknown"), which is no difference. replay.json `digests`: `{checked, same, not_checked, point (run_end's how: marker, inferred_t_engine), points: [{point, how, seq (the INPUT 14 record's), t_engine, same, before_finish_same, parts_differ: [part] (null: a record without parts), before_finish_parts_differ, counts_differ: {counter: [run, replay]}, run, replay}], run, replay (run_end's), replay_end (the replay's digests at the end of the journal)}`; `same`: every point the replay reached agrees. `arbx replay` prints the points and, where one differs, its parts and counters.

**replay.json** (`arbx.replay/1.0`): `{schema, run, out, producer, build: {run (meta.json build), replay (this binary's), same_source (same commit, dirty flag and dirty_sha256; null without meta.json), same_binary}, config, pools_file, pools_sha256, pools, pairs, engine_init: {min_tip, executor, strategy_sha256, knobs}, executor_replayed (false: a run's executor not rebuilt), executor: {run, replayed, exec_init: {run, replayed, mode, why_not, cfg_sha256}, records_staged, records_mismatch, records_missing, records_unused, settled_fed, sent_missing (WP-09 part 2)}, overrides: ["key=value"], window: {from_slot, to_slot, stopped_at_seq, stopped_at_t_ns}, journal: {boot_records, records, events, timers, skipped_inputs, gate_observed, gate_staged, t_first_ns, t_last_ns, span_ms, gaps: [{first_lost_seq, n_lost, t_before, t_after}]}, accounts: {missing, verdict_mismatches, gaps: [{first_lost_aseq, n_lost}]}, wall_ms, speed_x (span_ms / wall_ms), digest, decision_digest (after Engine::finish), digests, recorder_dropped, whole, not_whole, compare (replay-diff.json's summary, or null), reprice (or null), ok}`. A replay of a recording made by another build (`same_source` false) is warned about on stderr and in the CLI's output: its differences may be the build's.

**replay-diff.json** (`arbx.replay-diff/1.0`, `--compare`): the replay's decisions.jsonl and commits.jsonl against the run's, read a zstd frame at a time (one line per side held at a time). Decisions are joined by `decision` id (main lines; the `torn_suspect` / `fork_priced` follow-up lines are compared as sets) and compared field by field: `routes` and `stages` field by field (`routes[3].net`, `stages.t_commit`), the rest whole. Not compared: `stages.t_priced` and `stages.t_decided` (the run's real clock reads; the other stage times are journaled values). A run whose executor was replayed (an exec_init record): every field is compared, its verdicts, `sent`, `would_send`, `reason_ungated`, `ev`, `rank`, `crowding`, `attempt`, `band`, `band_at_opp` and the in-flight and band outcomes (S9, S11) among them, and so is attempts.jsonl: attempt by attempt (both in attempt order; those of the decisions compared), each without the fields a replay does not reproduce (`replay::ATTEMPT_NOT_COMPARED`: `t_ns`, the stage times of the build, the signature and the send and the decision's own clock reads, the LeaderBook's `leader` snapshot, a dry run's `tx.sig`, `copies[].sig`, `dry_run.signer`, `dry_run.tx_b64` and its signature check's detail; WP-09 part 2: the send queue's `queue.lambda`, taken from the recording as PLAN WP-09 says). A run whose executor was not replayed (engine_init `executor` true, `executor_not_replayed`): its own fields (`attempt`, `ev`, `rank`, `crowding`, `sent`, `would_send`, `reason_ungated`) are counted (`executor_fields_not_replayed`) and not compared, and so are `verdict` / `reason` where the run's reason is past the engine's state and pricing stages and the replay (no executor) gave its stand-in, `no_executor` or `gate_*` (`executor_verdicts_not_replayed`); any state- or pricing-stage reason on either side is compared (a replay that says `warmup` where the run sent differs); such a recording is never `ok`. The window: decisions whose trigger slot is in [`--from-slot`, `--to-slot`] and made before the replay stopped. Commit lines are compared in order, whole. A journal gap (a GAP record) skips the decisions made between the last record before it and the first after it, and so does an ACCOUNT record whose accounts.bin record is missing (its input is skipped); every difference after the first gap is marked `after_gap`. Fields: `{schema, run, replay, t_ns, not_replayed (null when the executor was replayed or there was none), attempts_not_compared, attempt_diffs: [{attempt, decision, field, run, replay}] (at most 2,000), not_compared, window, gaps, digests, summary: {what_if, executor, executor_replayed, attempts_run, attempts_replay, attempts_compared, attempts_identical, attempts_differ, attempts_missing_in_replay, attempts_extra_in_replay, attempts_by_field (the attempt counts: null without a replayed executor), decisions_run, decisions_replay, outside_window, compared, identical, differ, missing_in_replay, extra_in_replay, skipped_gap, executor_verdicts_not_replayed, executor_fields_not_replayed, field_diffs, by_field, by_class, own_landings, first_own_landing_t_ns, diffs_after_own_landing, flags_differ, commits_run, commits_replay, commits_differ, digests_checked, digests_same, differences_ok, whole, ok}, diffs: [{decision, t_ns, field ("reason", "routes[3].net", "stages.t_commit", ...), run, replay, class (state, pricing, policy, send_slot, inputs, build, sent, other, missing_in_replay, extra_in_replay; a reason difference is classed by the earlier of its two stages), after_gap, after_own_landing}] (at most 2,000; `diffs_truncated` counts the rest), flags, first_commit_diff, whole, not_whole, ok}`. `differences_ok`: no field, flag, commit, attempt or digest difference; `ok` (here and in replay.json): `differences_ok` and a whole replay. `own_landings`: our own landed transactions the journal names (INPUT 9 `settled` with a landing; INPUT 7 `wallet_tx` is not one: an owner's top-up has it too), whose commit groups the run's engine leaves to its executor; differences after the first are marked `after_own_landing`. `arbx replay` exits 0 when ok, 1 when not (differences, or not whole, with or without `--compare`), 2 refused.

**What-if** (`--set key=value`, repeatable): `strategy.<field>` (the `[strategy]` section), `state.<knob>` and `alts.<knob>` (the engine's knobs as engine_init carries them, `engine::EngineKnobs`), `accuracy.ring_versions`, `accuracy.queue`, `min_tip`, and (WP-06) `tips.<field>` (`tips.floor`, `tips.cap`, `tips.shares` and `tips.contested_shares` as JSON arrays of five shares, `tips.bands.edges`) and `cu.min_samples` (the engine_init `builder` model, checked as `[tips]` is; `min_tip` also sets the tip floor, raising the cap to it if needed; the replayed executor tips, sets min_profit and bands by the overridden tip model too, review WP-06 #8); an unknown key is refused before anything is written. The replay then runs on the overridden strategy (its hash replaces the run's) and the compare reports the effect (`summary.what_if` true; the digests are not checked).

**reprice.jsonl** (`arbx.reprice/1.0`, `--reprice-sizes 0.5,0.25,0.125,opt`; X3, toolkit `sizes`): in the replay's own pass. A header line `{schema, run, sizes, min_tip, cu_price_micro, cap_lamports}`, then one line per opportunity of the run's decisions.jsonl (a best route with `opp` set) in the window, priced right after the input whose handling made the decision: `{decision, t_ns, slot, buy, sell, mint, size, run: {gross, net, cu, verdict, reason}, requote: {size, mid, back, spent, gross, fee, net, cu, crossed, buy_end, sell_end} (the decision's own size again), requote_same_gross (the check that the repricing read the decision's state), at: [{f, size, mid, back, spent, gross, fee, net, cu, ...} or {f, size, error}], opt: {size, gross, net, ...} (the size a decision would pick, up to the ladder's top rung: `price_route`'s own search, the ladder's rungs then arbx-sizing's locator where a rung makes money, `Engine::quote_route_best`; WP-16 merge) or null}`; replay.json `reprice` also counts `requote_same_gross`. Prices are the engine's own, as a decision prices (`Engine::quote_route`, review WP-09 #10: arbx-sizing's legs as `route_in` parses them, on the Clock sysvar's unix time and slot, else the engine's time; the DLMM bin arrays a decision leaves out left out; `spent` the buy leg's debit and `gross` = `back` − `spent`, the guard's quote delta (WP-16); `price_route`'s CU estimate and fee rule, 5,000 + `cu_price_micro` × CU / 10^6 + the run's `min_tip`). **WP-06:** the header adds `fee_rule`: `tips_by_band` (the engine_init `builder` model: the base fee + the tip of the gross's band, as decisions price, §4.32; CU from the table or the fallback) or `legacy` (a recording made before WP-06: the rule above). The executor replay needs exec_init `version` 2 (§4.13); an older recording's executor is not replayed (`executor_not_replayed`).

**`arbx quote`** (`arbx.quote/1.0`; toolkit R4, R8): `--run <dir> --route <buy,sell,mint> --version <V> --size <lamports>`, or `--batch <file>` (JSON lines `{id, route, version, size}` in, one answer line each out, in order). `V`: an accounts.bin `aseq` (`42` or `aseq:42`) or `slot:write_version` (the state right after the journal handed that record to the engine), a transaction signature (after every update stamped with it: its post-state), or `pre:<signature>` (right before the first of them: its pre-state); a version in the boot section is answered on the boot state. One pass over accounts.bin finds the versions asked and one replay pass answers them, stopped once every question is answered (each record looks its questions up: a batch costs one replay whatever its size; review WP-09 #8). Answer: `{schema, id, ok, size, mid, back, spent, gross, fee, net, cu, crossed, buy_end, sell_end, route: {buy, sell, mint}, version: {asked, at_seq, at_t_engine, tip_slot}, state: [{acct, slot, wv, aseq, txn, pending, contested, unverified}] (every account of both pools the price read, at its version; `pending`: a commit group still writes it, `contested`: its slot is contested, `unverified`: not read again since the state stream's last connection: a decision would leave such an account out, so a torn pre-state is visible)}`, or `{ok: false, error}` (unknown version, a version the journal does not reach, a route not quotable on that state). Exit 0 when every answer priced, 1 otherwise, 2 refused.

### 4.51 Alerts (Telegram): what raises them, and how they read (new, WP-20)

Producer: `src/alerts.rs`, in `arbx run` and `arbx watchdog`; `arbx alert-test` sends one test message, and `arbx notify --text` an operator's note (📝 its first line as the title, all of it escaped: a field test's goal and result, docs/field-tests/README.md). The messages are sent, not recorded: each is built from the records (events, the segment's last status line, stop.json) or from the call that raised it; what was sent, dropped or failed is in events.jsonl (§4.6) and status.jsonl `alerts` (§4.8).

**Path.** A producer calls `Alerts::raise`, which never waits: a level check, a dedup check under a lock tried a few times without sleeping (the sender takes it only while a transition is held, and to prune it once a minute; still taken, the alert goes through), a `try_send` into a queue of 1,024 (a full one drops and counts). Nothing wakes the sender: it looks every 250 ms, so no producer touches a waker's lock. A replaying thread raises nothing. One sender task on rt_bg posts `sendMessage` (`parse_mode` HTML) over HTTPS to api.telegram.org (I-10: a plain `http://` base is refused unless it is a loopback address, the tests' fake server): at most `[alerts] rate_per_min` (20) messages in any 60 s and one a second; alerts within `coalesce_ms` (2,000) of the first go out as one message (a Critical one at once), oldest first, as many whole alerts as fit under Telegram's 4,096 characters; a 429 is retried after its `retry_after` (and later messages wait too), a transport error or 5xx after 1, 2, 4, 8 s (5 tries), HTML the API refuses as plain text, a group upgraded to a supergroup at its new chat id (a run.log WARNING: update the .env); a refusal for good (a bad token, the bot not in the chat) fails the message and pauses the next one 60 s. **Dedup:** an alert whose key was sent within `dedup_s` (600) is counted, not sent, and the next one sent says `+N like it since the last`. Transitions form a family (the state stream's down and back, the Jito result stream's, the chain fetch's stall and recovery, the SendGate's shadow and live, the blockhash's stale and fresh): a change the dedup suppresses is held and sent when its window ends, unless the state went back, so the last word is never wrong for longer than `dedup_s`. Lifecycle, money and watchdog messages are never deduplicated. **The end of a run:** before the runtimes stop, the sender empties its queue (at most 4 s); after stop.json, the stop message (when its level passes `min_level`) and whatever is still undelivered go out from the main thread on a thread of their own (at most 5 s and three messages; what is left then is lost with the process, `failed_total`). A panic on the main thread (the engine's) sends a Critical alert from the panic hook (at most 3 s); if it does not go out, it is put back with the undelivered alerts and the exit sends it (the stop message is then left out: the crash alert says it). A panic elsewhere is an Error alert through the queue. These sends now honour the chat's rate the sender keeps (its window and a 429's `retry_after`) when the wait fits, and a 429's `retry_after` once; what they cannot send goes back for the next try. `arbx alert-test` and `arbx notify` send whatever `min_level` says. Every message starts with `arbx · <segment or watchdog> · HH:MM:SS UTC`; an alert sent more than a minute after it happened says when (`(at HH:MM:SS UTC)`).

**Secrets.** The token and the chat id come only from the environment (`token_env`, `chat_env`: `/root/arbx/deploy/.env`, or `--env-file`); the token goes only into the request URL. Every error text the alerts keep or write (status `last_error`, `alert_failed`, run.log, stdout) is taken without reqwest's URL and redacted: the token, its secret half and their URL-encoded forms replaced, anything shaped like a bot token masked, URLs cut to their host (`rpc::scrub`). A message's text has the token's forms removed too, and every free-text field an event brings into a message (an error, a why, a detail) has its URLs cut to the host first (`rpc::scrub`): a provider's key in a URL never reaches the chat. A `token_env` or `chat_env` that is not a variable's name (a pasted value) does not load, and the error does not repeat it. Missing variables: one run.log WARNING (`alerts off: TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN and TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID not set`), the alerts off, the run unchanged.

**What raises an alert, and how it reads.** Levels: info ℹ️, warning ⚠️, error ❌, critical 🚨 (`[alerts] min_level`: info). In the messages, `·` separates the parts of a line and `/` here separates lines.

| Alert | Level | Message | Raised by |
|---|---|---|---|
| segment start | info | 🟢 `w6-p3 started` / `shadow · gate shadow (mode_shadow)` / `build 6760f10a · config 1a2b3c4d5e6f` / `wallet FRZh…yTWS · budget left 0.0340 SOL` | `arbx run`, after meta.json |
| segment stop | info (`deadline`, `signal`, `control`); error (`error`, `state_unavailable`, a failure while stopping); critical (`panic` and every safety stop, exit 20: `equity_day`, `disk_budget`, and WP-07's `equity_hour`, `equity_floor`, `token_floor`) | ⏹ `w6-p3 stopped: deadline (exit 0)` / `--minutes 60 reached` / `ran 1h02m · gate shadow (mode_shadow)` / `decisions 12,345 · opps 87 · would-send 40` / `sent 2 · wins 1 · net +0.000123 SOL` (the last status line's numbers, §4.8) | `end_run`, after stop.json |
| crash | critical | 🚨 `CRASH: w6-p3 panicked (thread main)` / `src/engine.rs:123: <the panic message>` | the panic hook |
| panic elsewhere | error | ❌ `panic in thread arbx-hot (the run goes on)` / `<file:line>: <message>` | the panic hook |
| a route that succeeded | info when its net is 0 or above, warning below | 💰 `succeeded · net +0.001145 SOL`; ➖ `succeeded · net 0 SOL: no profit after fee, tip, rent`; 🔻 `succeeded but lost: net -0.00036884 SOL after fee, tip, rent`; ✅ `succeeded · net not known yet` (the wallet record brings it: a second message with the net follows) / `PumpSwap>Dlmm · BONK DezX…B263` / `size 0.5000 · gross +0.00125 · tip 0.0001 · fee 0.000005 · rent 0.00151384` / `slot 371,234,567 · solscan` (a link). The net is the realised one from chain data: the route program's round trip minus the tip, the fee and the rent the transaction left locked in accounts it created (none for WP-06's create+close in the same transaction). Stage-1 plan review F12: a landing says "succeeded", never "profitable", and reads as a gain only when that net is above 0 | `alerts::win` (WP-05's settlement, `landed_ok`; wired at the integration: `Exec::finalize` and `amend`, `landing_alert`). Integration: the gross is the route program's round trip; the fee, tip and rent are the ledger's line of the same transaction when the wallet record brought one (its rent: created less closed, recoverable or not), else the record's fee, the attempt's tip and the estimate; with no round trip in the events, the ledger's change of SOL + WSOL plus what was paid; a landing found late, or told before its record brought the net, is told again with it |
| failed landing | error | ❌ `failed landing · fee 0.000005 SOL lost` / route · mint / `size … · <program error>` / `slot … · solscan` (one key: a run of them is one message per `dedup_s`, with the count) | `alerts::failed_landing` (`landed_fail`) |
| loss | warning | 🔻 `loss · net -0.0012 SOL` / route · mint / `size … · tip … · fee … · rent …` / why | `alerts::loss` (WP-07's ledger) |
| budget floor | critical | 🛑 `BUDGET FLOOR (failed_fee_cap): sending stops` / `at 0.0340 SOL · floor 0.0340 SOL` / `the executor stopped sending: failed-fee cap reached` | the status line's `exec.stopped`; `alerts::budget_floor` (the executor's `sender_state` when the failed-fee or spend cap stops it, at once: integration; WP-07's equity floor comes as its notice) |
| low balance | warning | 🪫 `low balance · liquid SOL + WSOL 0.0400 SOL` / `warn below 0.0500 SOL` | `money.equity_liquid_now` (WP-07) under `[alerts] low_balance_sol`; `alerts::low_balance` |
| state stream down / back | error / info | ❌ `state stream down: nothing is priced or sent` / `why stream down · last message 12s ago · reconnects 3` / `error <scrubbed>`; ✅ `state stream back` / `down for 2m · reconnects 3` | events `state_unavailable`, `state_available` |
| state never delivered | critical | 🚨 `state stream unavailable: the segment stops` | `state_unavailable` with `why` `boot_timeout` or `unavailable_stop` |
| a re-read that does not land | warning | ⚠️ `state re-read has not landed: the gate stays shut` / `why reconnect · chunks 3/12 · accounts 1,200` / `error …`; ⚠️ `gave up re-reading accounts` | `state_resync_slow`; `read_gave_up` |
| recorder | error (a core or replay stream, an I/O error, a dead or stalled writer); warning (other drops, an encoder); critical (`recorder_fatal`) | ❌ `recorder lost a core record: sending stops for the rest of the segment` / `stream attempts · class core · dropped 1 · why queue full`; ⚠️ `recorder dropped records`; ❌ `recorder I/O error` / `stream … · op … · records lost …` / `error …`; ❌ `recorder writer died`, `recorder writer stalled`; 🚨 `recorder stopped the segment` | `recorder_drop`, `recorder_io_error`, `recorder_encode_error`, `recorder_writer_dead`, `recorder_writer_stalled`, `recorder_fatal` |
| disk ladder | warning (soft steps); critical (`shadow_and_stop`) | 💾 `disk budget: stop_rivals` / `used 37.1 GB · budget 40.0 GB · free 30.2 GB` | `disk_action` |
| chain fetch | error / warning / info | ❌ `chain fetch died: sending stops for the rest of the segment`; ⚠️ `chain fetch stalled`; ✅ `chain fetch recovered` | `chainfetch_dead`, `chainfetch_stalled`, `chainfetch_recovered` |
| boot failed | error | ❌ `boot failed: no RPC provider answered` / `method … · attempts …` / `last …` | `rpc_boot_failed` |
| Jito | error / warning / info | ❌ `Jito auth token renewal failed` (`: no valid token, sends are refused` when the cached one is stale) / `retry in 2 s` / `error …`; ⚠️ `Jito result stream down`; ✅ `Jito result stream back` | `jito_token_refresh` with `ok: false`; `jito_stream_down`, `jito_stream_up` |
| WP-07's notices | WP-07's (`Warn` is warning) | its text as the title: 🛑 `equity_floor`, `equity_hour`, `equity_day`, `token_floor` (e.g. `equity_floor: <detail> (segment w6-p3): sending stopped, campaign stops`); ❌ `recon_miss` (`reconciliation miss (segment …): <why>: sending stopped`), `equity_unknown`, `segment_start_failed`; 🪫 `low_native_sol`; 🪙 `stranded_token`; 🟢 `campaign_start`, `equity_start`; 🚦 `campaign_shadow`, `segment_not_sending`; ⏹ or 🚨 `segment_end` (crash or safety class only), `campaign_end` | `alerts::notice_hook` (`alerts::notice_alert`), WP-07's `notify` hook: installed by `arbx run` for the engine (the ledger, the in-process supervisor) and by `arbx campaign` for its loop (its own alerts: integration). Keyed `<kind>:<subject>` and deduplicated by it (`dedup_s`; a repeat at the level last sent is counted, a higher level passes); `equity_stale` and `equity_fresh` (Error, Info) are one transition family. The recorder's tap leaves these kinds alone, so none is told twice |
| 503 / 429 storm | warning | 🌩 `unavailable (503) storm on state: 3 failed connections in 5 min` / `last <redacted error>`; 🌩 `rate-limit (429) storm: haan refused 45 calls in 60 s` | a gRPC stream's failed connections (the state stream and every gRPC feed, `alerts::note_stream_error`: three within 5 minutes that read 503 / `Unavailable`, which tonic also gives any connect, DNS or TLS failure, or 429 / rate limits); the RPC registry's rate-limited answers of one provider (30 within a minute) |
| SendGate | info (`control`, `mode_shadow`, `forced_shadow`, `dry_run`, back to live); warning (`state_slo`, others); error (`core_record_loss`, `recorder_unhealthy`, `disk_budget`, `chainfetch_dead`, `program_hash_mismatch`); critical (an equity, budget, fee-cap, `token_floor` or reconciliation reason) | 🚦 `SendGate shadow: core_record_loss` / `was live · for the rest of the segment` / `<detail>`; 🚦 `sending resumed: SendGate live` / `was shadow (control)` | a watcher of the gate (every second, every change of mode or reason after its first look; a stop is left to the stop message; with WP-07's notices hooked, the supervisor's rule reasons `equity_floor`, `equity_hour`, `equity_day`, `token_floor` and `reconcile_miss` are left to their notices, integration; `equity_stale` is error). It reads the gate's change count (`SendGate::changes`, lock-free) and takes the gate's lock only after a change: the engine takes that lock on every send decision, and the watcher runs on the niced rt_bg |
| stale blockhash | warning / info | ⚠️ `stale blockhash: 60 slots old (max 20): sends are refused (blockhash_stale)`; ✅ `blockhash fresh again` | the last status line, every 15 s: `health.blockhash_age_slots_now` above `[send_policy] max_blockhash_age_slots` twice in a row while the state is fine, or none after there was one; none at all for 2 minutes only in a segment that sends (a shadow run without a blockhash source is no fault) |
| heartbeat | info | 💓 `w6-p3 up 13m` / `shadow · gate shadow (mode_shadow) · state ok` / `last 1h00m: decisions … · would-send …` / `sent … · wins … · net … (wallet records)` / `budget left … · alerts sent …` | every `heartbeat_min` (60) across segments (the marker `runs/.alerts-heartbeat`, written at its first look): the current segment's gate and state, and the numbers of the last period over every segment of the runs directory, so segments shorter than the period still report |
| daily summary | info | 📊 `daily 2026-10-04 (UTC)` / `win ratio 1.2% (12 wins / 1,003 sent) · net +0.0031 SOL (ledger)` (or `win ratio: no sends (0 wins / 0 sent) · net …`) / `fees 0.005015 · tips 0.0012 · rent 0.0000 SOL` / `budget left 0.0290 SOL · segments 24 · crashes 1` | at 00:00 UTC, for the day before, by the first segment or watchdog to claim it (the marker `runs/.alerts-daily-<day>`; markers older than 8 days are removed); nothing for a day without a segment |
| watchdog | info / critical | 👀 `watchdog started on cherry` / `runs /root/arbx/runs · grace 120 s · running every 60 min`; ✅ `running: w6-p3` / `up 2h13m · status 4s ago` / `gate …` / `sent … · wins … · net …`; ❌ `arbx NOT running` / `<why>` / `watchdog on cherry`; ❌ `arbx still NOT running (1h00m)`; ✅ `back up: w6-p4 (down 12m)`; ⏹ `watchdog stopped on cherry (a signal)` | `arbx watchdog` (§4.52) |

**The daily summary's numbers** (docs/STAGE1-PLAN.md §1 "What you will see"; F12), from each segment's status lines over the UTC day (a `_total` counter's last value in the day minus its last value before it, §2.2): a **win** is a landed bundle whose route succeeded (`exec.ok_total`); **sent** is live bundles sent, `exec.sent_total` only (never `attempts_total`, which counts would-sends that were never sent); the ratio is wins ÷ sent, with no fallback: the line reads `win ratio 1.2% (12 wins / 1,003 sent) · net +0.0031 SOL (ledger)` (two decimals under 1%, `0.30%`), always with both numbers and the net, or `win ratio: no sends (0 wins / 0 sent) · net …` when `sent_total` is missing or 0; the **net** is after fees, tips and rent, from chain data only, and names its source: `ledger` (WP-07: `money.trade_net_total − money.rent_nonrecoverable_total`, since §4.42's `trade_net` is `trade_gross − fee − tip` and non-recoverable rent is its own expense line) or `wallet records` (WP-05's counters from our wallet's transaction records: `round_trip_total_lamports − tips_paid_total_lamports − fees_landed_total_lamports − rent`, `≥` while a win's round trip is unknown), with rent `rent_nonrecoverable_total` (the ledger) or `new_token_accounts_total × 1,513,840` lamports (an estimate, `≈`: the larger of an SPL account's 1,488,440 and a Token-2022 account's 1,513,840, so the net is never overstated); a line with neither has no net (`?`); fees and tips are the ledger's `fees_win_total + fees_fail_total` and `tips_total`, else WP-05's `fees_landed_total_lamports` and `tips_paid_total_lamports` (read at `money.<name>`, else at `money.bot.<name>`, where WP-07 puts the executor's counters once the ledger owns `money`); **budget left** is WP-07's test budget left (`money.budget_left_now`) of the newest segment, else its failed-fee cap minus its failed fees; crashes are the day's stop.json reasons `error`, `panic`, `state_unavailable`, a boot that failed before any status line included. The heartbeat sums the same way over its period; the stop message reads the segment's last status line.

### 4.52 watchdog.jsonl (new, WP-20)

Producer: `src/watchdog.rs` (`arbx watchdog --config <file>`), a separate process (docs/field-tests/README.md).
