Plain JSON lines in the runs directory it watches (`[watchdog] runs_dir`, else `out_dir`), no header; the same lines go to its stdout with a UTC time. `{t_ns, event, …}`: `start` `{pid, runs_dir, alerts}`; `note` `{msg}` (the alerts' notes: on, or off and why); `running`, `down`, `still_down`, `back_up` `{segment, why, sent}` (each message of its state machine; `sent`: whether alerts are on); `daily` `{day, segments}`; `stop` `{alerts}` (the alerts' counters, §4.8's fields). `<runs_dir>/.watchdog.lock` holds the pid of the watchdog holding its `flock`, and is emptied when that watchdog ends (still under the lock): a second watchdog on the same runs directory refuses to start, and `flock -n <runs_dir>/.watchdog.lock true || kill $(cat <runs_dir>/.watchdog.lock)` stops the running one and never a process that reused an old pid.

**How it decides.** Every `poll_s` (10) it finds the live `arbx… run` processes in /proc (argv: a binary whose name starts with `arbx`, then `run`; not a zombie) and the segment directory each writes (`--campaign-dir` and `--name`, else its open run.log, events.jsonl or status.jsonl), kept when it is under the runs directory. A segment is up when its status.jsonl was written within `stale_s` (60; the engine adds a line every 10 s), or while it boots without one for at most `boot_s` (600). "✅ running" at its first look that finds one, then every `status_min` (60); "❌ NOT running" when no segment process has been seen for more than `grace_s` (120: it covers the gap between two segments; the message names the last segment's stop.json reason), when the running segment's status.jsonl is older than `stale_s`, or when it boots for longer than `boot_s`; "❌ still NOT running" every `status_min` while that lasts; "✅ back up" when it recovers. It also sends the daily summary when no segment claimed it yet (§4.51). `arbx watchdog --once` prints one look's verdict as JSON (`{runs_dir, state: running | booting | not running, segment, pid, dir, status_age_s, why, live, last_end}`; exit 0 running or booting, 1 not) and sends nothing.

---

### 4.53 The pool refresh's report and inputs (new, WP-22)

Producer: `arbx pools refresh` (src/poolrefresh.rs), by hand or by `arbx campaign` during each segment (§4.1). Three files: the new seed list (`--out`: `venue,pool,source`, the kept rows in their order with their source, then the added ones tagged `wp22:<records|gt|ds>:<segment>`), the report (`--report`, default `<out>.report.json`) and the inputs file beside it (`<report>.inputs.json`, `arbx.pool-refresh-inputs/1.0`: the current list, the registry pools fetched, each segment's records as read, the candidates, every account fetched; **WP-22 review #7:** also `policy`, `segment`, `failure` (why the fetch failed: the refresh then kept the previous list), `current_sha256` and `segment_min`; `arbx pools refresh --inputs <file>` plans from it alone and writes the same list, keeps the previous list again when it records a failure, and refuses a config with another `[pool_refresh]` policy or another current list). The list is written first, then the report naming its sha256 (both atomically).

**The report** (`arbx.pool-refresh/1.0`): `{schema, segment, status (ok, kept_previous), reason (why the previous list was written: an RPC failure, the deadline), t_start_ns, t_end_ns, duration_ms, phases {records_ms, verify_ms, plan_ms}, policy, current {path, sha256, rows, unreadable_rows}, out {path, sha256, rows}, sources {records {segments, unlisted_pools_seen, candidates, status}, geckoterminal {requests, ok, failed, items, ms, status (ok, partial, failed, none, off), errors}, dexscreener {... , tokens, tokens_seen}}, rpc {endpoints, max_rps, calls, accounts, ms}, records [{segment, complete, ok, listed, active_pools, unlisted_pools, streams {triggers, opportunities, decisions, rivals, exec: {files, lines, bytes, error, deadline_stop}}}], counts {current, rows, kept, kept_active, kept_idle, kept_unconfirmed, idle_held, added, added_by_source, dropped, dropped_by (dead:missing, dead:owner, dead:layout, idle, cap), candidates, candidates_by_source, rejected_by (missing, venue, layout, mints, quote, vault, liquidity, mint_missing, transfer_fee, transfer_hook, mint_layout, group, in_registry, max_adds, cap, cap_group, address)}, added [{venue, pool, token, quote, source, src, via, score, symbol, quote_liquidity}], dropped [{venue, pool, why, source, activity / idle_segments}], kept [{pool, why (active, idle, unconfirmed_dead), activity, idle_segments, rpc (what the verification answer said of an unconfirmed_dead pool)}], shrink_guard {idle_candidates, idle_dropped, idle_held, idle_bound, floor_rows, applied}, segments_counted_for_idle [{segment, counts, complete, covered_min, span_min, elapsed_min, minutes}], segment_min, process {nice, io_class}, top_tokens [{token, symbol, pools, score}], rejected [{pool, venue, src, via, why}] (the first 300), inputs_file, inputs_sha256, inputs_error (null, or why the inputs file could not be written: the list does not depend on it)}`. The output directories are created first. A `kept_previous` report has only `counts {current, rows, kept, added: 0, dropped: 0}` beside the common fields.

**What counts.** Activity per listed pool and segment: its triggers.jsonl lines (`pool`), decisions.jsonl lines (the trigger's `pool`), opportunities.jsonl lines (`buy_pool`, `sell_pool`) and rivals.jsonl rows (`pools`); a segment is `ok` when its triggers and rivals were read whole and it recorded at least one trigger (a segment whose state stream was down says nothing about its pools). A pool is idle in a segment that listed it (its pools.json) with no activity; `idle_segments` consecutive such segments drop it, each `ok` and **(WP-22 review #3a)** with triggers spanning at least 80% of its minutes (`span_min`: from meta.json `run.started_ns` to the last trigger's `st.rx`; a complete segment's minutes are the campaign's `--segment-min`, else its own `--minutes`, else it does not count; a running segment's are those elapsed when the refresh read it, `elapsed_min`, at least half a segment) and a trigger in at least 80% of the minutes of that span (`covered_min`: the UTC minutes holding a trigger). **Shrink guard (#3b):** one refresh drops at most max(25, a tenth of the list) idle pools, the longest idle first (then the least active, then the list's order), and never so many that the list falls under 80% of its rows; the others stay, idle and last in priority (`idle_held`). **Dead pools (#2):** a listed pool the verification answer calls dead (missing, another owner, a layout no decoder reads) is dropped only when the last segment's boot had dropped it too (no pools.json of the most recent segment lists it) and the most recent segment saw no activity on it; otherwise it is kept (`unconfirmed_dead`): the next boot re-checks it on its own provider. More such pools than max(25, a twentieth of the list) in one refresh (without any boot record: more dead pools than that) keeps the previous list (the RPC or a decoder is suspect). Pools are added on PumpSwap, Raydium CPMM, Raydium AMM v4, Meteora DLMM and DAMM v2 only, whatever venue the account's owner names (#10); registry pools count toward a token's group only with `[pools] registry_all` (#9). The refresh's environment holds only the variables its verification endpoints name (never a HAAN one, #8) and it runs at nice +10 with the I/O class idle (#12). New pools from our records: exec.jsonl lines with `arb_like.is` and `ok` true (landed rival arbitrage), every swap on our venues (top-level or inner) read at the decoders' account positions, pools the segment did not list (the last `exec_segments` segments; at most 5 × `max_adds` candidates, the most transactions first).

## 5. Volume and disk budget

**Measured inputs** (revision 2, from arb-lab recordings of the w5h registry, 300-377 moving pools):
- 3.72 slots/s, with at least one watched write in every slot;
- 31-60 watched transactions/s, of which 41-48% touched a PumpSwap pool (PLAN §0).

**Estimates** (est.) from arb-lab's long-1-p1: 34 account updates/s, 669 forwarded tx/s; `exec.jsonl.zst` 227 MB/h for the whole PumpSwap program; `rivals` 75 MB/h; `txarr` 72 MB/h; `poh` 72 MB/h; `alts.jsonl` 157 MB raw. These are scaled to arbx's registry: 200-500 updates/s and 50-150 watched transactions/s for about 1,000 pools.

| Stream | Per hour (compressed) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| accounts.bin | 0.1-0.6 GB | XOR deltas make bin-array updates small; keyframes every 64 |
| journal.bin | 0.5-1.0 GB | forwarded transactions' bytes; needed for deterministic replay |
| exec.jsonl | 0.3-0.8 GB | full meta, logs and keys for watched transactions, all venues (PumpSwap from the stream, the rest backfilled) |
| slotidx.jsonl | 20-60 MB | about 13,400 slots/h; only the tracked signatures' positions are stored |
| wallet.jsonl | < 10 MB | |
| txarr, poh, rivals | 0.2-0.3 GB | as arb-lab. rivals (WP-08, plan addendum I-9): cmp2's arbx wrote 238 MB/h at zstd-3 (1.29 GB/h raw, 88% `why = venues` rows); keeping 1 in 10 of those (`[record] rivals_venues_sample`) leaves 54 MB/h (266 MB/h raw) |
| validators | < 1 MB | WP-08: about 1,000 lines of about 650 bytes every 10 minutes (4 MB/h raw) |
| decisions, commits | 0.05-0.15 GB | |
| entries-<feed> | 1-2 GB per archived feed | bulk; off by default unless a study needs it |
| everything else | < 0.1 GB | |
| **Total without entries** | **about 1.5-3 GB/h** | **about 35-70 GB/day** |

Defaults: `disk_budget_gb = 40` per segment (a 20-minute segment uses 0.5-1 GB) and `min_free_gb = 25`. A 24 h campaign needs 40-70 GB; Cherry had 131 GB free (res-stops). Finished segments should be copied off the host daily (an owner task). The bot never deletes recorded data.

**As the FT-000 fixes write it** (S1-5; measured on FT-000's segment 1, `docs/field-tests/FT-000-early-shadow.md`). The run wrote 4.58 GB/h compressed (110 GB/day): exec 2,585 MB/h, journal 1,642, decisions 84, txarr 79, rivals 69, accounts 36, commits 34, slotidx 13, the rest 38. In its exec.jsonl sample (`exec.055`, 13,290 records), 10,222 records (76.9%; 71.5% of the bytes) touched no watched account by §4.14's rule. Keeping those 1 in 20 (`[record] exec_unwatched_sample`) projects exec.jsonl to about 830 MB/h and the whole run to about 2.8 GB/h, **about 68 GB/day** (from 110): a 24 h campaign fits the 112 GB Cherry has above `min_free_gb`, with daily copies off the host still needed for longer ones. The journal (1.6 GB/h, every forwarded transaction in full) is now the largest stream.

**RPC volume of the chain-fetch service** (WP-03b), for sizing PLAN OD-1. The slot index, the sample and the poll go before the backfill in every bucket they share (§4.10); the backfill runs at what its providers have spare (status `chainfetch.cap_rps_now`). With the deploy split (Haan 4/s: wallet, blockhash, backfill; Shyft 4/s: slotidx, sample, backfill, wallet) and P1's load (3.7 slots/s, 30 uncovered signatures/s; test `the_slot_index_keeps_up_while_the_backfill_has_demand`), the slot index keeps up (lag at most a slot or two) and the backfill gets about 3.9/s, nearly all of it on Haan:

| Role | Rate | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| slotidx | about 3.7 getBlock/s (signatures only) | about 100-300 KB each (est.), so roughly 0.4-1.1 MB/s |
| backfill | about 16-35 getTransaction/s on the w5h registry, plus failed watched transactions; 2-3× for about 1,000 pools | sized automatically (`need_rps_now`); 0 if a second filter or provider covers the other venues |
| blockhash | at most 2.5 getLatestBlockhash/s, and none while the slot index's confirmed blockhashes are fresh | small |
| wallet | one getSignatureStatuses every 2 s while attempts are in flight, plus one getTransaction per landed transaction of ours | |
| sample | 1 full getBlock/min | about 6 MB each (slot 453,032,285: 6.15 MB for 1,193 transactions, base64): about 6 MB a minute parsed in the blocking pool, then about 1,200 `txjson::from_rpc` conversions on the service task |
| snapshots, leaders | a few per hour, 1 per 100 slots | |

---

## 6. Analysis toolkit (`tools/arbx-report`)

### 6.1 Architecture

```
tools/arbx-report/
  arbx_report/
    streams.py       readers: header check, zstd frames, rotation series, legacy v1 files, binary formats (§4.12, §4.13, §4.25)
    model.py         typed rows and joins (attempt ↔ decision ↔ outcome ↔ ledger ↔ blockctx ↔ verdicts)
    chain.py         optional fetches (getBlock, getTransaction, explorer) with a cache in <seg>/chain-cache/
    quote.py         calls `arbx quote` (Rust, WP-09) to re-price a route at a recorded state version
    reports/         r0_quality.py … r13_compare.py (§6.4)
    stats.py         percentiles, bootstrap CIs, binomial / Fisher / Mann-Whitney tests, one-sided trend tests
    render.py        report.json + report.md
  tests/             synthetic fixtures, golden outputs, legacy known-answer tests
  __main__.py        CLI
```

- Python 3, standard library only; uses `zstandard` if installed, else pipes `zstd -dc`.
- **CLI** (also installed as the `arbx-report` script): `python3 -m arbx_report <command> <segment-or-campaign-dir> [--rpc-url-env NAME] [--offline] [--out DIR]`. Commands: `segment`, `campaign`, `quality`, `ledger`, `funnel`, `attempts`, `blockctx [--fill-gaps]` (§4.41), `attribution`, `latency`, `leaders`, `feeds`, `predictions`, `rivals`, `experiments [x2|x4|size|margin|x5|x8|x9|all]` (a segment or, WP-11, a campaign directory), `census` (X1), `sizes` (X3 summary), `sizefit` (X6 summary), `caps`, `cu-table`, `compare A B`.
- **Dependencies on the bot** (F35): `quote.py` needs the `arbx` binary with `arbx quote` (WP-09). R4, the all-swap part of R8, and X3 cannot run before WP-09. Without the binary these sections are marked incomplete, never computed approximately.
- **Secrets:** an RPC URL is read only from the environment variable named by `--rpc-url-env`. The toolkit never opens `.env` files. `--offline` forbids every network call; reports then mark chain-dependent sections as incomplete.
- **Determinism:** the same files (and the same `chain-cache/`) give byte-identical `report.json`. Every report records the input files' sha256 (closed files) or size and last record (open files).
- **Outputs:** `<seg>/report/report.json` (machine), `report.md` (human), one section per report with its coverage caveats on top. Campaign reports aggregate segment reports plus campaign-level joins (late landings across segments).

### 6.2 Correctness rules (every report)

1. **Counters.** `_total`: take the last line of the segment; `_delta`: sum the lines; gauges: never sum. Across segments, sum each segment's last `_total`. A `seq` gap is reported. (arb-lab's cumulative `failed_fees_lamports` was mis-summed.)
2. **Money comes from the ledger** (`ledger.jsonl`, `snapshots.jsonl`, `recon.jsonl`), never from status counters. Counters are shown only as a cross-check with the difference.
3. **Rent is separate from trading.** Trading results use `trade_net`. Recoverable rent locked and reclaimed are reported on their own and move liquid equity but not total equity. Non-recoverable rent is an expense line (§4.42).
4. **Land rates** use attempts as the denominator (copies with `t_sent > 0` and `ok`), never `getSignaturesForAddress` (D §4.13).
5. **Acceptance is not landing.** Report accepted → landed separately, by leader kind (146 accepted against 17 landed in the arb-lab window).
6. **Landed-only caveat.** A bundle that overprices its gap fails its check and never lands, so realised/expected over landed attempts cannot show overpricing (W §3). Such ratios are printed with this caveat and beside would-fail statistics.
7. **Attribution uses block order** `(slot, idx)` and state change (§6.4 R4), not arrival time and not "who profited". Arrival time is used only for "seen before our send". PoH milliseconds are compared only on the same leader's clock.
8. **Missing is not zero.** A row without a value is excluded from that statistic and counted in the coverage line; no `except: pass` (arb-lab's supervisor turned a bad status line into 0 spent).
9. **Time windows.** Wall hours = `meta.started_ns` → `stop.ended_ns`; trading hours = warm-up ready → stop, minus shadow periods from `events.jsonl`. Rates per hour state which one they use.
10. **Medians beside means, and concentration.** Every money total shows the median per event and the top-1 share; a single jackpot (like the bolt trade) is flagged when the top-1 share exceeds 50%.
11. **Units.** Lamports as integers; SOL only for display; USD only with the rate stated (from meta or the command line). USDC-quoted results use the recorded SOL/USDC mid.
12. **Schema versions.** Readers refuse unknown major versions; minor versions may add fields; legacy v1 files go through the legacy reader, and incomparable fields are named (§7).
13. **Joins by full signature.** `sig8` joins (txarr, poh) check for collisions and report them.
14. **Segments end with in-flight attempts.** Campaign reports join late landings from later segments' `wallet.jsonl` by signature; segment reports list them as unsettled.

### 6.3 R0 Data quality (runs first; every other report cites it)

- Schema versions and producer binaries per file; missing streams.
- Recorder drops per stream (`manifest.json`). Core drops are flagged as a segment fault; replay-class gaps are listed with their `aseq`/`seq` ranges.
- **Exec coverage** against the denominator D1 ∪ D2 (§4.14):
  - by venue and by `found_by`, within 60 s and within 10 minutes, with and without `idx`;
  - the sampled unseen share with its sample count;
  - `need_rps` against `have_rps`.
- **Other coverage:**
  - attempts with verdicts, leader, `blockctx` complete, and a PoH trigger position;
  - wallet transactions against `getSignaturesForAddress` (if online), and `wallet_update_missing`;
  - slots with a slot-index line, and `idx_check` mismatches;
  - feed uptime and result-stream outages (`jito_stream_down`);
  - lookup tables given up.
- **Commit groups** by `w_src`, `late_member` and `torn_suspect` counts, and `fork_priced`.
- Clock sync offset (meta); status `seq` gaps; unsettled attempts and amends; reconciliation failures; snapshots with `slot_mismatch`.

### 6.4 Reports and how each is computed

**R1 Money** (`ledger.jsonl`, `snapshots.jsonl`, `recon.jsonl`, `outcomes.jsonl`). Equity at start and end; Δ equity total and liquid; Σ categories; trade net, rent (locked, reclaimed, non-recoverable), fees on wins and on failures, tips; each reconciliation window with its residual. Splits by band, venue pair, leader kind, experiment arm. SOL/h on trading hours with medians and top-1 share. Cross-check: status `money` and per-attempt `ledger` sums must equal the ledger; any difference is printed. WP-11: the money counts the ledger lines as the Rust side does (`ledger::history`: spend.json, the equity stops, the status money): no `dup` line, and nothing at or before the campaign's start, its first good balance snapshot in segment order (`ledger.start_slot`; a segment opened alone finds it through the campaign.json beside it, else uses its own first snapshot); what is left out is reported apart (`ledger.before_start_skipped`). The `pre_run` lines of a campaign's first segment (the wallet's last hour before its stream: the previous campaign's last trades) are before the start: counting them, as the toolkit did, gave FT-006 p1 +49,689, FT-007 p1 +51,440 and FT-008 p1 +111,648 lamports that were not theirs; the toolkit's campaign totals now match the FT entries' (FT-006 +232,600, FT-007 +235,938). A later segment's `pre_run` line after the start (a landing the previous segment's wallet stream missed) counts, as in spend.json.

**R2 Funnel** (`commits`, `decisions`, `attempts`, `outcomes`, `triggers`). Hints → commit groups (by outcome) → evaluations → routes priced → ≥ min_profit → each rejection reason (Appendix A.1) → sent → first verdict (accepted, would-fail by code, lost-auction, none with the stream up, none with the stream down) → landed → ok. Counts and per-hour rates; status `_delta` sums as a cross-check.

**R3 Attempts and Jito** (`attempts`, `outcomes`, `bundles`, `copies`, `leaders`). Would-fail rate by band × send delay (`t_sent − t_first_shred`, bucketed 0-5, 5-10, 10-20, 20-50, 50+ ms) × leader kind; time to first verdict; verdict sequences (e.g. accepted → would-fail, R §3.4's 130 cases); accepted → landed by leader kind, region and RTT bucket; lost-auction bids against our tips; blockhash age against `blockhash-not-found`; queue waits and λ; rate-limit refusals.

**R4 Attribution: who closed each gap** (`decisions`, `attempts`, `accounts.bin`, `exec.jsonl`, `slotidx.jsonl`, `report/blockctx.jsonl`, `txarr`, `poh`). Needs `arbx quote` (WP-09). For each attempt (and each opportunity ≥ min_profit):
1. Start from the decision's state version (route accounts at their `aseq`).
2. List, in block order `(slot, idx)`, every later transaction that wrote a route account, through the `blockctx` window (§4.41). The transactions come from `accounts.bin` (the `txn` of each version), with positions from `slotidx.jsonl` and `exec.jsonl`.
3. After each one, re-price the route at the attempt's size on the post-transaction state (`arbx quote --run <seg> --route … --version …`).
4. The **closer** is the first transaction after which net ≤ the attempt's on-chain min_profit. If our transaction landed first and won, the closer is us. If the route never closes in the window, the result is `persisted` (the would-fail then points to state lag, Jito's simulation, or our own pricing).
5. Classify the closer: `ours`, `arb_bot` (two or more watched pools, a known arber, or an arbitrage program), `user_swap` (one venue program), `other`. Record its position relative to the trigger (same slot and index difference, or slots later), its PoH ms after the trigger when both are on the same leader's clock, and whether it was seen in our feeds before our first send (`txarr`).
6. Failed transactions cannot close a gap (they write nothing). Those that touched the route are listed as "attempted closers".
This catches the single-pool and zero-capture closers that arb-lab's taker heuristic missed (W §4), lists reverted attempts separately, and replaces `opportunities.taker`.

**R5 Latency** (`attempts.stages`, `decisions.stages`, `commits`, `txarr`, `poh`, `outcomes`). Stage decomposition: first shred → account update received → commit complete → engine dequeue → priced → decided → signed → sent → acknowledged → first verdict → seen in our feeds → landed. State-lag SLO per minute. Ours against closers in PoH ms after the trigger. All wall-clock differences are on one host clock; cross-host comparisons only as PoH differences.

**R6 Leaders** (`leaders`, `validators`, `attempts`, `outcomes`, `bundles`, `blocks`, `slotidx`). Slot share by kind; our attempts, accepted and landed, by kind, region, client, host → leader RTT bucket and block-engine RTT bucket (`be_rtt_ms`); landings per 1,000 eligible slots by kind (X5), with confidence intervals. The host → leader RTT is labelled as such: it is not the bundle's path.

**R7 Feeds** (`feedhealth`, `txarr`, `arrivals`, `slots-feed`). Uptime, reconnects, messages; first-arrival shares and lead times; dead feeds; provider lag; PoH completeness per feed.

**R8 Predictions and accuracy** (`predictions`, `outcomes`, `verify`, `accounts.bin`, `exec.jsonl`).
- **In-bot hints:** exact-match rates per venue and pool against the pre-state version. The v1-style at-hint rate is shown beside it, and `pre_state_unknown` is counted, not scored.
- **Every successful swap on a watched pool** (needs `arbx quote`, WP-09): aggregator-routed and bot-routed swaps are decoded from `exec.jsonl` inner instructions. Each swap is re-quoted with its executed input at the version just before it, and the quote is compared with its executed output.
- **Closer check** (R §3.3): the closer's realised take against our quote at the version just before the closer (from R4).
- **Also reported:** quarantine events (log-only in Stage 0); realised against predicted round trips on landed attempts, with rule 6's caveat; and the verifier against the chain (Stage 1).

**R9 Rivals and the field** (`exec.jsonl`, `rivals`, `blockctx`). Arbitrage-like transactions on watched accounts, all venues; clean wins (signer's SOL + WSOL gain > 0 and no other token left); per signer: wins, profit, sizes, senders (full tip map), CPI tips, bundle membership, rung distribution (block index against the opener, rival-synthesis §5). Caveat printed: only transactions touching watched accounts are seen.

**R10 Experiments** (`decisions`, `attempts`, `outcomes`, `experiments`, `feedhealth`, `discovery`, `verify`). Randomization balance check first. X2: would-fail against delay arm per band, one-sided trend test, go if it falls ≥ 5 points per 10 ms (p < 0.05). X4: land rate and net per attempt by arm, bootstrap CIs, go if the land rate falls < 20% relative. X5: BAM/Jito landing ratio, go if ≥ 0.5, needs ≥ 200 eligible attempts. X8: Tier-A lag p50/p90 by block, Mann-Whitney, go if p50 +≤ 1 ms and p90 +≤ 5 ms with no 429s. X9: verifier p50/p99 per route class, go if p99 ≤ 3 ms. Thresholds are R §5's, fixed before the run. **WP-11:** `experiments` takes a segment or a campaign directory (every segment's attempts; the ledger across them, by the attempts' signatures, counted as R1 counts it; a landing without a ledger line counted apart, never 0). Per experiment (`experiments.<x2|x4|size|margin>`): the balance (decisions per arm against the weights, the assignment: status.jsonl's per-arm counters, else decisions.jsonl read one line at a time and never held, else the evaluations times the weight share; sends per arm; bands and trigger kinds per arm with chi-square independence: the size arm moves the bands by design); per arm attempts, landed and wins with Wilson 95% intervals, net per attempt (bootstrap 95%), net per win, tips/gross, failed fees, would-fails, lost auctions, the meme, common and `unknown` (no mint) wins by the route's mint (the owner's Stage 0 list, `tokens.py`), the executor's refusals of the arm's decisions by reason (`canary_wait` among them), and the net per hour as the arm's net per send times the run's sends per hour (the canary's one send a second binds, a caveat says so when its `canary_wait` refusals reach the sends: the arms compete for the slot); per arm by leader kind and by tip policy (`share_of_edge`, `bam_floor`); each arm against its control (experiments.jsonl `control`, the first arm; a record without one takes the run's config.toml value, else nothing is compared: never a guess), per decision (the randomized unit: every attempt by its assigned arm, `experiments_assigned`, a decision not sent netting 0; wins per decision by two proportions and Fisher, net per decision with a normal 95% interval), per send (descriptive: two proportions and Fisher for the win and land rates, a bootstrap interval of the net-per-attempt difference, the smallest detectable change and the sends per arm a ±30% / ±50% change in the win rate needs) and per send on Jito-led slots (`per_send_jito_led`: on a BAM-led slot the BAM floor often bids the same for every X4 arm); with two experiments or more, the factorial's cells (`factorial`). X2 (the direction fix): a two-sided trend test with the slope's 95% interval per 10 ms, `go_speed` when would-fail rises ≥ 5 points per 10 ms of delay added (one-sided p < 0.05; speed matters) and `go_delay` when it falls as much (adopt the delay), each band with its land rate beside it. X4's `go`: the land rate's relative drop under 20% on its 95% upper bound (the log rate ratio's interval), over the Jito-led sends (every send beside it); STAGE1-PLAN §4's `go_stage1` (no lost auctions, no fewer wins per send at one-sided p < 0.05, ≥ 1,000 sends per arm) and the same test per decision beside it.

**R11 Census and offline experiments** (X1, X3, X6). X1 (`census`): every gap on watched routes with gross, size, route class, route-wide age (from `accounts.bin`: time since the last write to any route account), winner rung, leader kind; capturable if size ≤ 4.2 SOL, age ≥ 354 ms, winner not rung 0-1, venue supported; output capturable SOL/h with medians and top-1 share against the go threshold max(0.5 SOL/h, B/0.15). X3 (`sizes`): summarises `arbx replay --reprice-sizes` (share of would-fails profitable at s/2, s/4, s/8, optimum, and summed net/h). X6 (`sizefit`): byte distribution and share within 1,232.

**R12 Caps** (`caps`, `opportunities`, R4). Budget tiers deduplicated into gap episodes (consecutive opportunities on one route within 5 slots, as `seg.py` did), now with the market's realised take from R4 instead of the "first profitable move within 5 slots" rule.

**R13 Compare** (`meta`, all reports). Config diff from the normalized configs, binary and program hashes, and metrics side by side, so segments are compared only when their provenance differs in known ways.

**cu-table** (`exec.jsonl`, `wallet.jsonl`, `attempts`; F36). It builds per-leg samples from the venue programs' `Program <id> consumed X of Y compute units` lines at the swap's invoke depth, in **every** watched transaction (rivals included), keyed by (venue, bins-crossed bucket). Our program's overhead comes from our own transactions: outer consumption minus their legs. A cell's limit = (overhead p99 + Σ leg p99 + ATA create/close + tip) × 1.10. A cell whose legs have fewer than 30 samples is marked `fallback`. The result goes to `data/cu-table.json` with sample counts.

### 6.5 Tests

All tests below are offline (CI) and run from files in the repository (F12). Field validation on a real Stage-0 segment is listed separately.
- **Synthetic fixtures** for every stream, written by a small generator in the test suite, including rotated and truncated files, replay-class gaps and `idx_update` lines.
- **Golden `report.json`** for a fixture segment; byte-identical on re-run.
- **Rule tests:**
  - summing `_delta` against taking the last `_total`;
  - land-rate denominators;
  - missing-is-not-zero;
  - R4 on a synthetic block with a single-pool closer, a failed attempted closer and a persisted gap;
  - `blockctx` windows and deduplication;
  - the coverage denominator D1 ∪ D2;
  - the ledger golden examples of §4.42 and the recon identity;
  - amended outcomes (the last line wins).
- **Legacy known-answer tests** through the legacy reader, on `testdata/legacy/` (WP-00b): res-pnl's p1-p10 decomposition (trading net −2,337,448; rent 16,383,000; `profit_lamports` −16,201,607) and W §2's bundle-arm figures.
  - Inputs: the raw v1 files preserved in `testdata/legacy/raw/` and the wallet transactions in `testdata/wallet/`.
  - Expected values: `testdata/legacy/expected.json`.
- **Field validation (owner-run):** `segment` on a Stage-0 run regenerates every report and agrees with the ledger's reconciliation within 10k.

## 7. Legacy (arb-lab v1) files

The legacy reader maps arb-lab's files to the v2 model where the meaning is the same and marks the rest as unavailable:

| v1 file | Mapped | Not comparable |
|---|---|---|
| status.jsonl | totals | no deltas; `profit_lamports` includes rent; `failed_fees_lamports` misses unrecorded landings |
| attempts / copies / outcomes | route, sizes, copies, landing | one verdict only; nonce-era; no state version, no leader telemetry; settle-time landings only |
| exec.jsonl | sig, slot, idx, ok, err, fee, cu, signer SOL/token deltas | PumpSwap-only (one program filter); no inner instructions, logs or keys. arbx keeps the same PumpSwap stream and adds the other venues by backfill |
| moves.log | first four columns | no write_version |
| poh.log, slots.log | as is | entries feeds only; one clock per feed |
| alts.jsonl | final dump | no events |
| triggers / opportunities | as is | decoded triggers were priced on predictions |
| bundles.jsonl | as is | no seq, no validator |
| meta.json | feeds and send paths | no hashes; Debug-string config |

Segments after w5h2-p3 have no recorder files at all (W §8); reports on them are limited to attempts, copies, outcomes, bundles, status and chain fetches.

**WP-00 fields in the v1 files (v1.1).** WP-00 adds fields to the v1 files and changes no existing field's meaning, except the state-trigger stage times and the latency percentiles below. v1 files carry no schema header, so a reader tells v1.1 by the fields' presence (`meta.json` `limits`).
- `triggers.jsonl`: `hint_only: true` on every decoded swap when `[strategy] decoded_sends = false`; `opp` is then always null (the hint was not priced), and its `st.decided` is the end of the hint's bookkeeping, not a pricing decision. Skipped triggers still write no line.
- `attempts.jsonl`: `crowded` (bool: a pool of the route is on `[live] crowded_pools_file`), `queue_rank` (`Prio::rank`: 0 seed pool (source `mriya` or `both`), 1 other, 2 crowded) and `st.fired` (t_ns at which the copies were handed to the runtime: `fire` spawns the send, so `signed→fired` is the spawn's cost; the bytes leave at copies.jsonl `t_sent`. Null when the sim gate fires them later). The line is now written after the copies are fired.
- `attempts.jsonl` and `opportunities.jsonl` `st` of a state trigger: `rx` = `decoded` = the receive time of the pool's first account update stamped with the trigger transaction (kept even when the engine queue emptied between the transaction's updates), `engine` = that update's dequeue, `resolved` = the start of the evaluation, `decided` = the end of the route search. In v1 all five held one time.
- `status.jsonl`:
  - counters `trigger_hint_only` (engine), `live_skip_big_band_throttle`, `live_skip_crowded_pool` (a crowded route expecting at least `[live] crowded_skip_min_expected_lamports`) and `rank_crowded` (exec); `trigger_skip_signer_fails` now also follows the skipped signer's swap (its statistics keep learning);
  - `exec.over_rate: {crowded, other}`, the totals of copies refused for a send path's rate, split by the crowded rank;
  - the latency series `state_recv_to_engine`, `state_engine_to_decided`, `state_recv_to_decided` (one sample per state evaluation, whatever it decided), `hint_rx_to_done` (one per hint: receive to the end of its bookkeeping), `decided_to_signed` and `signed_to_fired` (one per attempt) and `signed_to_sent` (one per settled attempt whose first copy was sent: signed to that copy's `t_sent`) (A.8). The v1 decoded series (`rx_to_decoded` … `rx_to_decided`) take samples only from priced decoded triggers (`decoded_sends = true`); with hints only they stay at n 0.
  - Latency `n` counts every sample since the start (v1 reset it at 200,000). The percentiles come from a uniform reservoir of 8,192 samples of the whole run (v1: all samples since the start or the last reset), so they still cover the run so far, not the interval.
- `meta.json`: `limits: {sending, max_size_sol, max_size_src, sim_max_size_sol, max_failed_fees_lamports, max_new_token_accounts, decoded_sends, state_triggers, big_band_min_expected_lamports, big_band_max_per_sec, crowded_pools_file, crowded_skip_min_expected_lamports, record}`, and `overrides` (what `--shadow` and `--no-record` changed for this run: `{mode: "shadow"}`, `{"record.enabled": false}`; `{}` for none).
- The `send` lines of `run.log` give `over-rate N (crowded M)` per path.

**WP-02 in the v1.1 files.** Every v1.1 stream is now compressed and rotated by the recorder (§1.6) and starts with a header naming `arbx.<stream>/1.1`; arb-lab's names change only for the per-feed slot log (`slots.log` → `slots-feed`, v2 columns), the raw entries archive (`entries.zst` → `entries-<feed>.NNN.bin.zst` with an ARBXENT header and the v1 frames) and the lookup-table dump (`alts.jsonl` → `alts-final.jsonl.zst`). The records are unchanged except: outcomes.jsonl's running `failed_fees_lamports` is now `failed_fees_total_lamports` (after this attempt) plus `failed_fees_delta_lamports` (this attempt's part); poh.log and moves.log are at v2 (§4.22-§4.23); status.jsonl is v2 (§4.8); the exec.rs diagnostic lines also go to events.jsonl (`log`).

**WP-01 in the v1.1 files.** Every `status.jsonl` line carries `send_gate` (`{mode, reason, detail, since_ns, sticky}`, the SendGate when the line was written; review M2), and the final line (written once the run knows why it stops) carries `stop_reason` (A.5). `exec.counts` (the executor's counters, `Exec::status`) gains `reject_gate_shadow` / `reject_gate_stopped` when the SendGate refuses a send (review M11: they are under `exec.counts`, not the engine's `counts`). `meta.json` is v2 (§4.2); a reader tells it by its `schema` field. The segment directory also holds `config.toml` (the frozen config), `stop.json`, `control.jsonl` and, while it runs, `control.sock`.

## Appendix A. Enumerations

### A.1 Decision rejection reasons (`decisions.reason`, `routes[].reason`)

| Stage | Reasons |
|---|---|
| State | `pending_commit`, `route_not_quiet` (unknown W(T): a route account was written within `quiescence_ms`), `parts_skewed` (within one pool's consistency set), `store_lagging`, `account_missing`, `clock_stale`, `warmup`, `fork_pending` (a route account's version is on a slot not yet on the confirmed branch while a sibling is known), `state_unavailable` (WP-04, I-4: the state stream is down or its newest message is older than `max_update_age_ms`), `resync` (WP-04 review: a part of the pool has not been read again since the state stream's last connection, or, as a decision's reason, the re-read has not landed) |
| Pricing | `unquotable_dlmm_array`, `unquotable_dlmm_liquidity`, `unquotable_damm`, `unquotable_cp`, `broken_quote` (round trip > input), `implausible_gap`, `below_min_profit`, `dlmm_cap` (informational: size capped); routes also `best`, `not_best` (WP-04). **WP-16** (every quote is arbx-sizing's, `src/quote.rs`): `unquotable_*` keep their meaning (an account the leg needs is missing; the venue cannot fill any ladder size or pays nothing; DLMM `unquotable_dlmm_array` when the walk needs a bin array we do not follow or our route would not name: the active one, then contiguous, at most 5); `pump_quote_not_sol` (a PumpSwap pool whose quote mint is not WSOL: not traded, as before); and the crate's refusal states, prefixed `sz_`: `sz_budget` (a DLMM leg's walk past its budget, the transaction's CU limit: 200 bins read, 100 each when both legs are DLMM), `sz_pump_held_capacity` (a PumpSwap sell past the quote the pool pays out net of its held-back amounts, 6063 on chain), `sz_ammv4_token_2022` (AMM v4's v2 swap takes SPL Token only), and one per arbx-sizing `Abort` (program/arbx-sizing/DESIGN.md §2): `sz_unsupported`, `sz_layout`, `sz_transfer_fee`, `sz_transfer_hook`, `sz_math`, `sz_model_refused`; `sz_pump_mayhem` (not given since the WP-16 review: mayhem pools are priced), `sz_pump_cashback`, `sz_pump_disabled`, `sz_pump_virtual`, `sz_pump_fee_total`, `sz_pump_fee_config`, `sz_pump_empty`, `sz_pump_held_exceeds_vault`, `sz_pump_unknown_tail` (a set byte of the pool's unidentified tail 287..301; since the WP-16 review byte 270 is `is_holder_reward` and priced, and a PumpSwap pool is `sz_layout` only outside 211..=301 bytes or with a flag byte that is no bool); `sz_cpmm_swap_disabled`, `sz_cpmm_not_open`, `sz_cpmm_owed_exceeds_vault`, `sz_cpmm_creator_fee_on`, `sz_cpmm_fee`, `sz_cpmm_empty`; `sz_ammv4_status`, `sz_ammv4_pnl_exceeds_vault`, `sz_ammv4_fee`, `sz_ammv4_empty`; `sz_dlmm_status`, `sz_dlmm_not_active`, `sz_dlmm_function_type`, `sz_dlmm_collect_fee_mode`, `sz_dlmm_zero_price`, `sz_dlmm_activation_type`; `sz_damm_status`, `sz_damm_not_active`, `sz_damm_rate_limiter` (in force for our leg's direction: the program then needs the instructions sysvar our leg does not pass, 6049), `sz_damm_mode`, `sz_damm_fee100`, `sz_damm_liquidity`, `sz_damm_range`, `sz_damm_time`. A route records its legs' refusal first, then a capacity or the budget, then "too small". **WP-21** (Raydium CLMM, Orca Whirlpool; program/arbx-sizing DESIGN.md §9): `unquotable_clmm` (an account the leg needs is missing, or no size fills), `unquotable_clmm_array` (a tick array the walk needs is not followed, not read yet, pending, or read missing once and not confirmed), `venue_upgraded` (the venue's programdata was deployed again since the binary its port was proven on), `mint_paused` / `mint_frozen` (a Token-2022 mint of a PumpSwap, CPMM, AMM v4, DLMM or DAMM v2 pool that is paused or creates its accounts frozen: every swap fails), and the crate's states `sz_clmm_status`, `sz_clmm_not_open`, `sz_clmm_fee`, `sz_clmm_bitmap_extension`, `sz_clmm_no_liquidity`, `sz_clmm_tick_arrays`, `sz_clmm_price_limit`, `sz_clmm_state`, `sz_clmm_mint_paused`, `sz_clmm_mint_frozen`, `sz_whirl_trade_not_enabled`, `sz_whirl_timestamp`, `sz_whirl_tick_arrays`, `sz_whirl_price_limit`, `sz_whirl_state`, `sz_whirl_mint_paused`, `sz_whirl_mint_frozen`; a CLMM leg's walk past its budget is `sz_budget` (swap steps: 100 alone, 50 each beside another walking leg). A priced route through a venue not switched to `send`, in a run that sends (canary, live), is `venue_quote_only`: recorded with its price, never the decision's pick (the executor refuses such a route with the same reason, gate open or not) |
| Policy | `gate_shadow:<reason>`, `gate_stopped`, `recorder_unhealthy`, `venue_quarantine` (deferred: not raised by WP-07, see PLAN WP-07 "As built"), `pool_quarantine` (Stage 0: never; log only), `token_cooling`, `experiment_holdout`, `rent_cap`, `size_cap`; WP-04: `no_executor` (a shadow run), `fee_cap`, `no_free_nonce`, `canary_wait`, `sender_stopped` (the executor's refusals as `Exec::refusal` names them) |
| Send slot | `inflight_same_state`, `inflight_same_leader`, `band_throttle`, `rate_queue_timeout`, `crowded_pool` (dropped from the queue behind less crowded pools, F6) |
| Inputs | `blockhash_stale`, `leader_unknown`, `leader_skip_kind`, `program_hash_mismatch`, `jito_auth_stale` (no valid cached token; the send path never refreshes it, F28) |
| Flags (not rejections; follow-up lines) | `torn_suspect`, `fork_priced` |
| Build | `uva_missing`, `mid_not_held`, `dlmm_ext_needed`, `oversized`, `build_error:<detail>`, `expected_below_min_profit` |
| Sent | `sent` |

**As WP-05 writes them.** The executor's reasons (`Exec::decide`, in order, §4.32): `gate_shadow:<reason>`, `gate_stopped`, `recorder_unhealthy`, `warmup`, `state_unavailable`, `resync`, `sender_stopped`, `fee_cap`, `canary_count`, `canary_wait`, `blockhash_stale`, `leader_unknown`, `leader_skip_kind`, `jito_auth_stale`, `expected_below_min_profit`, `implausible_gap`, `token_cooling`, `rent_cap`, `inflight_same_state`, `inflight_same_leader`, `band_throttle`, `crowded_skip` (a bucket-2 route expecting at least `crowded_skip_min_expected_lamports`), `would_rate` / `would_rate_crowded` (a would-send over the bundle path's modelled rate; WP-05 review #10), `oversized`, `build_error:<detail>`, `sent`; `no_executor` without one. `no_free_nonce` is gone (no durable nonce on the bundle path). **As WP-07 writes them:** `equity_unknown` (a live or canary run with a ledger before its first balance snapshot: the floor cannot be armed), then `equity_stale` (WP-07 review #3: the money view is stale), between `sender_stopped` and `fee_cap`; `size_cap` (WP-07 review #12: a route sized above the executor's size cap, the bankroll's included, whoever sized it; after `implausible_gap`); `rent_cap` also when the recoverable rent plus a new account's estimated rent (2,100,000) would pass `[supervisor] rent_cap_sol`; the bankroll (`[live] bankroll_share`) is a size cap, not a reason (the engine re-quotes at it; before the first snapshot the configured cap), except `bankroll_empty`: the engine's refusal, before the executor's checks, when it allows nothing (no WSOL, or the liquid SOL under `bankroll_reserve_sol`; counter `send_bankroll_empty`). A copy's `drop` (§4.38) uses `rate_queue_timeout`, `crowded_pool` (the queue served a less crowded send of its band first), `bad_tx`, `jito_auth_stale`, `send_task`: those are the queue's and the path's, never a decision's reason.

**WP-06.** `uva_missing` (a route check, after `token_cooling`: a PumpSwap buy, on either leg (a PumpSwap leg its quote token goes into, review WP-06 #13), while the wallet's PumpSwap user volume accumulator is not known to exist, unless `[builder] allow_uva_create`), `mid_not_held` (after `uva_missing`: `[builder] create_close = false`, the held-only control arm, and the wallet holds no account for the route's token; review WP-06 #9), `dlmm_ext_needed` and `oversized` (the build, §4.37) are produced; `rent_cap` is not (create+close replaced the new-account cap). `dlmm_cap` is decisions' `dlmm_cap_bound` flag (§4.32), not a reason.

**At the merge of WP-06** (onto integ/stage0, 2026-10-06; revision 2.15). The route checks begin `expected_below_min_profit`, `implausible_gap`, `size_cap` (WP-07: the canary's or `[live]`'s cap under the bankroll's on the ledger's money view, the one bankroll source: WP-06's store-derived hook is gone; the engine re-quotes every route at the same cap, so it refuses only a route sized past it), `token_cooling`, `uva_missing`, `mid_not_held`, then the in-flight lock and the rest as above. `rent_cap` is produced by nothing: WP-07's rent cap (`[supervisor] rent_cap_sol`) limits held-set additions only (the size budget's `held_ata` rung, `[builder] held_max` > 0: off by default), which it refuses while the money view's recoverable rent plus the rent the addition may lock (`build::HELD_RENT_BOUND`, 2,282,880) would pass the cap: the bundle is then `oversized` (its build error says `held_ata: recoverable rent … over the rent cap …`). A route through a token the wallet holds no account for is never refused for rent: its account is created and closed in the same transaction (rent-neutral), whatever the recoverable rent. `[live] max_new_token_accounts` is gone.

### A.2 Outcome finals

`landed_ok`, `landed_fail`, `not_landed_expired` (the blockhash expired without a landing, after one bounded status check), `not_landed_refused` (the block engine refused the send), `unsettled` (the process stopped first). A later `amend: true` line can change a final (§4.40).

**As WP-05 writes them:** `not_landed_refused` also covers a copy that never left (the rate queue, a stale token, a bad copy, a failed fire task); `unsettled` is written for every attempt still open when the run stops (`final_src` `stop`).

### A.3 Ledger categories

- **Summing** (Σ = `equity_delta`, the change of total equity; §4.42): `trade_gross`, `tip`, `fee_win`, `fee_fail`, `fee_other`, `rent_nonrecoverable`, `transfer_in`, `transfer_out`, `other`.
- **Informational** (never summed): `rent_locked` and `rent_reclaimed` (recoverable rent; they move liquid equity, not total equity), `liquid_delta`, `stranded`, and `inventory_reval` (recon only).
- **Transaction kinds**, not categories: `wrap` and `unwrap`. Their only summing category is `fee_other`.

### A.4 Transfer kinds (`exec.jsonl transfers[].kind`)

`sender_tip`, `cpi_tip`, `wrap`, `unwrap`, `self`, `venue_fee`, `platform_fee`, `rent_create`, `rent_close`, `other`.

### A.5 Stop reasons

**Stop reasons** (`stop.json`): `deadline`, `signal`, `control`, `equity_day` (campaign-wide rolling 24 h; exit 20 ends the campaign), `disk_budget`, `state_unavailable` (WP-04, PLAN-ADDENDUM I-4: the state stream delivered nothing within `[state] boot_timeout_s` of the engine's start; WP-04 review: or, when `[state] unavailable_stop_s` is set (0, never, by default: an owner decision), the state was unavailable that long without a break after it had delivered; exit 30, which the supervisor classes a crash: retried after its back-off, counted toward the crash limit), `error`, `panic`.

**SendGate shadow reasons** (`status.send_gate.reason`, `gate_change` events; the process keeps running and recording): `control`, `forced_shadow` (`--shadow`), `equity_hour` (campaign-wide rolling hour), `reconcile_miss`, `state_slo` (only with `slo_action = "shadow"`; recovers automatically), `core_record_loss` (for the rest of the segment), `recorder_unhealthy`, `disk_budget` (before the stop), `program_hash_mismatch` (WP-01: the route program on chain is not `[exec] expected_program_*`, or could not be read; for the whole segment), `mode_shadow` (WP-01: a run without `--live` or `--canary`, which sends nothing), `chainfetch_dead` (WP-03b: the chain-fetch service died; wallet.jsonl, the reconciliation source, stopped with it; for the rest of the segment). WP-04: the store's health (warm-up, state availability) is not a SendGate reason: `Exec::wants_send` reads it beside the gate (`reject_warmup`, `reject_state_unavailable`, `reject_resync`), so an operator's pause is never undone by a recovery. A deferred send (the sim gate's re-checks) and a scheduled bundle resend also ask the store's health (WP-04 review #8). A stop request turns the gate to `stopped` with the stop reason. A closed gate makes `Exec::wants_send` refuse, counted as `reject_gate_shadow` or `reject_gate_stopped` in `exec.counts` (A.7; the shadow reason is in the gate, not the counter name). A send deferred past its decision (the sim gate's re-checks, scheduled bundle resends) asks the gate again and is dropped when it is closed (review M10).

**WP-05:** the SendGate reason `dry_run` (`--dry-run`: would-sends are signed by a throwaway key and never sent; for the whole segment). **WP-07:** stop reasons `equity_floor` (SOL + WSOL below the campaign's start minus its test budget), `equity_hour` (OD-11: more than `max_drop_per_hour_sol` lost within a rolling hour, campaign-wide), `token_floor` (a non-SOL token held at the campaign's start below its start amount less `token_floor_tolerance`), with `equity_day` (more than `max_drop_per_day_sol` within 24 h): each exit 20, which ends the campaign; the same names are the SendGate's shadow reason set just before the stop (sticky), and `reconcile_miss` (a reconciliation miss, §4.44: shadow for the rest of the segment, no stop). In a run that sends nothing (shadow, dry run) a rule that fires is recorded only. **WP-07 review:** the stop rules measure the net spend since the campaign's start (§4.42; the owner's transfers are not in it): `equity_floor` is the net spend past the test budget, `equity_hour` / `equity_day` its growth within a rolling hour / 24 h; the SendGate reason `equity_stale` (the ledger's money view is stale, §4.43: not sticky, lifted when the view is fresh again unless another reason holds the gate; an operator's resume meanwhile is closed again at the next ledger tick). The sim gate, the Jito gate and bundle resends are gone, so no send is deferred past its decision. `Exec::wants_send` became the send checks of `Exec::decide` (`exec::send_checks`, one pure function: the gate, the recorder, the store's health, the sender, the blockhash, the leader, the token); a closed gate is counted `reject_gate_shadow` / `reject_gate_stopped` for every decision it held back, would-sends included.

### A.6 Event kinds

`feed_connect`, `feed_disconnect`, `feed_error`, `state_stream_reconnect`, `tx_stream_reconnect`, `jito_stream_down`, `jito_stream_up`, `warmup_ready`, `warmup_lost`, `lag_slo_breach`, `lag_slo_recovered`, `gate_change`, `fork_dead_slot`, `fork_abandoned`, `late_member`, `alt_give_up`, `programdata_change`, `quarantine` (Stage 0: `would_quarantine`), `recorder_drop`, `recorder_io_error`, `recorder_encode_error`, `recorder_writer_dead`, `recorder_writer_stalled`, `recorder_fatal`, `replay_gap`, `disk_action`, `leaders_refresh`, `leaders_list_stale`, `snapshot`, `snapshot_slot_mismatch`, `recon_miss`, `wallet_update_missing`, `blockhash_source_disagree`, `chainfetch_backlog`, `chainfetch_tap_drop`, `chainfetch_dead`, `chainfetch_stalled`, `chainfetch_recovered`, `rpc_boot_retry`, `rpc_boot_failed`, `jito_token_refresh`, `probe`, `log` (diagnostic lines that arb-lab printed to stderr), `config_note`, `stop_requested`; WP-04: `state_unavailable`, `state_available`, `state_resnapshot`, `tier_a_drop`, `alts_preload` (§4.6); WP-04 review: `state_resync_done`, `state_resync_slow`, `read_retry`, `read_gave_up`; WP-09: `engine_digests` (§4.50); WP-16 review: `pools_dropped` (F8; `subsystem` `engine`, once at boot, `{total, counts: {why: n}, pools: [{venue, pool, why, mint}]}`: every candidate pool the registry did not keep, `why` `missing` (no account), `owner` (not the venue's program), `layout` (a pool account the venue's quote does not read: a PumpSwap pool under 211 bytes, a DAMM v2 pool of another length or discriminator, ...), `transfer_fee`, `transfer_hook` or `mint_layout` (a mint arbx-sizing refuses; `mint` names it); the same object is meta.json `pools.dropped`).

**WP-03b's** (detail fields): `chainfetch_dead` `{why}` (the service's task ended while the run went on: a panic; a run.log WARNING too, and the SendGate closes for the rest of the segment, reason `chainfetch_dead`), `chainfetch_stalled` `{published_age_ms}` (no publication for 5 s) and `chainfetch_recovered` `{}`; `wallet_update_missing` `{sig, slot, found_by}`; `blockhash_source_disagree` `{slot, height, a: {src, hash}, b: {src, hash}}` (two sources, two hashes for one slot or height); `chainfetch_backlog` `{backlog, max_backlog, dropped_total, need_rps, have_rps, target_rps}` (signatures dropped past `max_backlog`; at most one a minute); `chainfetch_tap_drop` `{dropped_total, queue}` (the taps' queue was full; at most one a minute); `rpc_boot_retry` `{method, role, provider, class, attempt, elapsed_ms, error, suppressed}` and `rpc_boot_failed` `{method, role, attempts, secs, by_provider_class, last}` (§4.10, the boot policy).

**WP-05's** (detail fields): `jito_stream_down` `{stream_epoch, error}`, `jito_stream_up` `{stream_epoch, down_ms}`, `jito_token_refresh` `{ok, via, expires_in_s}` or `{ok: false, error, retry_s, stale}` (§4.6).

**WP-07 review's** (§4.6): `equity_stale`, `equity_fresh`, `owner_transfer`, `recon_undecided`; the campaign's `data_files_changed`, `segment_unreaped`. **WP-07's** (§4.6): `snapshot`, `snapshot_failed`, `snapshot_slot_mismatch`, `recon_miss`, `refused_bundle_landed`, `equity_floor`, `equity_hour`, `equity_day`, `token_floor`, `low_native_sol`, `stranded_token`, `equity_unknown`; the campaign's: `campaign_start`, `equity_start`, `segment_start`, `segment_end`, `segment_not_sending`, `segment_start_failed`, `campaign_shadow`, `campaign_end`.

**WP-22's** (the campaign's, §4.1): `pool_refresh` (a `pool_lists` entry), `pool_list_failed` `{segment, error}`.

**WP-20's** (`subsystem` `alerts`; detail fields): `alert_sent` `{alerts, kinds, level, attempts, ms}`, `alert_failed` `{alerts, kinds, level, attempts, error}` (redacted), `alert_dropped` `{why, kind, level, key, dropped_total, queue}` (§4.6, §4.51).

### A.7 Counter names (`counts_total` / `counts_delta`)

- **Engine:** `tx_first`, `trigger`, `trigger_hint_only`, `trigger_skip_will_fail`, `trigger_skip_signer_fails`, `trigger_unquotable`, `trigger_exact_out_unsolved`, `alt_wait`, `alt_learned`, `alt_retry`, `alt_gave_up`, `resolve_failed`, `commit_complete`, `commit_superseded`, `commit_timeout_unknown`, `commit_timeout_quiescent`, `commit_late_member`, `torn_suspect`, `fork_abandoned_slot`, `fork_priced`, `pred_scored`, `pred_exact`, `pred_pre_state_unknown`, `evaluation`, `route_priced`, `opportunity`, `state_opportunity`, plus one counter per rejection reason of A.1 (`reject_<reason>`). **As WP-04 writes them**, also: `commit_fork_dropped`, `commit_w_tx_stream`, `commit_w_shred`, `commit_w_unknown`, `fork_dead_slot`, `fork_versions_dropped`, `fork_refetch`, `fork_slotidx_missing`, `evaluation_deferred`, `evaluation_not_quiet_dropped`, `trigger_failed`, `signer_ok_tx`, `signer_miss_tx`, `signer_miss`, `signer_miss_unknown`, `state_unavailable`, `state_disconnect`, `state_resnapshot`, `warmup_ready`, `warmup_lost`, `tier_a_dropped`, `lag_slo_breach`, `alt_fetch_failed`, `alt_wait_dropped`, `alt_gave_up` (a transaction dropped for a given-up table), `alt_preloaded`, `pred_unquotable`, `pred_not_written`, `pred_not_committed`, `pred_trigger_failed`, `pred_fork_dropped`, `pred_queue_overflow`; `reject_other` for a reason without its own counter. `opportunity` (decoded) is gone: opportunities are `state_opportunity`. **WP-04 review:** `state_resync_done`, `state_resync_slow`, `state_resync_superseded` (a reconnection while a re-read ran), `read_chunk_ok`, `read_chunk_failed`, `resync_chunk_behind` (a re-read chunk read at a slot before the stream's resume slot), `read_retry`, `read_gave_up`, `read_missing` (accounts the node did not have), `read_answer_dropped` (an answer for a superseded re-read), `fork_late_version`, `reject_resync`. **WP-21:** `route_quote_only` (priced routes held back as `venue_quote_only`), `clmm_absent` (CLMM accounts a read found missing, held empty), `clmm_absent_confirmed` (newly followed tick arrays a second read found missing again: priced as not existing from then on).
- **Engine (the FT-000 fixes):** `alt_evicted` (lookup tables the bounded cache evicted, §4.26), `alt_refetch` (fetches of tables it had evicted).
- **Exec (WP-04):** `reject_warmup`, `reject_state_unavailable`, `reject_resync` (WP-04 review) (`Exec::wants_send` refused on the store's health).
- **Engine, WP-16:** `reject_<reason>` for every reason A.1 adds (`reject_pump_quote_not_sol`, `reject_sz_budget`, `reject_sz_pump_held_capacity`, `reject_sz_damm_rate_limiter`, ...; `quote::REASONS` lists them). **WP-16 review (F10):** the same reasons on the other paths: `trigger_<reason>` (a hint's check, §4.30), `probe_<reason>` (`simcheck`'s route probe) and `pred_<reason>` (the accuracy monitor, §4.34), in place of `trigger_unquotable`, `probe_unquotable` and `pred_unquotable`, which stay for the reasons that are not the crate's; and `pred_exact_out_solved` (F6, §4.34).
- **Exec:** `attempt`, `sent`, `send_refused`, `live_skip_big_band_throttle` and `live_skip_crowded_pool` (WP-00 only), `rank_crowded`, `verdict_<outcome>`, `landed_ok`, `landed_fail`, `expired`, `expiry_status_hit`, `amended`, `unsettled`.
- **Exec (WP-05):** `reject_<reason>` for every executor reason of A.1 (`reject_other` otherwise; one per refused decision: a decision a closed SendGate refused counts `reject_gate_*`, and what else refused it counts `ungated_<reason>`, so the `reject_*` counters sum to `refused_total`: WP-05 review #16), `drop_<code>` for a copy that never left (§4.38's `drop`), `send_unanswered` (a copy that left without an answer, kept open), `send_refused` (one the block engine refused), `cool_repeat` (a bundle's further would-fails, not counted toward cooling), `would_send`, `dry_run_ok`, `dry_run_check_failed`, `live_sent`, `canary_sent`, `rank_crowded` (a crowded route sent), `verdict_accepted`, `verdict_processed`, `verdict_finalized`, `verdict_would_fail`, `verdict_lost_auction`, `verdict_dropped`, `verdict_rejected`, `inflight_released`, `landed_ok`, `landed_fail`, `not_landed_expired`, `not_landed_refused`, `unsettled`, `expired`, `expiry_status_hit`, `amended`, `status_check` and `expiry_check` (status calls made), `sim_sent`, `sim_build_failed`, `sim_skipped_rate`. The spec's `attempt`, `sent` and `send_refused` are `exec.attempts_total`, `sent_total` and `refused_total`; `live_skip_*` (WP-00) are gone.
- **Exec (WP-07):** `reject_equity_unknown` (and `ungated_equity_unknown`). The ledger's own counters are status `money.counts` (§4.8). **WP-07 review:** `reject_equity_stale`, `reject_size_cap` (and their `ungated_`), `refused_then_landed`; `money.counts` adds `equity_stale`, `owner_transfer`, `recon_owner_transfers`, `recon_undecided`, `refused_bundle_landed`.

- **Exec (WP-06):** `reject_uva_missing`, `reject_mid_not_held`, `reject_dlmm_ext_needed`, `reject_oversized` (and their `ungated_<reason>`); `reject_rent_cap` stays defined and counts nothing. `held_addition`: attempts (sent or would-send) that keep the account they create (the size budget's `held_ata`; 0 unless `[builder] held_max` > 0; review WP-06 #2).
- **Exec (at the merge of WP-06):** `reject_rent_cap` (and `ungated_rent_cap`) stay defined and count nothing (A.1: the rent cap refuses held-set additions only, as `reject_oversized`); `reject_size_cap` counts the routes sized past the cap the engine re-quotes at (none from the engine's own decisions); the engine's `send_bankroll_empty` stays WP-07's.

### A.8 Stage names (`latency`, `stages`)

- **v2:** `first_shred→update_recv`, `update_recv→commit`, `commit→engine`, `engine→priced`, `priced→decided`, `decided→built`, `built→signed`, `signed→enqueued`, `enqueued→sent`, `sent→ack`, `sent→first_verdict`, `sent→seen`, `sent→landed`.
- **WP-00 interim names** (state path, before WP-04's commit stages exist; F13): `state_recv_to_engine`, `state_engine_to_decided`, `state_recv_to_decided`, `decided_to_signed`, `signed_to_fired` (handed to the runtime), `signed_to_sent` (first copy's `t_sent`), and `hint_rx_to_done` for decoded hints. WP-02's latency gate compares against the state-path ones.
- **v1 names** `rx_to_decoded`, `decoded_to_engine`, `engine_to_resolved`, `resolved_to_decided` and `rx_to_decided` remain for priced decoded triggers only (`decoded_sends = true`); hints go to `hint_rx_to_done`.
